# Design decisions log

Decisions made with the user where the GDS was silent or open. Newest last.

| Date | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-23 | Parcel generation (GDS §9 Q1) | **Seeded per run.** Procedural Mountain West parcel from a seed; QA uses a fixed seed. A parcel picker may come later. |
| 2026-10-23 | M1 parcel size | **40 acres** (~400 m square ≈ 67 world units ≈ 34 UE tiles). |
| 2026-10-23 | Build grid | **3 m sub-grid** (½ world unit, ¼ UE tile) for structures; terrain stays on UE's 12 m tiles. |
| 2026-10-23 | Merging | Claude merges each increment's PR itself once CI is green. |
| 2026-10-23 | M1 cabin placement | **Instant.** Real construction stages (scaffold → frame → clad, built by people over time) come with M2. |
| 2026-10-23 | Milestone 2 focus | **Construction + family:** a family of 4 (UE crowd kit) builds structures over time through scaffold → frame → clad. |
| 2026-10-23 | Time pace | **Speed tiers:** pause, 1× = 1 day / 60 s (watch the work), fast-forward up to 1 day / 1.5 s. A cabin ≈ 3 in-game weeks. |
| 2026-10-23 | Construction cost (M2) | **Money + materials.** Starting savings; materials bought and delivered, or produced. |
| 2026-10-23 | Starting family | **2 adults + 2 kids**, different adult skill mixes; kids help a little. |
| 2026-10-23 | Deliveries | **Truck on a gravel county road** along a parcel edge with a driveway gate; orders arrive after a lead time and unload to a visible stockpile. |
| 2026-10-23 | Self-supply (M2) | **Fell own timber:** mark trees, fell, buck into logs, haul to the stockpile. |
| 2026-10-23 | Saves | **IndexedDB autosave now** (version 1 + migrations, autosave each in-game day, JSON export / import). |
| 2026-10-23 | What logs are for | **Log cabin blueprint + firewood.** Conifers give saw logs (2–4), aspen 1, cottonwood firewood only; tops and limbs give firewood. No sawmill / selling for now. |
| 2026-10-23 | How felling plays | **Mark trees, the family works them** as a scheduled task: fell → buck → skid to the yard (haul time grows with distance). With building and felling both waiting, every other person goes to the woods. |
| 2026-10-23 | Firewood (provisional) | The stove burns firewood on cold days (≈ 0.12 rick/day in January, none in summer, ≈ 4–5 cords a winter). Running out is logged; comfort / health effects wait for the needs system. Start with 2 ricks. |
| 2026-10-23 | Next builds (user picked all) | Order: **more buildings** (workshop / garage, root cellar, chicken coop + run, garden beds, log house, **outhouse**) → **perimeter + sightlines** → **water + power** → **underground bunker**. Every building has a furnished interior. |
| 2026-10-23 | Plumbing and electricity | **Allowed on higher-tech, self-sustaining compounds, but only through real infrastructure that costs something.** Running water needs a source (well / spring / cistern), pressure (gravity tank or pump), pipe runs, fixtures and waste handling (outhouse → composting toilet → septic tank + leach field). Electricity needs generation (solar / micro-hydro / wind / generator + fuel), batteries, an inverter and wiring. Each costs materials, labour, know-how (someone with plumbing / electrical skill, or slower and riskier without) and upkeep (pump wear, battery ageing, frozen pipes in winter, fuel). Interiors show the tier: oil lamps, hand pump and outhouse at first; faucets, a water heater, a flush toilet and electric lights once a building is connected. |
| 2026-10-23 | What the perimeter protects against | **Both:** (1) **events that test you**: wildlife (bears, coyotes after the hens), trespassers and thieves, and later raids during a crisis, picking targets by what they can see and reach; fences, hedges, lights and dogs deter or delay them, and you see the outcome; (2) **real-time raids**: attackers visibly approach and you respond (defenders to positions, lights, gates). Build order: perimeter pieces + sightlines → threat events → real-time raids. |
| 2026-10-23 | Sightlines | **Both views:** an exposure heatmap (visible from the county road, from the ridge, at night from your lights) with a per-building "seen from road %", and a click-a-point viewshed for towers, gates and posts. Terrain, trees, hedges, berms and walls block sight. |
| 2026-10-23 | Lived-in spaces | Every building is dressed with seeded clutter (dishes, books, toys, boots, pets, pictures, woodpiles, laundry lines …) so no two homes look alike. |
| 2026-10-23 | Scale | **Everything, now and later, is to human scale** (user). 1 unit ≈ 6 m, a person 0.28 ≈ 1.7 m; furniture and props are authored in meters × M. `src/game/scale.test.ts` guards doors, storeys, furniture, clutter, animals and perimeter heights: add a line there for every new piece, and check new work with a street-level shot that includes a person. |
| 2026-10-23 | Water + power model (provisional) | **Pipes and cables are drawn lines** (trenched by the family, costed per length) that join buildings into a water network and a power network. Sources: hand-dug well + hand pump (no pressure, water is carried), drilled well (contractor drilling; the pump needs power), rain cistern (fills from connected roofs by season), gravity water tower; waste via outhouse → septic tank + leach field. Power: solar array, battery + inverter shed, generator shed (burns fuel). Each building gets **Plumb it / Wire it** jobs (fixtures / wiring kit + labour; faster and safer with a mechanic) that change its interior (faucet instead of the hand pump, water heater, flush toilet with septic, ceiling lights, a fridge). A **daily balance**: solar by season, demand per connected building, battery state of charge, generator fuel, water supply vs. use, rain into cisterns. Upkeep: battery fade, pump failures (repairs), generator fuel. Shortages are logged (morale effects wait for needs). |
| 2026-10-23 | Graphics correctness (user: "realistic graphics and detail and immersion are very important") | Procedural art must never show impossible geometry: no window or door corners through roof lines or corners, nothing floating or sunk, no z-fighting, nothing through walls. Every gabled shell is checked by `validateSpec` when built (openings inside their wall with a margin, under the eave, clear of each other; gable glass under the rakes, auto-fitted or rake-shaped); `buildings.test.ts` fails on any problem. Add a rule there whenever a new failure mode turns up. Every new piece also gets eye-level and aerial screenshots that are checked before merging. |
| 2026-10-24 | Underground (provisional) | **Bunkers are built room by room** (GDS §2.2 underground layer). A bunker starts at an entrance (a steel hatch with a ladder down a 5 m shaft, or a stairwell under a small headhouse); every other room must share at least one 3 m cell of wall with a room already planned, and gets a doorway through it. All rooms of a bunker share one floor level, the entrance's ground less ≈ 4.8 m; each room needs ≈ 0.9 m of earth over its roof slab and can't be dug deeper than ≈ 6 m. Rooms go under the yard, gardens and houses but not under the creek, wells or the septic field. Rooms: tunnel, buried 40 ft container (the cheap one), bunkroom (8), galley, storeroom, plant room (NBC filter, batteries, generator, 2,000 L tank), medical bay, comms & armory. Digging uses a hired excavator; the net earth dug out goes on a **spoil pile** that an earth berm draws on (60 % less shovel work); ditches add to the pile. While a room is built the terrain over it is an open pit (dug → walls poured → roof slab, waterproofed → backfilled). **Underground view** (U, or the Underground button; it comes on when you pick a bunker room): see-through ground, trees hidden, surface buildings ghosted, bunker rooms cut open. Walking down into a bunker, the escape tunnel to a hidden exit, and bunker water / power / air (the plant room feeding the rest) come next. |
| 2026-10-24 | Cutaway decor | Things hung on walls above the cut (pictures, signs, lamps, curtains) are a `decor` layer that the cutaway hides with the upper walls, so nothing floats. Lit windows glow only from outside (a one-sided glow over the glass); from inside you look out into the night. Lamplight reaches about its own room (no shadowed point lights → no lit pools on the yard through walls); sheds keep a dim work light at night. |
| 2026-10-24 | Escape tunnel (user: "very securely locked from the inside … outsiders should NEVER be able to bust through from the exit side"; "a looooong tunnel"; "half a mile long or longer"; "dimly lit"; "hidden/buried"; "too conspicuous out there in an open field") | A line drawn from a free wall of a bunker room out to an exit (Underground menu → Escape tunnel), ≥ 60 m and with its exit ≥ 50 m from every building; it may **run on under the neighbors' land** (up to 360 m past the line, +40 h of work for that stretch), so routes of half a mile or more are possible. **Inside** (the user's photo): a whitewashed, board-formed concrete arch you walk upright in (2.2 m wide, 2.6 m to the crown), a wet concrete floor with a drain gutter, a cable run on hooks, and one caged warm bulb every 24 m: dim (only the four nearest bulbs cast light). Cut and cover with a hired excavator, poured in 6 m vault sections (trees stay; only the hatch spot is cleared). The floor leaves the bunker level and follows the land at ≤ 8 % so there is always ≥ 0.9 m of earth over the vault. **Egress only, absolutely:** `canEnter(run, 'outside')` is always false: no event, raid, tool or stat may ever open it from outside (threat systems must call `canEnter` and respect it). The bunker end is a blast door shut in its frame with two steel drop-bars on the room side; the exit is a chamber with a ladder up a concrete shaft to a steel lid with no handle, hinge or hasp outside (hinges, drop-bolts and locking wheel on the underside), its collar sunk below grade. **Hidden:** in the trees it is buried under soil, a thick layer of leaves, fallen branches, a fallen trunk and brush; in open ground it sits under a hand of turf, flush with the grass, so nothing shows. Walk mode: along the tunnel, up the ladder and out from below; from above, standing on it does nothing. Rules: not under the creek, wells, the septic field or other rooms; exit shaft ≤ 9 m. |
| 2026-10-24 | Bunker light (user: "dampen the lights … more warm lighting") | Bunker and tunnel lamps are warm (≈ 3000 K) and low; no daylight underground. |
| 2026-10-24 | Bunker hardening (user: "lined with nuclear proof/fallout survival material, steel cage/faraday cage shield emp, etc. … upgrades that obviously cost more money") | Four upgrades, each in three tiers, ordered per room in the Hardening panel or with "Whole bunker"; the family fits them as retrofit jobs (sim/hardening.ts). **Fallout:** Basic: extra earth and a shielded hatch (PF 40) → Shelter: dense concrete and a baffled entry (PF 1,000) → Military: lead and steel lining (PF 10,000+). An unhardened room is PF 20. **Faraday (EMP):** Mesh cage (70 % of electronics inside survive) → Bonded cage, gasketed doors, filtered entries (97 %) → Welded shell (100 %). **NBC air:** Particulate HEPA and hand crank (keeps out fallout dust) → Gas-tight: carbon and blast valves → Overpressure. **Blast:** blast doors on every opening (15 psi) → reinforced roof and I-beams (50 psi) → hardened shell and posts (100 psi). Small rooms take half the materials and time. Each tier is visible inside: copper strap, rubber door gaskets and a filtered entry box, mesh at the ceiling seams, deep concrete reveals, lead sheets with seams, the HEPA blower, red blast valves, a pressure gauge, green blast doors, I-beams and posts. Save v8. |
| 2026-10-24 | Collapse clock (GDS §4; user picks: one clock for every disaster, chained aftermath, ticker, Surprise / Scenario / Free build, day jobs, barter after) | `sim/disaster.ts`: a run goes peace → warning → impact (3 days) → survival. Eight outside-world **tracks** (grid power, fuel stations, grocery stores, deliveries, hospital & pharmacy, police & 911, phone & internet, banks & cash) each fail and recover on a curve the disaster's variant sets, with recovery days rolled per run. A follow-on (e.g. end of the rule of law after a long grid-down) combines with the main disaster; the worse level wins. **Grid-down** variants: ice storm (back in 1–4 weeks), substation attack (1–4 months), cyberattack (rolling, 2 weeks to 3 months), cascading failure of the old grid (2–12 months). Stores empty in about 3 days; police hold for about 2 weeks, then fray. **Nuclear:** distant strikes on the missile fields, a nearby base, a full exchange. **Modes:** Surprise rolls grid-down or nuclear, hidden, 3–6 years out. Scenario: pick the disaster and 1 / 3 / 5 years. Free build: nothing until you trigger it (with no / 1 / 2 / 6 weeks of warning). **Warning signs:** a news ticker along the bottom (local news in peacetime; headlines darken and the strip reddens), and prices climb up to +60 % by the hit. After it, the yard stops delivering once deliveries fall below 25 %. **Money:** start with $30,000; each adult has a day job from their strongest skill ($140–280/day take-home, weekdays, off the land while at work; quit or go back any time before the hit); household costs are $160/day while stores and banks work. Two jobs save about $50k a year, and quitting both burns savings. The jobs end when it hits. Save v9 (old saves become Free build). |
| 2026-10-24 | American spelling (user: "we american here") | All game text, docs and code use US spelling (neighbor, defense, color, center, gray, meters, liters, curb, plow, cozy). |
| 2026-10-24 | Grid power and grid-down (user: "Grid by default, go off-grid by choice") | A new game starts on the county grid: $0.14/kWh plus $0.90/day. Any building can be wired off it, and a drilled well pumps on it. Leave the grid at any time (the co-op pulls the meter); rejoining costs $250 while the grid is up. Each day the power balance groups buildings by their own power network: solar first, then the battery, then the grid while it's up, then the generator while there's fuel. Loads are served in priority order: pumps, the root cellar (freezer), homes, the coop, the workshop. Whatever can't be served goes without and is listed. **Grid-tied solar shuts off in a blackout** unless a battery (hybrid inverter) is on its network. A building on no network of its own runs on the grid or not at all. Every building has a breaker. Wired buildings without power yesterday fall back to lamplight. When the generator runs, the panel notes it can be heard about 1 km away (encounters will use this). Save v10 (old saves stay off the grid). |
| 2026-10-24 | Food and needs (user: "Calories + variety + spoilage"; "Injuries heal with care; death possible") | `sim/needs.ts`, run once a day. While the stores work the family eats from them (part of household costs). After that it eats from the stockpile: fresh food first (it spoils: 8 %/day in the open, 1.5 % in a root cellar), then freezer meat (thaws 30 %/day once the root cellar's freezer has no power), then canned, then bulk staples. Need: 2,400 kcal per adult, 1,700 per kid. **Grown food:** about a dozen hens give 3–8 eggs a day, more in summer; a kitchen garden peaks at about 2,500 kcal/day in August and September, better with a gardener; a hunter (skill 2+) sometimes gets a deer in the fall (about 40,000 kcal). **New materials:** freezer meat $65 / 10,000 kcal, a case of canned food $36 / 6,000 kcal, bulk staples $120 / 80,000 kcal, medical kit $150. A new game starts with two cases of canned food. **Each person has** health, morale, hunger (calories short, accumulated), radiation dose and wounds. Hunger and wounds cost health; a dose above about 1 Sv causes radiation sickness. Wounds heal faster with a medic, medical supplies (a kit lasts about ten wound-days) and a medical bay. Morale drifts toward a target raised by variety, a home and light, and lowered by hunger and a death. Low health or morale slows work. At 0 health a person dies (Cozy floors health at 5). |
| 2026-10-24 | Neighbors, arrivals, encounters (user: "4–6 households"; "Stance choice + vignette card"; "Threat-type matrix"; "Humanity track graded"; government "Help-or-hindrance roll") | `sim/encounters.ts`. **Neighbors:** 4–6 named households per seed (old farmer, young family, prepper, widow, ranch, loner), each with a disposition (−100 feud … 100 allies), size, a radio or not, and their own skill. They gossip in the ticker during the warning signs. Trading with and talking to them raises their disposition; waving them off lowers it. **Allies on the radio net** (disposition > 50) ride over when there's shooting. **After the hit**, someone comes every 3–30 days: more often as law and order fail and the more the place shows. **Who comes** follows the threat matrix: arrivals (family, townsfolk, a nurse, a lineman, ranch hands, each with real skills and mouths to feed), refugee columns (drawn by visibility), looters (put off by a fortress look), armed gangs (drawn by a fortress look and visibility), county requisition teams (only if the government roll is hindrance), traders, and neighbors. Nobody comes during the first two weeks of a nuclear war (fallout). **The clock pauses** at the gate. The dossier shows their strength against your defense: walls (how much of a ~240 m ring holds people, by type and growth), fighters (adults; teens count half), the armory, early warning (a hunter's eye, the radio net), and a bunker to hide in. **Stances:** hide (a bunker keeps everyone safe even if they're found; they take what's lying about), parley / trade (negotiator skill), warn off (fortress look; useless against a gang), defend (odds from defense + allies vs their strength; wounds, sometimes deaths). For arrivals: take in, a month's trial, trade, turn away. **Humanity:** taking people in raises it; turning them away or firing on refugees lowers it. The report card will read it. The escape tunnel is never a way in. Hardcore makes groups 35 % stronger, Cozy 40 % weaker. Save v12. |
| 2026-10-24 | Nuclear war (user: four warning tells, "Distant flashes + sky", "Distance-based" dash, "Managed shelter life", severity roll with a rare nuclear winter, dose per person with contaminated food and water, Faraday room protects what's inside) | `sim/nuclear.ts`. **Dose:** the dose rate at the compound follows R1 × t^-1.2 (the 7-10 rule). R1 at H+1 is 2 Sv/h for strikes on the missile fields (fallout arrives in about 6 h), 4 for a nearby base (about 1 h), 3 for a full exchange. Unsheltered, the first two weeks are lethal; in a PF 1,000 room they come to a few mSv. **Shelter PF:** bunker rooms take their hardening tier (unhardened 20); root cellar 40, log house 8, cabin 3, shop 2, open camp 1.5. **The first two weeks** everyone shelters (no outdoor work), with nobody outside the first three days and a half-hour dash after. After that adults work outside 8 h a day and take what dose there is. **Contamination** lasts two months: creek water when there's no well or cistern, and the garden's produce. **The EMP** at the strike kills the solar inverter, battery charger, generator and well-pump controller; each needs its spare (inverter, generator, pump) and a hardware kit to run again. Spares in the stockpile die too unless a Faraday room (share = its tier's survival) keeps them. **The dash:** anyone at work in town gets home ahead of distant fallout; with a nearby strike or full exchange they're hurt (burns and cuts, 0.4 Sv) and home two days later. **A full exchange** brings a nuclear winter: growing cut to 20 % and heating demand up, lifting over 1–2.5 years. Nobody comes to the gate during the shelter fortnight. **Visuals:** a flash, then a procedural mushroom cloud on the northern skyline (about 20° tall for the nearby base, about 6° for the missile fields) that rises in about 10 minutes, cools from fireball orange to tan-gray, spreads and fades over a day. A hazed, overcast sky for three weeks; gray fallout dust falling for the first two days. The HUD shows the outside dose rate (mSv/h), where everyone is sheltering and its PF, and the days until people can go back out. A "Replace the fried …" button appears on fried buildings. Save v13. |
| 2026-10-24 | Endings and the report card (user: "Rescue, new equilibrium or wipe"; "Newspaper-style + specific lines") | `sim/report.ts`. From the day it hits, the sim tallies hungry days, dry days, dark nights, calories grown against needed, wounds, the worst radiation dose, and peak headcount. **The run ends** when everyone is dead (wipe); when the outside world has been back (every track ≥ 90 %) for 30 days, at least 90 days in (recovered); or after 365 days in a row of the place feeding itself (equilibrium). The game pauses on **The Valley Courier** front page: a headline, a grade, and lines on what this build did. Examples: the cistern kept the water coming; grid-tied solar died with the grid; the batteries kept the freezer on; the shelter held; radiation sickness; nobody went hungry / N hungry days; the garden, hens and hunting covered N % of what everyone ate; no one knew first aid; every wound was dressed; who did not make it; took in / turned away; the line held / broke; the tunnel was never needed; which neighbors stood with you. **Grade:** 50 % survival, 20 % fed, 15 % humanity, 15 % raids held (a wipe is an F). Keep playing (endless) or start a new game. Groceries now need open stores *and* trucks restocking them (deliveries). Save v14. |
| 2026-10-24 | Buying land (user: named neighbor parcels, "Yes, by disposition", every land benefit, "Yes, by land type") | `sim/land.ts`. The home place is 40 acres (67 units, a quarter-quarter section). Around it, on the same survey grid, are five more forties: west, north, south and the two west corners. East is the county road. Each belongs to one of the named neighbor households. The world **surveys** each one once from the real terrain: creek bottom where the creek runs through, timber where the trees are thick, hay ground on the level flats, dry grazing elsewhere. **Prices** (2020s Mountain West, per acre): dry grazing $1,400, timber $3,200, hay ground $5,600, creek bottom $7,400. A neighbor who likes you (disposition > 50) takes 10 % off; a sore one (< −20) asks 35 % more. Prices climb up to +120 % through the warning signs. Nobody sells for cash once the banks are down. **Willingness:** a neighbor sells if their disposition is ≥ 30 or the land is up for sale; in peacetime a parcel comes up about once a year (retiring, an estate, needing the money). A forty must share a side with land you own, so the corners need the forty beside them first. **Bought land** counts everywhere the home place does: building placement, tree marking and felling, the off-parcel measure for tunnels. Its trees are replanted as near, markable trees in their own id range (the home square's tree ids never change, so saved felling jobs stay valid). Survey laths with orange flagging mark the corners of everything you own. Save v15. |
| 2026-10-24 | All sixteen disasters (user: the 16-row table; "primary + chained aftermath"; pulpy ones as unlockables) | Every disaster in the GDS table now has a ruleset on the collapse clock: warning headlines from the research, track curves, and follow-ons. **Financial collapse** (bank holiday / hyperinflation): cash stops buying anything (orders need banks above 25 %); chains into the end of the rule of law. **End of the rule of law.** **Drought:** the creek stops (nothing to haul); a hand-dug well fails in about 45 days, a drilled well holds at 35 %+; the garden grows at 30 %, or 75 % on stored water (cistern with 2,000 L+); refugees and looters up; may chain into wildfire. **Pandemic / engineered bioweapon:** anyone taken in or traded with can bring a fever (25 % / 55 % a contact), which spreads at home (6 % / 12 % a sick person a day) unless a medical bay keeps them apart; more arrivals and refugees. **Solar flare:** a grid outage of 8–24 months; a Faraday room doesn't matter (it's the long lines that burn). **Wildfire:** the world measures the trees within about 24 m of each building when fire weather starts; at the fire front each building burns with a chance that rises with that fuel (bunkers and the root cellar don't); smoke and ash in the sky. **Ash winter (Yellowstone):** a year of 25 % growing, recovering over the second; ash falls for ten days; long low roofs (shop, coop, garden shed) may come down and go back to the roofing stage. **Cyberattack:** rolling partial outages. **Asteroid:** months of warning; a flash, then an impact winter (10 % growing for two years). **Pulpy (unlocked after finishing any run):** zombies (herds drawn by noise and light: hide / defend / warn), invasion (occupation patrols), civil war between invented factions (the Cascade Compact and the Plains Alliance: militia recruiters), the unknown event (lights in the sky; half the neighbors vanish). Surprise rolls only the grounded ten (including the end of the rule of law); Scenario lists all sixteen; Free build triggers any. |
| 2026-10-24 | Landscape realism audit (user: "make sure forest densities are realistic, and other landscape features") | The canopy grid gave at most about 39 trees an acre: open ponderosa parkland. Real Douglas-fir / ponderosa stands run 100–250 stems an acre, mostly young, and aspen grows in clones of many stems. **Understory:** a second, half-cell-offset planting with its own random stream and id range (500,000 + cell, so canopy trees and their ids are untouched). Young firs (scale 0.32–0.62) grow under thick timber, and aspen suckers round its edge. **Stand-grown conifers:** in thick timber (forest > 0.7) the crown starts a third of the way up, as trees grown in a stand shed their lower limbs; you can walk under them. **Sagebrush steppe** on the dry open benches (big sagebrush, 0.6–1.1 m, ragged gray-olive sprays over a small core): instanced patches of eight plants, a near LOD with leaf cards and a cheaper far one, thinning into the timber's edge and kept off buildings, the camp, the drive and fence lines. **Willow thickets** (sandbar / Geyer willow, 2–4 m, leafy to the ground) in a dense band just above the creek water. Both fields share the vegetation material (wind, seasons, snow), hide in the underground view, and thin out on Medium and Low. |
| 2026-10-24 | Scripted playtest round 1 (user: "Scripted full runs + real play") | `scripts/playtest.test.ts` (run with `PLAYTEST=1`) plays four strategies (idle, homestead, prepper, fortress) against nine disasters for up to two years after the hit. It writes `playtest-report.md` and fails on NaN, negative stock or a stuck gate. **Fixes from it:** (1) while town is open, the well company repairs a dead pump ($850) and short water is bought; with town closed, the family hauls from the creek (morale −4) unless the creek is dry. Before this, families died of thirst beside a running creek in peacetime. (2) Visits to the gate come every 45 − 30 × (1 − law) days, at least 10; before, they were near daily. (3) Starving non-family members move on (8 % a day). (4) **Field plot** (new, 10 × 10 cells ≈ a quarter acre, deer-fenced): potatoes, dry beans and wheat, about 700,000 kcal a season by hand, dug from mid-August to mid-October. 40 % keeps dry as staples; the potatoes go in fresh. It costs a season's field seed ($180). A family of four needs about one acre plus the garden and hens to feed itself. (5) **Canning:** with a home kitchen, fresh food beyond five days of eating is put up at two canner loads a day (85 % kept). Lids are single-use ($6 a sleeve of 12); the jars come back. (6) **Equilibrium** is now counted over a fading year of food grown vs needed (≥ 95 %, at least 400 days in), so a harvest that carries the winter counts; 365 days in a row was impossible with winters. (7) The gate card shows days of food put by, now and with the newcomers. **Result:** prepper builds come through all nine; homesteads with three fields come through most; no food plan means a wipe in about 75 days. Save v16. |
| 2026-10-24 | Hands-on playtest round 1 (user: "Scripted full runs + real play") | `scripts/playUi.mjs` plays through the real UI in a browser: it places buildings with the mouse, orders through the panels, fast-forwards a week at a time, and answers the gate with its buttons. **Found and fixed:** (1) a family with $68k in the bank starved 120 days after the hit, because nothing said to spend it on food. The Pantry panel now warns during the warning signs when there are under six months put by, and after the hit when the trucks run again and food is under a month; both come with an "Order three months of staples" button. The same build then came through (recovered, 5 of 5). (2) The field and garden showed crops in the March snow; crops now stand from mid-May to late October, bare soil otherwise. (3) The Water panel says when the family is hauling from the creek. (4) Once the banks are down, land rows say "no cash sales" instead of a price. (5) The build panel no longer runs under the bottom toolbar; "1 day of food". **Follow-up:** unstarted plans are laid out in lime lines on the ground (they read from the air); looking down, the shadow frustum is fitted to the ground on screen, so tree shadows no longer stop at a hard edge at the top of the view. |
| 2026-10-24 | Regions and land listings (user: "Pick from a few listings", "Savings budget", "5 to 80 acres", choose the region and shape the land, "All seven, built now", realistic prices) | `sim/regions.ts` and `sim/site.ts`. **Seven regions:** Mountain West, Appalachia, Desert Southwest, North Woods, Great Plains, Pacific Northwest and Upper Midwest. Each sets four things. (1) A 2025 land price per acre for a 40 of homestead-grade land: $3,500, $4,500, $1,500, $2,200, $2,800, $7,500 and $5,000 respectively (USDA NASS land values and land-broker guides). (2) Odds of a creek or spring, groundwater depth and quality, timber and soil. (3) Frost dates, which set the garden season and the field harvest, plus winter cold (firewood burned) and yearly rain (rain catchment). (4) Game, and how likely each disaster is (the Surprise roll is weighted by region). **New game in three steps:** region, then land, then the family and how it plays. The land step has filters (size 5/20/40/80, water creek/spring/dry, timber, distance to town) and six listings. Price = acres × the region's per-acre price × a size factor (5 acres ×3.2, 20 ×1.4, 40 ×1, 80 ×0.85) × the features (creek ×1.35, spring ×1.12, shallow water ×1.1, very deep ×0.85, arsenic ×0.8, timber, soil, close to town up to ×1.2). Savings are $180,000, and the land comes out of it. Listings that leave less than $20,000 are marked over budget; on an open search the agent always finds one in budget. **Size on the survey grid:** 5 acres is a lot mid-forty off the road; 20 is the east half; 40 is the forty; 80 adds the west forty. The neighbor parcels ring whatever you bought, including the rest of your own forty, and are priced on the same regional scale by land type (grazing ×0.75, timber ×1, hay ×1.15, creek bottom ×1.4). **The site in play:** a dry place has the creek a quarter mile off on a neighbor's land, so hauling from it costs more morale. A spring gives 1,200 L a day. A hand-dug well needs water within 9 m. The driller bills $150 a meter past 60 m. Soil scales the garden and field (thin ×0.75, deep ×1.2). Miles to town add delivery days. The world moves the creek off the place when it isn't on it, scales the timber, and puts the camp, the gate and the corner pins on the land you bought. Save v17 (old saves become the classic Mountain West 40 with the creek). Only the Mountain West world is built so far; the other six are listed as coming as each one's terrain, trees and look are built. |
| 2026-10-24 | HUD panels (user: "make the panels more organized/easier to read") | The left panel has three tabs: **Build** (groups you can fold: home & work, food, water & power, pipes & cables, underground, perimeter, woods; each item is one tidy row with the price on the right and the hours and notes under it), **Projects** (the selected building's progress, services and hardening, plus lines going in and the woods), and **Log**. Picking a building on the map opens Projects. The right panel has five tabs: **Family** (each person with health and morale bars, then work and money), **Supplies** (food and the stockpile, with the order list), **Utilities** (power and water), **Land** (the place and the land next door) and **Outside** (the outside world and the neighbors). A colored dot on a tab means something there needs attention. Values are label-and-value rows; warnings are colored notes. |

Hardening tiers cost rebar, concrete and blast valves in proportion. **Hired crews** (`sim/crews.ts`): hire a local crew of four for any unfinished building from the Projects tab. They work 8 to 5 on weekdays at $60 a person-hour (2025 rural rates), billing only the hours they work. | 2026-10-24 | What comes with the land (user: some listings come with improvements; "Both kinds" of house; "Off-grid until you pay (Recommended)"; "Gravel drive at a $/ft quote (Recommended)"; "if it's just raw land, there's nothing and you have to build and buy") | `sim/improvements.ts`. **Improved listings** (about 2 in 5; more of the 5-acre lots near town) have power in, a gravel drive, a drilled well and a septic system, and a house. The house is an old two-storey farmhouse (1920–75) or a 1990s manufactured home, both needing work, or a newer cabin or log house (2005–20) in good shape. About half also have a barn. Each old system has a condition (good / fair / poor): the house's roof, wiring and plumbing, the barn roof, the well pump and the septic leach field. **Asking price** is the land plus the improvements at 2025 rural resale: farmhouse $210k, manufactured home $75k, cabin $130k, log house $260k, each scaled by condition; barn $40k; well $16k plus depth; septic $13k; power $9k; drive $4k. **In play:** the world sites them on open, level ground by the camp, with a 3 m yard round each. The house comes piped to the well and septic and wired where those systems are sound. A poor roof makes the stove burn 35 % more (fair: 10 %). Poor wiring or plumbing is shut off until it's redone. A failing leach field doesn't count as septic. A worn-out pump starts broken (the well company fixes it for $850 while town is open). Each repair has its hours and materials (re-roof 160 h, 14 roofing, 3 lumber, 1 hardware; rewire 120 h, 5 cable, 2 hardware; replumb 90 h, 5 pipe, 1 fixtures; new leach field 60 h, 2 excavator days). The family or a hired crew does them, and Projects lists them with an "Order the repair materials" button. **Raw land has nothing.** The listing gives the feet of line to the nearest pole and the drive's length. **Power:** the co-op runs the line at $28/ft plus $3,500 for the transformer and meter, in about 5 weeks plus a day per 150 ft. You're off the grid until it's in. **Drive:** a 12 ft gravel drive with a culvert, $20/ft plus $1,200, in 5 days plus a day per 300 ft. Until it's in, the truck leaves deliveries at the road by the gate (a pile you can see) and the family carries them in first (lumber 1.5 h a bundle, fieldstone 2 h, roofing 1 h, …). A two-track shows where the drive will go, and the truck stops at the gate. Both hookups are ordered from the Land tab, and both stall if the banks or the law fail. **New buildings:** the old farmhouse (white clapboard peeling to gray, asphalt shingles, a front porch, two storeys, a basement that serves as a PF 10 shelter), the manufactured home (vinyl siding, skirting, wooden steps), and a 12 × 18 m board-and-batten barn with a hay loft (PF 2; also in the build menu, ≈ 900 h). Listing filter: Built (any / raw land / with a house). Save v19. |
| 2026-10-24 | The world doesn't always come back (user: "it's not a given that the world recovers, each playthrough is random and its own thing. should vary based on what the disaster is") | `disaster.ts`. Each run rolls a hidden **fate**, on its own random stream, so a run's other rolls don't move. **Full:** services come all the way back on the variant's curves. **Partial:** they start back 1.5× later and stop part way, each between 35 % and 80 % of normal, and stay there: power some hours, the store open some days. **Never:** nothing comes back. Odds [full, partial, never] by disaster: wildfire 92/7/1, grid-down 68/24/8, cyberattack 65/27/8, pandemic 62/30/8, end of the rule of law 45/38/17, financial collapse 40/42/18, drought 35/45/20, solar flare 30/42/28, ash winter 20/45/35, nuclear war 12/43/45. Variants that never come back (the nearby base, a full exchange, the pulpy ones) stay that way. Follow-on disasters share the fate. The fate shows itself only slowly in the news: "some things are coming back, but not the way they were"; at day 120, "nobody is coming". **Endings:** recovered (full), **a new normal** (partial: everything back as far as it's coming for 30 days, at least 90 days in; "A thinner world" on the Courier), or the place feeding itself (equilibrium) or a wipe. The long game (endless play) builds on this. Save v20. |

Ages follow the household: parents 29–45 with kids, 27–57 without; kids 18+ years younger than a parent; grandparents 60+. | 2026-10-24 | The paperwork (user: "Legal & paperwork"; permits "Real but light"; afterwards "Fades with the tracks") | `sim/paperwork.ts`. **Closing costs:** 3 % on top of the land price (title, escrow, survey, recording). **Property tax:** the county rate on the assessed value, billed in halves on May 1 and November 1. Rates by region: Mountain West 0.55 %, Appalachia 0.5 %, Desert 0.6 %, North Woods 1.2 %, Plains 1.3 %, PNW 0.9 %, Upper Midwest 1.5 %. The assessed value is the land paid for plus 1.6× the materials of everything built. Two finished fields put the land on the ag rate (40 %). **Insurance:** $1,200 plus 0.6 % of the dwelling's value a year, +30 % more than 8 miles from town, +$250 for the wood stove, paid monthly. On by default; drop it on the Land tab. While the banks work, a building burned or a roof brought down by ash is paid out at its materials. **Permits:** homes, the shop, the barn, the root cellar, wells, septic, solar and bunker rooms need one. Fees run from $300 to $4,000, scaled by how strict the county is. Work waits for the county's review: 21 days for a home, 7 for the rest, also scaled by strictness. Strictness by region: PNW 1.6, Upper Midwest 1.3, Mountain West 1, North Woods 0.8, Appalachia and Desert 0.6, Plains 0.5. **Skip permits** (Land tab) and there are no fees or waits, but an inspector may see the work from the road: about 0.25 % a day × strictness while it's going up and for a year after. Then it's a fine (3× the fee + $500), the permit at double, and a two-week stop-work order if it isn't finished. **When the county stops working** (banks or law below half), there are no permits to pull, no inspectors and no bills. The tax piles up as back taxes, billed if banks and law come back (≥ 90 %), and no insurer pays out. Save v21. |
| 2026-10-24 | Seasons and upkeep (user: "Seasons & upkeep") | `sim/upkeep.ts`. **Seasoning:** wood you fell goes on the green pile and is ready to burn after about nine months (270 days). Seasoned firewood can now be bought ($110 a rick, about $330 a cord delivered). With no seasoned wood, the stove burns green: 1.5 ricks for the heat of one, and three times the creosote. **Creosote:** each rick burned lays down creosote (1 seasoned, 3 green). Past a dirty flue (25), a hard-burning cold day risks a chimney fire, at 0.2 % × (creosote − 25) × demand a day. Seven in ten burn out and crack the liner ($1,800 to reline while town works); three in ten get into the roof, which goes back to the roof stage (insured: 35 % of its materials paid). Sweep it from the Utilities tab: a sweep for $250 while town works, or yourselves with a brush. **Frozen pipes:** a plumbed house with no fire on a hard-freeze night has a 25 % chance a night that the pipes burst. Running water stops until the plumbing is fixed (20 h, a coil of pipe and a hardware kit). **Snow:** in regions with real winters (cold ≥ 0.9), from December to February a drive longer than 250 ft drifts shut and deliveries wait 2–5 days, unless there's a plow blade on the truck ($3,500). **Mud season:** from about three weeks before the last frost to two weeks after, the county's weight limits hold heavy loads (ready-mix, tanks, a container, the excavator, the drill rig) until the road firms up. Save v22. |
| 2026-10-24 | Shelf life and pests (user: "Shelf life & pests") | `sim/shelf.ts`. **Ages:** canned food, bulk staples, frozen meat, medicine, field seed and gas each carry an average age. New stock comes in fresh and pulls the average down. **Shelf lives:** cans 4 years, bucketed staples 10, frozen meat 1, medicine 3, seed 3, gas 6 months (2 years with stabilizer, $15 a bottle for ten cans). Past them a good slowly spoils, per day: cans 0.03 %, staples 0.01 %, freezer burn 0.1 %, medicine 0.05 %, seed 0.06 %, stale gas 0.25 %. **Rotate the pantry** (Supplies): eat what you store while the stores are open, and the grocery run replaces it, so the stores stay young. No extra money, just the habit. **Mice:** without traps ($35 a box, about two months), they take about 17 % of the staples a year (none in a concrete bunker storeroom), plus seed and fresh food. **Pack rats** chew the wiring in a wired building about once every four months, and it's dead until rewired (24 h). The Supplies tab shows each good's age against its shelf life, the rotation switch and the traps. Save v23. |
| 2026-10-24 | Body and household (user: "Body & household") | `sim/body.ts`. **Chronic conditions:** seeded per person, on their own random stream, by rough US prevalence. High blood pressure: 12 % of adults under 45, 35 % at 45+, 60 % of grandparents. Underactive thyroid: 5 % of adults, 15 % of grandparents. Asthma: 8 % of anyone. Type 1 diabetes: about 1 %. **Medicine:** while the pharmacy is open it's refilled monthly at a copay ($10–35 a month per prescription; insulin at the $35 cap). You can fill up to 90 days ahead from the Family tab, as far as insurance covers. When it runs out: blood pressure wears on you from the second month, with a small daily chance of a stroke; thyroid saps health and morale after two months; asthma brings attacks (2 % a day); without insulin, health falls 4 a day, so a few weeks. Insulin keeps about a month without a fridge. **Dental:** about one toothache per person every three years. The dentist ($450) while town works; after that it's a wound that heals with medicine and a medic. **Hygiene:** soap, toilet paper, toothpaste and pads ($25 a person-month) come with the groceries. Once the stores close they come out of the stockpile, and without them morale sinks and stomach bugs go round. The Family tab shows each person's conditions and days of medicine left. Save v24. |
| 2026-10-24 | Money, metals and the shop window (user: "Accounts you manage", "Catalog window", "Spot + dealer premiums"; earlier: "nest egg depends on the disaster", gold and silver kinds, storage, barter drifting with scarcity, spot modeled from 2025) | `sim/finance.ts`, `ui/ShopWindow.tsx`. **Accounts:** checking (what everything is paid from), savings (4 %), a brokerage index fund (≈7 % a year, 16 % volatility), cash in the safe, and metals. Move money between them while the banks are open; cash withdrawals over $10,000 are reported. **What a disaster does:** the market crashes on the day it hits (financial collapse −45 %, nuclear −50 %, solar flare −30 %, pandemic −30 %, grid-down −15 % …). Checking, savings and brokerage freeze with the banks. A financial collapse that doesn't fully come back takes a 30 % bail-in on balances over the insured $250,000. **Cash** spends when the banks are shut but the trucks still run and the law mostly holds; it buys less as prices climb. **Metals:** gold eagles (6 % over spot), 10 oz gold bars (2.5 %), silver eagles (18 %), silver rounds (7 %) and junk silver ($1 face = 0.715 oz, 10 %). Sold back a little under spot. Spot starts at about $3,400 gold and $38 silver, walks daily (15 % / 25 % volatility), and runs up as trust in money fails (financial collapse up to +120 % gold, more for silver). Dealers ship insured in about a week; buys of $10,000+ go on the dealer's books (for OPSEC later). Keep it in the safe, buried (out of looters' reach, dig it up to sell or trade), or in a safe-deposit box ($150 a year, out of reach while the bank is shut). Barter value at the gate = spot, bid up by scarcity (up to +60 %). **Shop window** ("Shop & money" in the top bar): tabs for Building, Bunker, Water & power, Food, Medical & household, Fuel & tools, Gold & silver and Money. Item cards have a drawn icon, price at today's level, unit and what you have, with a cart on the side and one order. Save v25. |

The background check clears at the counter, except about 1 in 10 is delayed three business days; Washington (PNW) has a 10-business-day wait. Every sale goes on the shop's records (counted for OPSEC). In the warning signs, panic buying runs gun and ammo prices up to +120 % and limits ammo to 4 boxes a caliber. Warning them off works better armed (+15) and worse unarmed (−10). Guns left out in a house with kids under 13 risk an accident (0.04 % a gun a day). A gun safe ($1,800) holds them. Save v26. |

The dogs eat about 1,000 kcal a day from the stores once the stores close. **Hardening:** steel entry doors ($1,200, +5 barrier each up to 10), window bars ($900, +8), sandbags (100 for $300, filled with your own dirt; +5 per hundred up to 15), and a safe room ($8,500), where the family hides like in a bunker (nobody hurt running for the timber). A tanker load (15,000 L, $450) fills the storage in one go (Utilities tab). **Storage:** cisterns and the tower, plus IBC totes ($180, 1,000 L each). Sealed 5-gallon jugs ($9, 19 L) are drinking water put by; when water runs short they cover drinking and cooking (4 L a person). **Hauling:** with a water trailer it's one trip a day, so a creek on the neighbor's land costs no more morale than one on your own. **Treatment:** creek water untreated gives giardia (0.6 % a day), unless there's a gravity ceramic filter ($350, about a year of cartridges for a family) or it's boiled (0.05 rick of firewood a day). Arsenic in the well wears on health (−0.01 a day each) without reverse osmosis for drinking water ($1,200). Hard water scales the pump (about one seizure in four years) without a softener ($900). **Greywater:** a diverter and mulch basins ($900) send sink and shower water to the garden, which then needs 200 L a day of well water, not 400 (with a plumbed home). Timber on the place makes big game likelier (×0.6 open … ×1.4 heavy). **Peacetime:** deer season about Oct 7–Nov 26 (tag $40); elk Sep 7–Oct 27 where there are elk ($60); small game fall and winter ($25); fishing May 10–Oct 27 ($35 license). Licenses run by the year. Hunt without a tag (poach) and the warden catches 15 % of kills: a $1,500 fine and the meat taken. **After it hits:** no seasons, no wardens, everyone hunting. Big game thins 0.12 % a day while the stores are shut (small game and fish slower, 0.06 %), each kill takes more (2 % big, 0.4 % small), and it comes back slowly. An ash winter kills half the game; a drought kills four fifths of the fish; fallout makes game and fish hot for 150 days (dose with every meal of it). **Gear:** big game needs a hunter (skill 2+) and a rifle with rounds (`armory.ts`), with chances of 3 % a day × skill/5 × abundance × timber, halved in deep winter. An elk is 120,000 kcal, a moose 180,000, a deer 40,000. Snares ($40) or a .22 / shotgun get rabbits; fishing gear ($120) gets fish from the creek (15 % a day if it runs through the place, 6 % down the road). The meat goes to the freezer; with no freezer, a smoker ($300) turns big kills into jerky that keeps like staples (80 % of it); otherwise it's eaten fresh. Hunted meat counts toward food grown. New calibers 7.62×39 ($10 a box of 20) and .45 ACP ($30 for 50). Washington (the PNW region) has banned sales of AR / AK / MP5-pattern guns since 2023; the Mini-14, SKS and M1A aren't on its list. **Machine guns:** full auto is the National Firearms Act. Only guns registered before May 1986 can go to a civilian, so they're collector pieces: a MAC-10 $9,000, an Uzi $18,000, an AK $28,000, a Colt M16A1 $35,000, an H&K MP5 $45,000. Pay the dealer and the $200 tax stamp, send ATF Form 4 with fingerprints, and wait 4–9 months. The gun waits at the dealer until it's approved and a dealer is open to pick it up from: buy late and the collapse may keep it there. They can't be bought in Washington, or once the phones are down. **In play:** at the gate a full-auto rifle counts 1.5 (a semi rifle 1.2) and a sub-gun 1.25 (a semi 9mm carbine 1.0). Full auto burns three times the rounds in a fight and needs 90 rounds on hand to count at all. A 7.62×39 rifle hunts deer. No save change (new guns and calibers fit the armory as it is). |
| 2026-10-24 | The long game (task #34: "endless play with eras (varied next era, escalating toward permanent collapse), aging + heirs + births, chronicle, dynasty score, milestones"; earlier: "it's not a given that the world recovers") | `sim/eras.ts`, `sim/lineage.ts`, `disaster.ts nextEraDisaster`, right panel → Years. **Eras:** the report card's "Keep playing: the next era" closes the era (grade and fate go in the chronicle; the score goes in the dynasty score). Once the world has settled (back to where it's going, a year of a world that never came back, or three years at most), the next era is rolled. It is a different disaster, and one that still has something to knock down: no grid-down, cyberattack or solar flare where the grid is gone, no financial collapse with no banks, no lawlessness with no law. It starts from where the last era left the world: services never rise above that baseline, which mends 5 % a year if anything is left (above 30 %). Each era escalates: the quiet before it shrinks from 4–8 years (era II) to ½–1½ years, the odds of a full recovery fall 20 % an era (partial 8 %), and the rest goes to never. Only a wipe ends the line. Free build: the next era is quiet until you start one. **Aging:** birthdays (a seeded day of the year). At 18 a kid is an adult, with a day job if the town still works; at 67 an adult retires and is an elder. Kids learn a point of the household's best skill most years from age 6, and a bit of everything with a teacher in the house. **Old age:** a yearly risk of 0.003 % × e^(0.095·age) (≈1 % at 60, 6 % at 80, 16 % at 90; US life tables), doubled with no medical care. **Partners:** an unpartnered adult of 20–45 meets someone (12 % a year while the law holds, 5 % after). That's someone already living here who isn't kin, within 10 years of age, or someone who marries in from town (after: "came up the road one spring and stayed"). Partners are opposite-sex 88 % of the time. **Births:** a partnered couple who can have a child: 10 % a year (7 % once the stores are gone), not within two years of the last birth, rarer with four kids. The mother is 18–43. With no hospital and no medic (skill 5) or bunker medical bay, 4 % of babies and 1.5 % of mothers don't survive. Couples who can't have a child adopt (4 % a year), or after the collapse take in a child with no one left. Children take the family name, are kin and are a generation down. Babies and toddlers eat 1,000 kcal a day. Married-in kin never leave when food runs short (only people taken in do). **Chronicle:** eras, births, deaths, partnerships, coming of age, retirements and milestones, with the year. **Milestones:** first winter (10), a home of your own making (10), the first era (25), three eras (75), the first child born on the place (20), a child of the place comes of age (40), three generations (40), a dozen at the table (30), 10 / 25 / 50 / 100 years (30 / 60 / 120 / 250), five years in a world that never came back (60). **Dynasty score:** a point per person-year on the place, each era's score (0–100), 25 per child and the milestones. Save v30 (old saves: the founders become kin, the couple partners, the kids theirs). |

The pigs go to the butcher in November (about 165,000 kcal each); the steer too once it has had 150 days on the place (about 450,000 kcal). Let him go (humanity +2), or after a month, healed and at trust 70+, let him join the family. **Leave them:** humanity −2 −1 a man; morale −4. **Finish it:** humanity −12 −4 a man, everyone's morale −15 (the kids hear it). Stances: **Lock in and call 911** (sheriff 15–45 min by region and miles to town: Mountain West / Plains 20–45, Desert / North Woods 25–45, Appalachia 20–40, PNW / Upper Midwest 15–30; they stay 8–15 min and usually leave first with loose cash, guns not in a safe, tools, pills; caught in the drive if the deputies are quick), **Lights on and shout** (≈ 70 % run; otherwise a confrontation), **Confront them** (a peacetime battle: pistols at most, no armor or explosives, they start at the house, no breach, they run at the first shot; "I called 911!"). **After a shooting:** the state's castle doctrine (Idaho, Tennessee, Arizona, Maine, Kansas, Washington, Wisconsin; 88–96 % justified inside at night): $5k–$25k in lawyers and the guns used held as evidence 30–120 days; shot while running (someone firing after them across the yard) → charged, $30k–$90k, 45 % convicted → a felony record: no guns or ammunition (they go to a dealer at half value), no buying either; a civil suit (5 % justified and dismissed under immunity, 40 % otherwise); the local news (a fading OPSEC "news" source). Deputies on the place see what's in plain view (harder after a shooting, with a warrant): contraband guns or gear or mines → a federal felony, everything illegal seized (`blackMarket.seize`), $25k + $5k an item, $40k lawyers, 80 % convicted. Held guns stay in the locker while the law is down. |

Tests: `interiorDetail.test.ts` (floors, irrigation, walkable aisle, clear truck bay, per-building mesh budget), scale lines for the new pieces. |

| 2026-10-24 | Getting the taken back (user, on ruthless crews: "evil raiders could straight up execute people including the kids or take them and kidnap them … think walking dead or the road or mad max. not all groups will be that ruthless"; then approved the three ways back offered: radio rumors, a rescue raid, a ransom trade) | `sim/rescue.ts`, Outside → **Taken**, the gate card. **A record per child taken** (`rescue.captives`): the crew (a name like "the Dry Creek crew"), its temper, how many came, the day; and, hidden, how many hold the camp (those who came, less the fallen, plus 0–4 who stayed), where it is (4–34 km) and what has become of the child. **Fate:** a seeded daily hazard (0.015 + 0.0004 × days held; ×0.6 for a hard crew, ×0.4 desperate): about 9 in 10 still held after a week, half after a month, 1 in 5 after two months; then traded on (55 %, out of reach) or dead. The crew moves camp every ~25 days. **Leads** (what the family knows): the radio (a ham rig 5 %/day, a shortwave 2.5 %/day; true at the set's reception odds), a friendly neighbor riding over (1.2 %/day each, up to 3; true 3 in 4), a trader or neighbor at the gate after a trade or a talk (half the time; true 6 in 10), a prisoner who talks (true 9 in 10); all fading with the trail (× e^(−days/60)). A lead gives the place, the distance, a head count (±20–45 %) and whether the child was seen alive, traded on or buried; it is warm 10 days, cooling to 21, then cold. **Ransom:** 45 % of savage crews deal (hard 65 %, desperate 80 %); they send word unasked now and then (3.5 %/day fading, from day 4), or answer word the family sends through whoever gave a warm lead (2–5 days). They ask 2–3 of: 5–10 cans of fuel, 150–300 rounds of the family's main caliber, 2–5 med kits, 12–30 cases of food (×1.3 savage). Pay, haggle (35 % + 7 × the best negotiator − 15 for a savage crew: six parts in ten; fail and they walk) or refuse (half the time they won't ask again). A double-cross: 35 % savage, 20 % hard, 10 % desperate, and always if they no longer have the child: half keep the goods, half come to the gate shooting (the compound fight; if it holds, 4 in 10 times the child is left in the ditch). **Rescue raid:** pick who goes (adults and teens 15+, health 35+, no bad wound); they leave tonight, hit the camp before dawn (a day later past 25 km) and are back the day after. While they're away they're off the line (`task: 'away'`, out of `postureOf` and `planBattle`): the panel says who stays and how many can shoot. A wrong or stale lead: an empty camp. Otherwise the camp fight (`fightAtCamp`): battle.ts's hit chance, `hitRoll` (where a round lands, plates / soft vest / helmet, kills) and wounds; surprise (0.75 − 0.08 per fighter past two + 0.04 × the best hunter − 0.3 if they keep dogs + 0.1 with night vision); on surprise, a quiet snatch (15 % + 5 % × hunter − 6 % per extra fighter + 10 % NVG, up to 60 %); the camp lit by its fires, the team in the dark; the team goes in at ~18 m once the camp is down to their number or after a minute; the crew breaks at 55 % down (savage), 50 % (hard), 40 % (desperate); the team pulls back at half down or badly outnumbered, carrying the downed it can (the rest crawl off half the time, or are shot); every shot in the camp can hit the child (0.08 % a round from cover, 0.3 % in the assault; half killed, half grazed). Odds measured over 200 seeds, a savage camp: two untrained parents with ARs vs 4: about 1 in 7 home; two trained (skill 6) with plates, helmets and NVG vs 4 / 7 / 10: 8 / 5 / 2 in 10, team deaths 0.03 / 0.11 / 0.13 a raid; one adult with a deer rifle: ~6 % (nearly all by a quiet snatch). "Weigh the odds" in the panel runs 40 fights on the lead's head count (a guess). A win brings back 4–13 canned and 1–4 fuel and +0.3 respect; a loss moves the camp and a savage crew kills the child 1 time in 4. **Coming home:** `died` and `taken` cleared, back to the day's work and meals, missed birthdays counted, thin and hungry (health ≤ 85 − 0.8/day gone, floor 35), morale 20, "home from captivity: nightmares, won't sleep alone" (heals in 3–8 weeks); the rest of the family +12 morale; written back into the chronicle (⌂). **Give up:** the family −10 morale; "stopped looking" in the chronicle. Lines in the log and ticker are the parents talking (frightened, determined). A wipe still ends the run: the taken are not alive on the place. **The user's follow-ups (2026-10-25):** (1) "Allies can join rescue raids": the households that ride over to a fight at the gate (home, on the radio net, disposition > 50) each send a man (two if 3+ at home) when asked ("Ask the neighbors" in the panel). They fight as country shots (skill 3–5, a semi-auto, 90 rounds, no armor) and count toward the team's numbers. Two untrained parents with ARs vs a savage camp of 4: 14 % alone, ~66 % with 2 neighbors' men, ~84 % with 4; vs 7: 7 % / 39 % / 57 %, at 0.2–0.4 neighbor deaths a raid. A dead man costs his household a member (none left: they're gone) and 25 goodwill; a failed raid −10, a wound −3; a clean success +8. (2) "Traded on gets a long-shot trail": a child traded on is at a trading post or work camp 80–400 km off (moved every ~2 months, dies 0.3 %/day). Rare radio rumors only: 0.8 %/day with a ham, 0.4 % shortwave, 0.15 % with none, fading over 8 months (a shortwave hears one in the first eight months about 45 % of the time); true at the set's reception odds. A rumor is good for 45 days. "Take the truck": up to three go in a running pickup; fuel there and back by the truck's mpg (≈ 100 L for 240 km in the used pickup, from the tank then the jerry cans), 3 % wear, a day on the road per 150 km each way. On the road 1 in 5 trips hits a roadblock (someone wounded). At the post, on a true and current rumor: bought back 65 % of the time if they carry the price (fuel / ammo / meds / food, as a ransom), 25 % if not; otherwise the man won't sell and moves the child on. A wrong rumor or a moved child: nobody knows the face (or, if the child died there, the grave). (3) "Hard crews take adults for labor": a hard crew that gets in may take one working-age adult (16–55) hiding in the house or down on the ground (6 % a second while they're inside; ~1 in 3 breaches of an unarmed place). Hard crews deal 80 % of the time (was 65 %), send word 1.5× as often, and of their captives who aren't held any more only 20 % are dead (savage 45 %, desperate 30 %); hazard ×0.6. Lines read by the partner for an adult taken. Taking a worker doesn't mean the 90 % strip a savage crew does. (4) "Keep the grim odds as they are": unchanged. Still save v38 (new optional fields only: `Captive.farWhere / farKm / farDir`, `Lead.far`, `RescueRaid.kind / allies`). Save v38: `rescue` (`captives`, `raid`); old saves get an empty record, and anyone already taken gets one the first day. |

The room's point light follows the same answer. The window/lamp system is the existing one (`kit_glow`, `setWindowGlow`), extended with variants, not duplicated. **Fire pit** (`props/firePit.ts`, a reusable prop: stone ring, coals, flames, log seats, flicker light on High/Medium, smoke): it replaces the camp's own ring at the same spot; it burns as the camp cookfire before a house stands and, after, of an evening when people are sat out round it (not in rain, not on deep-winter nights, not after dark under blackout once the collapse has come). **Flashlight cones** (`fx/Flashlights.ts`) are attached to whoever has task `watch` (the daily-life schedule will assign it), else, while there is a threat (a radio warning, someone at the gate, a fight), to the adults awake outdoors; one instanced additive draw, plus one real spotlight on High. **Generator**: when the generator made power yesterday, exhaust from the shed and a blinking green running light through the evening (no audio in the game). **Weathering** (`aging.ts` pure, `weathering.ts` shader): one `aWear` vec4 per building / fence mesh read by the shared kit materials (no material clones): unpainted wood goes silver-gray in a couple of years board by board, paint chalks after ~5 years and peels by ~15, galvanized roofs and the container wall rust in streaks and blotches (containers from ~2 years, roofs from ~8), moss on north pitches and low stone scaled by the region's rain (little in the Mountain West, a lot in the PNW), splash grime at the foot of walls. Age is years since `finishedAt`; what came with the land adds its build year and a worn roof's condition. Fields and gardens go to weeds within a summer of nobody tending them and to saplings after a few years (and weeds come up round every wall of an empty place). **Seasons** (`seasonCover.ts` pure): drifts against every wall (deep in the lee of the westerlies, scoured on the windward side) and both faces of the container wall, one merged mesh scaled to the lying snow (High/Medium); mud season round the thaw (the same window as the county's road weight limits) darkens bare ground and the drive, with water standing in the ruts; morning frost on cold clear mornings in the shoulder seasons; a stock tank by the barn or coop that ices over in winter and on hard-frost mornings. Snow on roofs and trees, frozen creeks and ponds, and fall color were already in `engine/world/season` and are reused; frost and mud ride in the season patch's spare uniform (`uSeaD.y/z`, `writeSeasonExtras`). |
| 2026-10-25 | Encounter realism 1: honest fight odds and the battle bugs behind them (playtest round 3 Q2) | **The gate card shows the battle sim's own odds** (`encounters.fightOdds`: `planBattle` 24 times with other seeds, 10–16 for a crew of 15+, cached per encounter, ~60 ms): "you hold about N in 10 · someone of yours killed or taken about N in 10". The strength-vs-defense number is gone from the card. **Bugs fixed in `sim/battle.ts`:** (a) raiders always started 30–34 units (≈ 190 m) from the house, so with a palisade 21+ out they were at the wall before the alarm, and the family arrived at the wall posts with the raiders already there; they now start 22 units beyond the line, and the watch's sighting distance is measured from the line; (b) any armed pair after day 120 had up to a 50 % chance of a rocket, and the blast at the house hit the children sheltering in it at full force; rockets are now for organized crews only (gangs, militia, patrols, requisition; `organized`), grenades rare for the rest, and someone sheltering in the house takes 35 % of a blast; (c) prepared positions gave worse cover than a raider in a ditch (wall 0.55, house 0.5–0.6 against 0.3); now wall and sandbags 0.22, tower 0.28, house 0.35 (bars 0.28), raider cover 0.4, and fire into solid cover rattles 30 % less; (d) a breach went by head count alone: unarmed crews now need 4 to 1, and each rifle on the wall slows the climb. **Double take fixed:** a lost fight no longer takes the raiders' wants on top of `applyBattle`'s 30–90 % (`encounters.ts`). **Bot:** hides when the odds say it would lose (under 60 % to hold, or over 25 % to lose someone); the prepper fights when it holds ≥ 85 % and loses someone ≤ 10 % (`PREPPER_HIDES=1` for the old always-hide). Playtest (36 runs), before → after: odds calibration on 76 fights: predicted < 25 % → won 2 of 21, 25–50 % → 5 of 10, 50–75 % → 3 of 7, ≥ 75 % (mean 98 %) → 36 of 38; the old ratio over 1.5 had won 1 of 15. Wipes 14 → 11, alive 83 → 95 of 154, killed or taken in fights 36 → 20 (homestead 17 → 5, fortress 19 → 15); the homestead's wipes 3 → 1. |

Looters, gangs and militia may torch an outbuilding (8 % desperate, 20 % hard, 40 % savage). **They come back** (50 / 70 / 85 % by temper, once): in 10–25 days, sometimes with one or two more, with the same temper; a crew that's back finds the place 20 points likelier, and a hard one back may follow the trail into the timber (35 %) as a savage one already does with dogs. A statement every 30 days is paid from checking as far as it goes; under the minimum (1 % + the month's interest, at least $35) is late with a $40 fee; 6 missed statements → charged off to collections; 18 → a judgment: a lien on the land (`land.buyProblem` refuses a purchase) and 25 % of day-job wages garnished. Paid off, the lien is released. **While the banks are shut nobody collects:** no interest, no statements, no garnishment (the balance sits there). Shown in the top bar ("owe $X") and under Shop & money → Accounts, with a Pay-it-down button. Playtest: before, checking ran to −$7,668 (grid-down prepper) and −$2,813 (pandemic prepper); after, those two owed up to $7,789 and $3,188, both reaching collections before the banks closed. |

| 2026-10-25 | Granular food (user: "itemized stores (rice, beans, flour, oats, canned veg/meat/fruit, frozen, snacks, oil, salt, sugar, honey…) with nutrition/shelf life, pantry menu, and food graphics on shelves wherever food is stored") | `sim/pantry.ts`, Supplies → Pantry, shop Food tab, `game/props/foodShelfPlan.ts` + `FoodShelfRenderer.ts`; save v39. **Compatible by design:** the aggregate `stock.staples / canned / frozen` stay the currency every system reads and writes; `s.pantry` is a breakdown inside them (item → real units + age). `syncPantry` reconciles: a drop it didn't make (raiders, barter, the prisoners' rations, a thaw) comes out of the items in proportion (a 10 %+ raid takes salt and coffee too); a rise it didn't make (gifts, a generic order) comes in as the category's default mix. Eating, named purchases, canning (home-canned jars), the field harvest (home-grown beans & wheat) and the smoker / freezer (smoked meat, game) go through the pantry by name. **27 items** with kcal, protein, fat, shelf life, storage and 2025 prices: rice / pinto / wheat in sealed Mylar buckets (30 yr, $55–70), rice and black beans in bags (3–4 yr, mice get in), flour and cornmeal (1 yr), oats, pasta, sugar / honey / salt (forever), oil (1.5 yr), powdered milk and freeze-dried #10 cans (20–30 yr), peanut butter, coffee, canned vegetables / fruit / meat / soup (2–4 yr), home-canned jars (2 yr), snacks, freezer meat. Cool-stored items (oil, flour, oats, cornmeal, PB, home-canned) keep 70 % as long without a root cellar or buried storeroom. **Past "best by" it is still food** (user: "expired food can still be eaten most of the time, just less effective cal wise and risk getting food sick"; `DECAY`, `quality`, `sickness`): each item has a decay kind. Calories and food value fade toward a floor (sealed grain −12 % over about four years, cans −25 %, home-canned, rancid goods, frozen and snacks −30 %; honey, sugar, salt never), so eating old food takes more of it for the same calories. Sickness odds (a day of eating nothing else, kcal-weighted over the plate, rolled per person): zero in date and for honey / sugar / salt; sealed dry goods ≤ 0.2 %; bags ≤ 0.6 %; commercial cans 0.4 %/year past, to 3 %; rancid oil, flour and nuts 0.6 %/year to 2 % (stomach upsets); home-canned the same plus botulism 0.002 % in date rising 0.02 %/year to 0.1 %; meat from a dead freezer 2 %. Food poisoning (sev 20) is gone in 3–5 days with water and faster with medicine; botulism (sev 80) mends with medicine, a medic or the medical bay, and without care costs 2.5 health a day on top of the wound. Truly gone is thrown out and logged: moldy / buggy sacks, bulging cans (more each year past), failed jar seals, oil and flour past two years over, freezer burn and stale snacks past a year over; sealed buckets never. While the stores sell, food long past date (cans and sacks a year, risky kinds six months) is thrown out and bought fresh out of the money (home-canned just goes); after the collapse it's eaten. The family eats in-date food first (fresh, then the freezer, then canned and staples side by side), old food only when it runs out; a month of mostly old food is −2 morale. The pantry shows how far past date each item is and what it's still worth, and "what we're eating" says when some of it is old. (An item's age is the average of what's on hand, so a fresh delivery rejuvenates the lot.) **Eating** (decided: side by side): fresh first, then the freezer, then canned and staples together each day in proportion to the calories of each on hand; inside a category it draws soonest-to-expire first (weight climbs over the last six months), mixed across items, with caps (sugar ≤ 8 % of a day, honey 6 %, oil 15 %). **Diet:** a month's running mix → effective number of foods (inverse Simpson; the grocery store counts as 8, the garden as 3) → the variety term in the morale target (rice and beans only ≈ 1.2 of 4; a varied pantry ≈ 3–4); sweets in the mix +2; fat under 12 % of kcal −2 morale and health capped at 85; protein under 25 g / 1,000 kcal caps health at 80; a week without salt −3 morale, six weeks caps health at 90; coffee +2; running out is −6 easing to 0 over ten days, then −1, only for a family with a habit (coffee bought or in the pantry in the last 90 days, `pantry.coffeeDay`); a family that never stocked it gets −2 for the first week, nothing after. Salt (6 g a head) and coffee (2 cups an adult) are used up by the day; none of it applies while the grocery store feeds the family. **Shop:** itemized cards on four shelves (long-term, bulk, cans, freezer) plus a one-year kit and a three-month pantry sized to the household; order lines carry `food` + `n` (units) with `material` / `qty` as the category equivalent so stock counts keep working. **Shelves:** builders mark food slots (shelf boards, pallets) with a non-drawn marker; the storeroom has 40 boards and two pallets, the root cellar 16 boards and a floor patch, the galley / container / cabin / log-house pantries their boards. Props per item (a bucket each, a case of 24 cans shows six, a case of quarts twelve jars), packed board by board in turn round every store; more food than shelf cuts each item back by the same share so the mix shows. **The house pantry holds what's in use** (decided): about 18 days of eating, an even share of every item plus a bag of salt and a can of coffee; the rest sits in the storeroom, cellar and bunker rooms, which empty first while the house share stays topped up (`splitInUse`). **The chest freezer** is in the root cellar, where the sim keeps its freezer (frozen food holds only while the cellar has power): lid up, butcher-paper packages filled by the frozen stock (three a 5 kg box). Ten instanced meshes for the whole compound. **Left as is (decided):** generic `staples` orders (the playtest bot's) arrive as the bag mix of rice, beans, flour and oil, so the flour and oil now go off within a year or two and that bot's long-run food is a little lower than before. The daily pantry work allocates almost nothing (item lists cached, no per-day sorts); about 2 % of a simulated day. |
| 2026-10-25 | The world round the place (user: "all of these", in reply to: scavenging runs, a trade network, neighbors with lives) | `sim/scavenge.ts`, `sim/market.ts` (+ `sim/tradeGoods.ts`, extends `sim/barter.ts`), `sim/neighborLife.ts`, `sim/pact.ts`; Outside → **Scavenging**, **Crossroads market**, **Neighbors**; save v42. **Scavenging:** from the day the stores close after the hit (after the shelter weeks of a nuclear war), the family sends 1–4 people (16+) on foot (≈ 30 km a day, a 25 kg pack each) or by truck (rescue.tripFuel, a 600 kg bed) to the town's hardware & feed store, pharmacy, gas station and high school, the jackknifed semis on the interstate, the old Pruitt place, and every neighbor's farm once it stands empty. Each place has its own goods and is picked over by others on its own half-life (pharmacy 30 days, gas 45, hardware 75, interstate 90, school 150, farm 200), slowing to a quarter over the years as fewer people are left. They carry the best value per kilo first. Gas is good for 4 months, then goes to varnish (25 % good by two years). Risks: other scavengers (most in months 1–4; the run's orders: keep clear, talk first, shoot first; savage crews shoot first), a savage crew that has claimed a town place (60 % they're met), feral dogs, falls, cuts, rusty nails (6–15 % tetanus 4–14 days later), fallout dose after a nuclear war, the sickness after a plague. Fights are `rescue.fightAtCamp`. Books raise the least-skilled adult's matching skill by 1 (to 6). **Market:** Saturdays at the crossroads from 70 days after the hit, while money doesn't work, if the law is at a quarter or the neighbors have organized. Barter prices plus: salt, coffee, batteries, tobacco, liquor in a trade-goods bag, climbing ×(1 + 3…6 × (1 − e^(−days/120))); fuel and meds dearer as the gas station and pharmacy are stripped; gold at 30 % of its trader value at first, 80 % by year three; silver 70 → 100 %. Stalls ask value × greed (0.9–1.3) × reputation markup (1.1 at −100 … 0.8 at 100) × (1 − 3 % per negotiator level); unwanted goods count 60 %; within 85 % can still go. Honest +1, generous +4 (and the neighbors hear), cheat counts as 130 % but is caught 10–35 % of the time (−15, the vendor bans you) or found out 1–3 weeks later 40 % of the time (−20). At −40 stalls start refusing; at 40 the co-op invites you. The road home: robbed (3–15 %, ×1.5 carrying over $200, ×0.5 armed), or followed (≈ 8–20 %: OPSEC exposure +8 fading 3 %/day, a crew at the gate within a week). **Neighbors:** each head of household has an age (Gompertz death odds, ×1–4 after the collapse), sickness, raids (0.15 %/day lawless, ×0.6 in a pact, ×0.5 in the militia; hold by numbers and guns), fire (chimneys and lamps, more in a grid-down winter; wildfire), leaving, asking to join (an arrival at the gate), refugees settling (up to 3 households), a co-op or militia forming from day 45. News comes by shots heard (3 km), smoke (8 km), the radio net, a visit, gossip, the market; what isn't heard isn't known. **Pact:** households at 30+ trust (prepper 40, loner 50); members within 3 km or on the net come when there's shooting (`pact.defenders`, used by the gate fight), the militia sends 3 men to members; a member under attack calls (go: a `fightAtCamp` with their people alongside; stay home: −25 and likely out of the pact); a work day every 14 days, alternating hosts. Burned neighbor farms are flagged for the world (`neighborLife.burnedFarms`). |

Click a built tower in the Defense tab (Upgrade the towers), pay the new tier's cost (blueprint, materials, hours, county permit), and the new tier rises BESIDE the old one; the old keeps working until the new is finished, then comes down and half its materials come back (`Structure.replaces`, `construction.completeUpgrade`; the walls work the same way, drawn 0.12 to the run's right). Each tier is a better battle post (higher, more cover, longer reach), adds "seen coming" (`postureOf`) and, from tier 4, floodlights (need power; dark without it). The manned mount (tier 5) needs a person on the tower and 60+ rounds of 7.62/5.56 in the armory and fires full-auto bursts; the turret (tier 6, last) needs power and 200+ rounds, fires with nobody on it, and can jam.
