| 2026-10-27 | The raid cutscene (user: "raiders/invaders should have their own cutscene whenever they come. like if it's 2:15AM and invaders are there, the time pauses until you hit 'watch' and all you can do is watch your party as it unfolds, see how they react. real time still elapses, but you're unable to build anything during that duration. architect how long the battles/invasion attempts will last, but it should be realistic. then in the aftermath of the battle, the cutscene ends and you gain full control again and pick up the pieces.") | `sim/siege.ts`, `sim/raidCutscene.ts`, `store/gameStore.ts` (`cutscene`), `ui/CutsceneOverlay.tsx`, `game/battle/CutscenePlayer.ts` + `director.ts`; save v43. **Decide first, then watch:** a raid (looters, gang, militia, patrol, requisition, horde; not traders, neighbors, refugees or a break-in) reaches the gate at an *hour* (`arrivalHour`: gangs in the small hours, requisition teams in daylight; the engine holds the raid in `gate.incoming` and stops on that very quarter-hour). Time pauses on the Watch prompt (letterbox, the clock, one line of what you see and hear); the stance is picked on the gate card, then **Watch**. **Locked while it runs:** every store action that builds, plans, buys, hires, opens the shop or selects a building is refused (`locked()`), the HUD is unmounted; camera orbit and zoom stay. **Duration, a realistic mix:** one scene of 2-8 minutes (lead-in: the approach or standoff, time-lapsed; the fight, 1 playback s = 1 game s; a tail); an organized crew at night (gang 70 %, militia 40 %, patrol / requisition 20 %; day ×0.18) may lay a **siege** of 2-5 scenes (probing the line, harassing fire, fires set, then the assault `planBattle` fought) with 10-90 min of game time skipped between them, at most 7 h in all. Each scene is skippable (Skip scene / Skip all). **Clock:** the stance is resolved on a copy and held back (`stageRaid`), so the visible game stands still; the overlay clock reads off the siege timeline (`locate`); on commit the staged result is stepped by exactly the siege's length (`commitRaid`), once, so nothing is counted twice. Earlier siege scenes are cosmetic fights that cost the rounds fired, never blood (hits become near misses). **Aftermath:** a report card (killed, taken, hurt, stores lost, rounds fired, raiders on the drive), then control returns and the existing aftermath choices (treat and release / hold / leave / finish) apply. **Camera:** a camera operator (`director.ts`) cuts between wall, house, raiders, close-ups on a wound or a speaker, and wide shots. **Follow-up (user's answers):** the earlier siege scenes cost the rounds fired plus **lost sleep and morale** for everyone (`siegeStrain`, scaled by the scenes and hours; `Needs.tired` slows work and wears off 3 hours a day) and never casualties before the assault. **Night break-ins** get the same Watch cutscene as a single 2-4 minute scene (arrival 1:30-3:30 AM; `breakInScene` for shout / call 911, the peacetime battle for confront). **One change of stance between scenes:** a card after each scene (until it is used) offers Carry on or a new stance; `restageRaid` re-resolves from the untouched game with the new stance, keeps the scenes already shown (so the virtual clock does not jump), drops or keeps the cosmetic scenes still to come, and the clock commits once by the new plan's length. Build, shop and the rest stay locked. |

Self-contained so the daily-life roster can later call `careHours` / pass its own `capacity` (TODO in the file); the unmerged daily-life branch is untouched. **Heart**: `dogEffects(s, personId)` is the single morale/stress function (called from `needs.ts`, easy to port to the people-depth mental-health system): a cared-for dog lifts morale and eases stress, a suffering dog weighs; a dog's death, loss or being given away is grief by bond, doubled for little ones, heavier with children grieving, fading over weeks. **Fighting**: dogs still warn (12 detection, 6 deter, two count, up to +4 / +2 trained); the player CHOOSES per dog: on guard at the post (warns and deters, can be hurt or killed in a fight: `dogsInBattle`, savage crews shoot them first) or stays inside (warns, safe). Never an automatic cost; every loss is a death with grief. Dogs are drawn as procedural quadrupeds (`game/people/DogRenderer.ts`, 7 instanced draw calls) that follow their person, sleep by the house, stand the post at the gate and bark. | 2026-10-28 | Tools (user: the family needs hammers, saws, drills, shovels … accounted for; "there's gotta be a way to work with the tools even degraded once SHTF and there's no more stores") | `sim/tools.ts`, `sim/toolTrade.ts`, `ui/ToolsPanel.tsx`, `ui/ToolShop.tsx`, save v47 (renumber at merge). **Hybrid rule:** each kind of work (`toolFor(task)`: a build stage, felling, a wall by kind, plus garden / firewood / plumb / wire / repair for the daily roster) lists the roles it leans on with a share of its time. A *required* role (cut lumber, drive nails, dig, fell, wrench, wire) cannot be done without a tool; a *soft* role (screws, soil work) goes at half pace by hand. Speed is the harmonic mean over the roles, so a power saw only helps the share of the job that is sawing. A hand tool is 1×, power tools 2.4–3.5× (they need power or gas, and fall back to hand tools when it is out), crude tools 0.35–0.55×. A tool kept decently (edge ≥ 60 %, condition ≥ 50 %) works at full speed, so existing build times hold. **Wear:** per hour of use (hand tools ~1,600 h, power ~800 h, crude ~170 h; a dull tool wears faster, a chainsaw burns fuel); edges dull and are put back with a whetstone / file (or a river rock, to 80 %), each honing grinds the metal a little; at 0 the tool is broken but stays in the kit as parts. **Never a dead end:** *mend* (a little hardware, or parts from a broken tool; each repair lowers the tool's best condition by 12 points), *improvise* (stone axe, scrap saw, rock hammer, scrap driver, digging stick, scrap wrench, bent pliers: always possible, better with hardware), and the family makes one itself after two days without a required tool. *Getting more:* the shop's Tools tab while the stores are open, used tools from hardware stores and farm sheds on scavenging runs (new loot `tool`), and used tools for trade goods at the crossroads market. **Starter kit** (seeded, second-hand condition): everyone has a hammer, hand saw, screwdrivers, shovel, axe, wrenches, pliers, hoe and a whetstone; a carpenter's family adds a circular saw, cordless drill, a second hammer (and a nail gun for a master builder); builders and hunters a chainsaw; mechanics a file, bolt cutters and more wrenches; gardeners a fork; a grandparent a shed of old tools. **UI:** Supplies → Tools (coverage per kind of work, every tool's condition with Sharpen / Fix, make-a-crude-one buttons, market trade), Shop → Tools tab, and a "Tools: saw (needed) …" line plus "Needs a saw" on a project. Only the family's own hands use and wear its tools (`addWork(..., own = true)` from the engine; hired crews and neighbors bring theirs). **User answers (applied):** the toolbox is *shared* but crowded (`crowdRate`: more people on a role than tools of that role = each runs at the average of the tools handed round, floor 30 % of the best; the starter kit has a plain tool per household member so a normal family is not slowed); the family sharpens dull blades daily by itself, costing a little work time (`kit.owed`, paid out of the next hours worked; toggle, default on); **cheap vs pro grades** (pro 2.5× the price, half the wear; starter kits are mostly cheap with some pro, scavenged and gate tools are mixed); tools are a **barter lot** (`tool:<kind>` goods in `barter.trade`, a gate trader sometimes brings a used tool) and **something raiders steal** (a burglary or a crew that goes through the place; `stealTools` on its own dice; a finished workshop, metal garage, bunker stores room or a gun safe each cut it, `toolSecurity`); crude tools stay free and instant; hired crews and neighbors bring their own. Not yet wired (after the daily roster lands): plumbing / wiring fits, hardening and repair jobs, the garden (the roster calls `toolFor`). |

| 2026-10-28 | Daily-life balance fix (playtest round 2: standard prepper dead 0% -> 24% after #76) | **Cause:** the harvest chore backlog grew 22 per field per day around the clock but was worked at most 14 h a day, so with 14-16 fields it sat at its cap and every field yielded about 40% of its crop; the Auto planner served the biggest need first, so animals, dogs and cooking starved behind it; and chores took the weekend project hours (build pace -35-50%). **Fix** (`sim/daily.ts`, no schema change): harvest backlog `HARVEST_RATE` 7 per field per harvest day (16 fields need ~10 person-hours, findable by two adults) and a standing crop loses at most 30% to a full backlog (was 60%); the planner staffs livestock, pets and cooking first (priority by consequence, not size); with a project staked out each person keeps their project hours (`BUILD_KEEP`) and chores take the chore slots first, except crops once their backlog passes `CROP_BACKLOG` 50; employed adults get no chore in their weekday job hours (07:00-16:00) but can take the 19:00-21:00 evening for animals, dogs and the stove, so a working family's animals are fed by the people at home or after work. Care mechanics (neglect deaths, cooking gating canning, cold-meal morale) are unchanged. **Noise:** the `needs the vet` line shows once per dog per 14 days and a chore's `falling behind` line once per 21 days (`sim/nag.ts`, lazy `nagged` map). **Crash:** `neighborLife.dies()` no longer calls `cap('')` when the last member of a young family / prepper / ranch / loner household dies. Playtest harness ignores insurance payouts in the money-jump check. |

Wear = 1 - exp(-trips x days / 700) per trail, summed over every walk on it: **no tracks on day 1** (a busy walk shows as a trampled line in about a week and is bare earth in a couple of months; a rare one takes years); what came with the land is already worn. A track to the **bunker hatch never wears past a faint line, is never listed and cannot be laid** (a trail would give it away). **Laying** (Build -> Footpaths, `PathsPanel`): gravel, stepping stones, duckboards, poured concrete (concrete only between the main buildings); one click lays the busiest walks (cost and metres shown; short materials can be ordered), or one walk, or take it up; the cost comes out of the stock at once (no tracked hours: the family lays it in an afternoon; $12/m gravel, $6/m stones, $32/m duckboards, $19/m concrete). A laid walk is "plowed" (cleared of snow) and does not go to mud, a worn track does (the season patch's `SEA_PLOWED` / `SEA_MUD`). **The one saved field**: `GameState.paths.laid` (save v56; worn tracks are not saved). **Pace**: `walkFactor` (0.8-1.12) in the people renderer only: a little quicker on a path (paved 1.10, gravel 1.07, dirt 1.05), mud slows the way off the paths up to 15%, snow 9%; the sim moves nobody, so nothing in the sim changes. They **arrive with the age of the building** (a mat in a couple of days, pots in two months, a flagpole in a year; what came with the land is old on day 1), never sit on a building, in water, in a wall, on a path, in front of a door or on each other, and come in High / Medium / Low tiers (Low keeps the mat, lanterns, barn lights, hay and the mailbox; a lower tier is always a subset of a higher one). Lifestyle becomes full-time; belongings and a pantry come up (movers $1,800 + $350 a head); jobs that can be done from home (developer, accountant, insurance agent) are kept unless you tick "quit them all", the rest are quit (a 90-minute drive each way). The sale adds a line to `startFunds`. **The hit:** on the place, they stay (the house is kept, locked, the mortgage unpaid); in town, a "Caught in town" card: drive straight home / back roads (slower, fewer mishaps) / fill up and shop first (adds a day and a day in the city), with road trouble from the fuel and law tracks and the disaster (breakdown, checkpoint shakedown, hurt in a crush, a vehicle taken, on foot if nothing runs); meanwhile the empty place is looted when the law is gone, the animals go unfed, a paid caretaker stops coming. | 2026-10-29 | Farm & kitchen depth, part 1: fields, seed and soil; the kitchen; cold storage; the Farm tab (user: "seed saving, crop rotation and soil fertility, succession planting; a real kitchen: food quality, recipes from stores, a pantry/root cellar capacity, cold-storage spoilage, dehydrating, fermenting, curing, smoking, rendering lard, bread from flour with a wood oven; readouts: a Farm tab with a seasonal calendar, stores in days, spoilage risk, a Food plan"; the old `claude/farm-kitchen` WIP was read and its ideas ported, not merged) | **Fields** (`sim/crops.ts`, save v61): each field grows one crop a year (potatoes / dry beans / wheat), Auto rotates beans > potatoes > wheat staggered across the fields, or the Farm tab pins a crop or rests the field under cover-crop seed; the same crop twice, blight / bean beetle / rust (seeded per game and year, a rotated field takes half), tired soil all cost crop; a legume year before potatoes or wheat is worth +6 %. **Soil**: fertility per plot (yield ×(0.7 + 0.3·f)); harvest draws it down, recovery +0.075 a year makes a three-year rotation a wash; manure (the animals' pile, or $15 a load) and compost (scraps, weeds) are spread at sowing, 3 loads a field; above 1 it leaches 35 % a year. **Seed**: every field is sown each spring from the store inside the sowing window (14 days before the last frost to 85 days ahead of the first), one unit a field; heirloom (`seed`, true-breeding, adapts +1 %/yr to 1.04), hybrid (`seed_hybrid`, +12 % and a third less trouble the year it is sown, saved seed ×0.8 recovering 4 %/yr) and saved (`seed_saved`, kept back from the harvest: harvest days fill next spring's seed first, up to 40 % of a day's crop); no seed means a fallow field. The field's gross crop is the old net 700,000 kcal plus the seed unit it now sets aside, so a normal year feeds the family as before. New chore `sowing` (Fields duty; behind it the whole sowing is late, up to 40 % off). **Gardens**: successions 1–3 (+7 % crop, +8 days of season, +20 % weeding each). **Kitchen** (`sim/kitchen.ts`): jars need lids and a pressure canner (two cases a day) for anything but the high-acid third of a glut (a water bath, one case; vegetables and meat in a water bath are how botulism happens, so the game does not offer it); a dehydrator on house power (3 baskets) or sun screens on dry days (2); crocks and salt ferment 2 baskets a day into `ferments` (no lids, canner or power). `putUpMeat` (one function for hunting, butchering and the pond): powered freezer > smoker (80 %) > salt-cure in the cold months or a cool cellar (85 %, pork 90 %: `cured_meat`) > pressure canner > the table; pigs render 15 % of their calories as `lard`. The table: 12 dishes the pantry can make, the best three set the quality (0–1; salt, herbs and bread add), morale term (q − 0.4) × 6 clipped −1.8…+2.4, about 0 for beans and rice. A wood-fired oven (`wood_oven`, Build → Food) makes bread (with flour or a grain mill) and roasts and burns a little wood on bake days. **Storage** (`sim/storage.ts`): a root cellar's bins hold 2.5 million kcal of potatoes and apples (`cellar_roots`, in the pantry), shrinking by the cellar's temperature (0.04 %/day at 3 °C to 0.25 % at 15 °C; powered cellar 3 °C cooler); the harvest that does not fit sits in the kitchen and rots; fresh food in a cellar spoils 0.9–2.2 %/day against 8 % in the kitchen (old flat 1.5 %). Pantry shelf room by building; past it, cool-kept goods lose up to a quarter of their life. **Farm tab** (right panel, `ui/FarmTab.tsx`): Season (this season's work and the 12-month calendar from the region's frost dates), Fields (crop, soil, seed, soil amendments, garden successions), Kitchen (methods on/off with the reason one is out, storage bars, the table and every dish with what it lacks), Animals (livestock and the greenhouse / orchard / pond / yard that were in Supplies), Food plan (stores in days by where kept, a year's food against a year's need, spoilage risk, what to do). Garden and field work now wear the tools and run at tool speed (`toolFor('garden')`). Existing saves: every field counts as sown, one season's saved seed per field, a canner for families that had lids. Bot: the playtest bot buys a canner and crocks with its lids. |

(5) **Guidance** (`sim/weekendPlan.ts`, pure, tested): `weekendBudget` (a month in numbers), `nextStageOrder` (one stage at a time, two in the free first week and before a long stay: a $30k account cannot buy the whole cabin, and the house waiting on its first stage outranks the belt card), `nextLongStay` (Memorial Day, spring break, the July weeks, Labor Day, Thanksgiving, Christmas: have the next stage on site before it), `crewIdea` (a crew of four builds the weekdays while the family is at the jobs; offered when a stage is supplied and a month of crew is in the account). Save v66 (step 65 to 66 sets `belt: false` and touches nothing else). **Bot** (`scripts/playtest.test.ts`, weekend only): buys land with the weekend budget and a lot that leaves a third of it, orders a stage at a time, waits for the roof before the guns, hires a crew with $55k+ in the account, takes the belt card; `TOWN=rent`, `BELT=0` switches. **Numbers** (Standard, LIFESTYLE=weekend, 9 disasters x 2 seed offsets = 18 runs a cell, offsets 0 and 100; dead = share of people; built = buildings finished at the hit): before (main) prepper 48 % / 2.2 built, fortress 57 % / 2.4, homestead 61 % / 4.3; bot fixes only: 42 % / 5.7, 52 % / 4.8, 61 % / 4.3; game with the $330k floor: 41 % / 14.4, 50 % / 11.4, 33 % / 14.3; **final ($400k floor): prepper 28 % / 17.4 built, fortress 55 % / 12.4, homestead 27 % / 16.2** (wipes 4, 8, 2 of 18). Full-time Standard prepper + fortress offset 0 (18 runs) is byte-identical before and after. Full-time built at the hit is 20.7 / 15.8, so the weekender now gets 84 % / 78 % of it (target 60 %). Fortress stays high (55 %): its deaths are fights and people taken, and full-time fortress is 32-50 % with the same bot, so that is the bot's fortress, not the pacing. 18-run cells carry about +-10 points of noise. Not built: rent rising each year, a HELOC, the family's own return-trip routing, remote-work pay failing when the phones go. |

Every non-square outline gets a flat chord ≥ gate + 0.4 where the driveway (else the farm gate, else the east side) crosses it, so the gatehouse garage always has its straight stretch. `planPropertyWall` takes a `Region` (loops + contains) instead of rectangles; `towerSites` sites corners and every ~6 u of wall on a polygon with the whole tower footprint inside; the walkway, passage, garage, yard-ring and enclosure checks are unchanged (a shaped ring is a closed run of > 3 points, which the yard check already reads). Fortify my ring has a Shape row (the whole-property scope keeps the land's own line and says so); Wall my property has an Around row (Property line, or a compound of any shape round the family's buildings, so you can wall a round compound in chain-link). `PropertyGeometry.ground` feeds the irregular ring. No save change: a ring is the points it was laid with. |

| 2026-10-29 | Terrain has mechanics (inspiration gap 1, user: "terrain cannot be cosmetic") | **The lot's relief is analysed once and read by existing systems; nothing new scales the raids.** `sim/terrain.ts` (pure, tested; 1 cell = 1 unit ≈ 6 m) turns a ground function into per-cell slope, aspect, high ground (rise over an 8-point ring at 4 cells: full advantage at 0.7 u ≈ 4 m), sight (16 rays, a crest hides what is behind it), foot-approach cost (level 1, scramble up to 8, cliff 30 = ladders, creek ford 7), flow accumulation (floodplain = < 0.5 u above the creek's water within 14 u; wet flats and draws), frost pockets (0.3 u below the ground 8 cells round, near level), wind exposure and sun (1 ± 0.8 × slope, cap ±25 %). Lanes: cheapest walk (Dijkstra, 8-connected) from each of 8 compass sectors to the camp, with the choke (≤ 3 cells = 18 m of cheap going across the path). `siteCandidates` suggests a terraced-keep ridge, a tower hill, a gorge (creek cross-section whose rims stand ≥ 6 m over the bed within 200 m rim to rim: found on Mountain West, North Woods, Pacific NW, Upper Midwest, New England, Hill Country and Rust Belt parcels, never on Plains, Desert, Ozarks or Appalachian ones), a reservoir bench (≥ 7 m over the camp), micro-hydro (creek fall ≥ 1.2 m over 60 m), frost pockets, the choke. `sim/terrainState.ts` rebuilds the same ground headless from `ParcelTerrain` (seed + region + creek + timber), cached. **Effects, all in `sim/terrainFx.ts` (`FX`), each tested:** (1) solar: array output × (1 ± 0.8 × slope facing south/north, cap ±25 %); (2) floods (flood, hurricane remnants, flash flood): candidates are the creek's bottom (any structure in the floodplain, and cellars / gardens / fields / outhouses / hand wells < 6 m above the water); a bench 6 m or more up is spared; no creek through the place keeps the old rule; (3) spring mud (days 60–150, storms): building on the floodplain goes 15 % slower; (4) gravity feed: a reservoir ≥ 7 m above the houses cuts the well pump's draw 60 %; (5) raids: a crew comes by the cheapest lane; the road side counts ×0.75 and a lane must be 15 % cheaper than the road's to draw them round (`planBattle`, `BattleOptions.terrain=false` keeps the old road side); (6) `terrainDefense(s)` = 0.5 × lane hardness + 0.3 × tower high-ground share + 0.2 × choke held (a finished tower or wall within 4 u): one input to the shared strength: `adaptOf(game, posture, terrain)` takes it as defense-equivalent points (score x 15, `terrainPoints`; the scale runs 40 = nothing to 100 = full), so crews adapt to a hard lane, a held choke and high-ground towers exactly as to walls; also shown in the Defense tab (no second scaling system). Frost pockets and wind are analysed and shown on the overlay and in cards, not yet wired to crops. **UI:** a Terrain overlay (Off / High ground / Lanes / Water / Sun) beside Sightlines with a plain-language legend; Next-step cards (at most one, only with a finished home and a reason to look: "This ridge suits a terraced keep", "The gorge is a natural choke point", "The way in narrows", "A bench above the camp for a reservoir") with a Show me on the map button; the player confirms by building, nothing appears unasked. Costs: the analysis is ~0.5 s once per place, then cached; the daily hooks are one height probe (~50 µs) per solar array / flooded structure. |
| 2026-10-29 | Varied lots and elevations, part 1: the ground (user: "varied lots and elevations, especially in the mountains, is basically the point"; picks: ridge-top knoll + hillside slope, valley bench, canyon / gorge / creek bed, mesa, saddle, lakeshore bluff, island / peninsula by causeway; "strong but buildable with extreme outliers": typical mountain lots drop 30-80 m across the parcel, special listings 150-300 m; other regions' hills stay gentle) | `sim/lots.ts` (pure, tested), `game/parcel/parcelTerrain.ts`, `sim/lotListing.ts`, save v68 (provisional number, renumber at merge). **Eight archetypes**, each a seeded height function over the home rect (relief in world units, 1 u = 6 m, above the regional datum): *ridge* (a crest with a small flat top and outcrops), *hillside* (benches and shallow gullies, falls toward the road or away from it; a creek down the fall line sometimes), *bench* (cut bank, one broad flat bench, the hill behind, river below), *gorge* (creek bed 1-2 u wide, cliff on one side, ledged slope on the other, rims both sides; 30-80 m typical, 150-300 m for the outliers), *mesa* (a flat tongue with cliffs on three sides that ramps down to the road at about 17 %; the outlier is a butte with no ramp), *saddle* (a pass between two peaks, a spring), *bluff* (flat upland over a steep face and a lake), *island* (a knoll in a lake, a causeway to the road bank; a peninsula on 5 acres). **Mechanics, not scenery:** the creek bed is a running minimum eastward, so water never runs uphill, and it always lies below the ground beside it; the lake level is a plane (`LAKE_Y`) and a cell under it is water for placement (`ParcelTerrain.isCreek`); the county road at x = 70 never floods. `lotStats` reads the lot the way the sim and the listing need it: drop, mean / 90th / steepest slope, **pads** (connected cells at most 12 % slope, 450 m2 or more; the buildable fraction is their share of the dry land), cliff share (over 70 %), water share, aspect and sun of the best pad, **the road in** (straight from the county road: length, steepest 60 m stretch, average, climb), how far the pad stands above the land 100 m round it, how many of 8 approaches on foot are open (no cliff, no water, nothing over 50 %), the highest flat bench within 180 m (a gravity-fed reservoir), the creek's fall and its floodplain. **Render:** `ParcelTerrain` takes `lot` and `home`; the lot's relief replaces the regional bench / hollow shapes on the place (the regional field outside is unchanged), the valley carve is replaced by a channel cut, timber is by altitude (tree line 3,050 m Mountain West, 1,750 PNW, 1,950 Appalachia), bare on cliffs, scree and outcrops, glades on the flats, thinner on south faces; rocks cluster on cliffs and outcrops; a lake lot draws its own water plane through the existing regional-water renderer and a lot with no creek draws no stream. The camp goes on the lot's best pad (the island, the bench), not the middle of the rect. One terrain mesh as before, so the draw calls and the quality tiers (cells 0.35 / 0.5 / 0.8 u) do not change; the cost is in the one-time height sampling. `?lot=gorge&relief=9&acres=40&region=mountain_west&lotseed=3` starts a place on any archetype for QA (`scripts/lotShots.mjs`). Old saves get `site.lot = null` (the classic ground). |

A search for water gets a kind that can have it (creek: bench, gorge, a hillside with a creek, bluff, island; spring: saddle, ridge, hillside; dry: ridge, mesa, hillside, saddle). **Relief** 4.2-9.7 u (25-58 m) times the region (Appalachia 0.8, PNW 0.85) times the lot (a 5-acre lot has less room to fall); 4-10 % of gorge, mesa and bluff listings on 40 or 80 acres are outliers of 26-50 u (150-300 m). **Altitude** from the region's range (Mountain West 1,350-2,700 m...), higher for ridges, mesas and saddles. The rolls come from their own random stream, so the rest of a listing is what it always was. **Price** (`lotPriceFactor`, times the region's per-acre price): flat pads 0.55 (none) to 1.10 (40 % or more flat); the road in 1.06 (to 6 %) ... 0.6 (over 25 %); kind: ridge 1.05, hillside 0.95, bench 1.10, gorge 0.85, mesa 1.00, saddle 0.90, bluff 1.10, island 1.30 (on top of the creek's 1.35, which a lake also counts as); outlier 0.70, dry heights (a ridge, mesa or saddle with no water) 0.90 and the Water row says "Dry heights: the well is N m down". The well is shallower on benches, gorges and islands and deeper on ridges and mesas (`GW_FACTOR`, 0.3-2.2 times). The drive is as long as the straight run from the road to the best flat. **The card** shows a small oblique view (canvas 2D, no WebGL: the card is a button in a list), a cross-section through the camp pad (across the valley for benches and gorges, along the fall for the rest, the lake as water, the pad as a flag, vertical exaggeration printed when over 1.25), and nine rows: Ground (kind, elevation in m and ft), Relief, Flat ground, Road in, Sun, Water head, Creek (micro-hydro when it falls 3 m or more), High ground, Approaches, Floods. The terrain analysis for the in-play overlay (high ground, approach lanes, drainage, sun, wind, water head) is the perimeter work's `sim/terrain.ts`; the listing reads the same quantities from the lot's own ground function until that lands, and should switch to it then. |
| 2026-10-29 | Varied lots and elevations, part 3: slope and altitude in the sim (user picks, all four: slope-driven costs, altitude climate, winter access and snow, buildable-area limits; "every effect small") | `sim/slope.ts`, `sim/climate.ts` (pure, tested). **Road and line:** the steepest 60 m of the road in: to 8 % a plain gravel drive; 8-12 % cut and fill and culverts (+25 % a foot, +10 % long); over 12 % it switchbacks (length times grade / 9 %, up to 3.6; a foot costs 1.5 times to 18 %, 2 to 30 %, 2.6 above; an outlier 1.2 and 1.3 times more; an outlier butte has no ramp: a cut zigzag, 3.6 times the length and 3.4 times a foot, priced at half, not drawn); the power line costs 1 to 2.6 times by mean slope and cliff share (`driveQuote`, `powerQuote` take the site). **Buildings on a slope:** a footprint is graded by the rise across it (`Structure.grade`, stamped at staking); under 6 % nothing; 6-12 % the pad takes 60 % longer and the foundation 2 pallets of fieldstone more; 12-20 % +120 % and 8 pallets; 20-30 % +180 % and 18; over 30 % +240 % and 32 (a retaining wall behind the pad; the foundation 25 % longer per band). The existing per-kind `maxSlope` still refuses the steepest ground; flat pads are the scarce resource because every band costs. The plans' needs, the shop's "order what the plans need" and the guidance read the graded blueprint. **Altitude climate** (`regionFor(site)`, used wherever the sim read `REGIONS[site.region]`; a place without a lot gets the region itself): 6.5 C per km colder (1.17 F per 100 m on the July high and January low); the frost-free season 6 days shorter per 100 m (3 off each end); heating 6 % more per 100 m; snowfall +4 % and rain +3 % per 100 m (the region's blizzard odds scale with snow, capped); reference altitudes Mountain West 1,600 m, Appalachia 450, PNW 200, North Woods 350. **Aspect and pockets:** a pad 4 m or more below the land round it is a cold-air pocket (10 fewer frost-free days, 4 F colder winter lows); 6 m or more above it a thermal belt (4 more days, heating +5 % for the wind); a south face (sun 1.08 or more) 4 more days and 6 % less wood, a north face the reverse; a lake adds 6 days. Never fewer than 40 frost-free days. **Winter access:** the road's difficulty = (steepest stretch - 6) / 8 + (altitude - 1,900 m) / 900 (+0.2 in a gorge); times (1.5 - the best running road vehicle's off-road rating, pickup 0.6 ... motorhome 0.85); at 1 or more it closes on heavy-snow days (a day of the cold season, Nov-Mar, starts one with chance snowfall / 12 cm / 150) for 1 + 1.5 times that many days up to 6; a plow blade (the existing 3,500 dollar item) halves it and removes it below 1.8. A closed road holds deliveries a day each day, holds the weekend family at the foot of the hill, and makes the deputy 2.5 times as slow (1.5 with the plow); a road steeper than 10 % slows response by 25 %, 16 % by 50 %. Nothing is stored: closure is a pure function of the seed and the day. **Guidance cards:** "This slope needs a retaining wall" (a staked building on a 12 % or steeper pad before its foundation; one-click order of the fieldstone) and "The road closes in snow: buy a plow" (a hard road, Aug-Dec, no blade). On Low a lot plants 35 % of its trees, Medium 85 %, High all. The Land tab shows the ground, the frost-free days against the region's, the notes and how often the road closes. |

wood treads on stringers, rails on both edges, an amber strip under every tread nose, caged lamps at each turn and the foot, a fire extinguisher. **The house** has the matching opening: `BuildOpts.hatches` cuts the floor deck and the foundation boxes under it (`shell.minusHoles`) and furniture that would stand in it is dropped (`interior.piece` avoid rects); Walk mode: step into the opening to go down (the lid swings up), step off the last flight to come up beside it. **Construction stages** on every bunker dig (`HomesteadWorld._digCrew`, `props/digSite.ts`): the pit, spoil heap and excavator that were there, plus a tipper truck at the edge while the earth goes out (loaded) and while the backfill comes back (bed tipping), plywood formwork with walers, braces and two layers of rebar mats standing in the pit through the wall pour (stripped as it finishes), two rusted corrugated culvert lengths on cribbing beside the trench while a tunnel is built, a fresh earth mound over the roof slab through the second half of the roof stage and the whole fit-out, then the finished entrance. Draw calls: the site is a handful of merged meshes per stage and exists only while the dig is open; the floor hatch is one mesh per layer and material plus four lid meshes. |
| 2026-10-29 | Hillside cutbank lot and walking on slopes (user: "an example of a lot that allows building into the side of a mountain", then "make sure the game handles it correctly, especially people pathfinding"; no save change) | **Lot:** an optional `LotSpec.cutbank` on a dry hillside (`sim/lots.ts`): a rock face (15-25 m, over 100 % slope, ragged crest, bare rock) across the slope at about 0.1 of the lot uphill of the middle, with a level shelf (about 80 m deep) cut at its foot; stats see it as cliff share ~5 % and a bigger best pad, so slope costs, approach lanes (cliff cost 30), high ground and the Terrain overlay read it with no new code. One dry hillside listing in eight rolls it, as a special listing (a hash of the lot seed, so the other rolls do not move) with the card line "Rock face above the pad"; `?lot=hillside&lotcut&region=pnw&lotseed=8&relief=9&elev=600` starts the example. Saves are unchanged: the flag lives in the saved lot spec and an old spec without it is the old ground. **Walking:** people used to walk straight at their goal unless a worn trail was within 1.3x the straight length, so they crossed walls, buildings, cliff bands and risers. Now `game/paths/walkRoute.ts` (pure, tested): trails if not much longer; if the straight line crosses something hard (building, built wall, creek, ground steeper than 1.0) trails up to 3.2x; else a local search round it (`findRoute`, margin 12). Cliff ground (> 1.0) is blocked for the trail router too, and a walk whose way round is far off its own line is searched again with a 24-unit margin (a garden outside the wall's gate used to get no trail at all). Walking pace follows the grade (`slopePace`: climbing is slow, going down a little slower than flat). **Stairs:** a trail stretch steeper than grade 0.32 for 3.6 m or more gets a dry-stack stone stair (`stairRuns`, `stairGeometry`, one tread per 27 cm of rise, drawn by PathRenderer); crossing a riser stays a cost (router slope cost + `slopePace`). **Dogs** use the same `walkRoute` (DogCtx.via). Not done: other animals, raiders and vehicles (raids still use the lane angle only). `scripts/hillsideWalk.mjs` is the bot that checks it. |

The coarse grid resolves features of about 4 units (24 m) and up, so a choke is now cheap going 8 units (48 m) wide or less (was 3 units on the fine grid). |
| 2026-10-29 | Roads follow the land (user: "as far as roads, it doesn't always have to be a straight road in/out. curved roads, natural mountain tunnels, passes. winding roads if the terrain calls for it, bridges, etc. vary it like the real world. study real world us roads/topography for each region"). Stacked on varied lots (#103-#105), terrain (#101); the tunnel-bridge (#107) is merged and its files are untouched. **The road itself is not saved** (it is derived from the seed, the lot and the ground); the one new field is the bridge's state, save v69 | **One planner, one plan, read by the price, the closure, the raid, the delivery and the drawing.** `sim/roadPlan.ts` (pure, deterministic by seed, tested): A* over a 1-unit (6 m) grid of the owned land whose state is the cell and the heading, so a turn is bounded (a turn of up to 29 degrees a move in the plain country, 40 in the hill country: hairpins of about 10-25 m radius) and the road holds the grade by running along the contour and doubling back. Costs per unit of length: the *ground's* slope along the move against the style's design / maximum grade (the road is cut into the slope, the profile is held to the grade afterwards, the earthwork is priced), cross-slope (cut and fill, 0 under 12 %, forbidden past 85 %), rock, timber, a seeded meander where the ground does not care (a winding country winds), and the creek, which is only crossed square, at the price of the structure the region builds there. Phases: 1 the habitual entrance first, then the whole frontage (ground slope along a move to 30 %, cross-slope to 85 %); 2 a short straight bore under a spur (portals cut into a slope, 8 m or more of rock over the arch) only if nothing else reaches the yard and the country holds one (Mountain West, Appalachia, Pacific Northwest, New England; never Plains, Desert, Ozarks, Hill Country, Rust Belt, Upper Midwest, North Woods); 3 the engineer's road (45 %, cross-slope 130 %); 4 anything, and if nothing at all reaches the yard a zigzag blasted across the face, priced as what it is. A bore is therefore rare (only the extreme saddle and gorge listings): it is the answer to a spur, never a shortcut. The grid path is smoothed (Chaikin), resampled every 0.5 unit, given a vertical profile (moving average, the grade limit forward and back, a landing at 5 % on a hairpin, a level deck over a bridge, the slab level at a ford, the bank level over a culvert), superelevation (the outside of a curve up to 5 %), and read for structures, walls, rails, earthwork and the price. **Region character** (`sim/roadStyle.ts`, design / maximum / hard grade, % : Mountain West 8 / 12 / 17 switchbacks, steel guardrail, cattle guard; Appalachia 9 / 14 / 20 hollow road that keeps to the creek, low-water crossings, log guardrail; Desert 6 / 10 / 15 dips across washes, cattle guard; North Woods and Upper Midwest 6 / 10 / 15 gentle curves, culverts; Plains 4 / 6 / 10 the survey grid, right-angle jogs only, a culvert or bridge on the draw, cattle guard; PNW 8 / 12 / 18 logging spur, culverts to 4.5 m, arch culvert, log rail; New England 8 / 12 / 17 a stone-walled lane that curves with the old boundaries; Ozarks 9 / 14 / 20 low-water crossings; Hill Country 7 / 12 / 17 low-water crossings, cattle guard; Rust Belt 8 / 12 / 17 township spur, steel rail). Numbers from AASHTO low-volume roads (8-10 % comfortable on gravel, 12-14 % the limit, landings 4-5 %), the IFC / NFPA 1 fire-access rule (12 %, 15 % with approval; inside radius 12 m for a pickup and trailer here), USFS logging-road practice (8-10 % sustained, single lane), the county habits (cattle guards on open range, low-water slabs with vent pipes in the Ozarks, Hill Country, Appalachia and the desert, 18-48 in culverts, one-lane steel and timber bridges 12-14 ft wide). **Crossings by channel width** (the creek is 8.4 m): up to the region's culvert limit (2.5-4.5 m) a culvert (an arch culvert over 3.2 m); in the low-water regions a ford under 5 m and a low-water slab to 12-14 m; else a one-lane bridge to 12 m and a bridge beyond. Cross-drain culverts at each sag the road fills across and at least every 150 m on a grade over 6 %. **One price table** (`ROAD_PRICE`, `improvements.ts` re-exports the two old constants): gravel $20 a foot, culvert $1,200, arch culvert $9,000, ford $1,500, low-water slab $3,000 + $300 a foot of channel, one-lane bridge $8,000 + $2,200 a foot, bridge $15,000 + $4,200 a foot, rock bore $60,000 for the portals + $4,000 a foot, cattle guard $2,500, dry-stack wall $55 a foot per metre of height, guardrail $28 a foot (log rail 0.6), earthwork $11 a cubic metre, +$0.9 a foot per percentage point over the design grade, engineer $6,000 (and the lot ×1.25). A bore is dearest, so a bore comes only where nothing else reaches: it is the answer to a spur, never a shortcut. **The road bed** (`sim/roadBed.ts`, shared by the price and the world): a flat bench 0.45 unit each side of the centre line at the road's height, banked; the uphill side a cut at 1.2 : 1, the downhill a fill at 1.8 : 1, to where they meet the ground; a bank over 2.3 m gets a stone facing (a wall priced by its height, capped at 3 m), a drop over 2.4 m a guardrail where the region builds one. **What it feeds** (`sim/roadFx.ts`): the driveway quote and its days are the plan's (`driveQuote(imp, site, seed)`; a lot listing reads it on the lot's own ground; the card's Road in / Road cost rows and the Land tab's way-in line are the plan's words); the winter closure difficulty is the profile grade past 6 % plus 0.12 a hairpin (cap 0.6) plus 0.25 a km plus 0.1 for a bridge or ford (a switchbacked road holds a lower grade, so it closes less than the old straight-line grade did, and a plow blade often keeps it open); the deputy, ambulance and fire take 35 % longer a km past 300 m (cap +50 %), 3 % a hairpin, 15 % over 10 % grade, 25 % more over 16 %, 5 % a single-file structure; heavy loads (concrete, tanks, containers, an excavator, a drill) are shuttled across a one-lane bridge posted at 10 tons (2 more days); a low-water crossing or ford is under the creek for the days of a flood, a flash flood or a hurricane's remnants (up to 3 days) and holds the truck; in a raid a one-lane bridge, a bore, a ford and a chain of two hairpins are single file, so a vehicle crew bunches and dismounts further out (3 units a choke, 1.5 a hairpin, cap 12), a tower or wall within 4 units of the choke makes it a held choke and adds at most 25 % to the terrain's share of the shared defensive strength, and a slow road is less of an easy way in for a crew (the road lane's 0.75 eases toward 1 by 0.1 for each time the drive is longer than a straight level road's). **Scope:** the plan prices and draws on every place; the raid, delivery, winter and response effects apply to places on a lot archetype (the hill listings, like #105) because the plan costs a search over the land and every battle and every test seed would pay it; a flood still takes a low-water crossing anywhere. **The county road** (`game/region/countyRoad.ts`) is the region's: ruler-straight with a right-angle jog on either side of the place on the Plains, short bends in Appalachia, New England, the Pacific Northwest and the Ozarks, long easy curves in the Mountain West, near straight in the desert. **The world** (`game/region/roadWorks.ts`, about 8 draw calls however long the road): a ribbon on the benched ground (`ParcelTerrain.setRoadBed`, the terrain mesh is redrawn along it when the drive goes in, and the banks are replanted), stone-faced banks and parapets, guardrail posts and beams, culverts with pipe ends and headwalls, a ford or slab with vent pipes and depth posts, a one-lane plank-and-steel bridge on stone abutments with rails (people and the truck ride its deck), a rough stone portal and a dark bore at each end of a tunnel, a cattle guard, a cairn on a pass, a curve-warning sign and white delineators at each hairpin, a dry-stack lane wall in New England. The unbuilt drive is a two-track along the surveyed line. **The bridge on the way in can be dropped, like the tunnel-bridge** (save v69, `roadBridge { down, due }`, migrated to standing; Defense tab, The way in): Drop it is free and instant; while it is down nothing with wheels crosses, so deliveries stop at the creek and are carried in by hand (the existing roadside carry), the bridge is no choke any more and the road's share of the defense counts the break as a held choke (0.6 of its 25 %), your own trucks stop there too, and the world draws its abutments only. Rebuild pays the structure's price from the table and takes its days (18 for a one-lane bridge, 30 for a bridge). **Not built:** dropping a bore, the family's cars stranded at the creek (the weekend commute does not read a dropped bridge), switchbacks that stack on each other in the 3D view, snow plowing of the drive, wildlife crossings, the player choosing the gate. |

| 2026-10-29 | Orchard plots: apple, pear, cherry and plum rows with real years, blossom, frost pockets and bees (atmosphere batch B; no user question: it extends the 2026-10-24 greenhouse-and-orchard row) | **Same two plots (`orchard` 12 trees, `orchard_grove` 48), now a row of each fruit** (a column of the grid is one kind; the peach is gone, the cherry and plum are in). `sim/orchard.ts` (pure; 24 tests) replaces the orchard half of `sim/greenhouse.ts`; **no state added, no save bump** (a plot's fruit is what it is planted with, the frost date is seeded from the game and year, the pocket is read from the lot's ground). **Years:** a semi-dwarf nursery tree bears nothing for years, then a fifth to a sixth more each year to the full crop: plum 3 / 7, apple 4 / 8, sweet cherry 4 / 8, pear 5 / 10 (first-fruit year / full-crop year). **Blossom and frost:** each kind blooms against the place's median last frost (cherry 8 days before it, plum 6, pear 3, apple a day after; 8-10 days long; `regionFor`, so the lot's altitude and cold pocket already move it); the last hard (28 F) frost falls about 11 days before the median, seeded +-9 days per game and year; it takes nine tenths of the blossom that is open, so in an ordinary year it costs the cherry about a fifth of the crop, the plum 17 %, the pear 10 % and the apple 4 % (the old model took everything one year in six, whatever the tree). **Frost pockets (#101 terrain):** a plot lower than the land 8 units (50 m) round it on near-level ground is a cold-air pocket (the Terrain overlay's white cells): the hard frost reaches it 9 days later in the season, which multiplies every kind's loss (apple 4 -> 20 %, pear 10 -> 35 %, plum 17 -> 49 %, cherry 21 -> 55 %); a plot on a slope of 8 % or more drains the cold air away (4 days earlier: apple 1 %, cherry 12 %). Staking an orchard in a pocket says so at once (while it can still be moved). **Bees:** most kinds need an insect on the blossom: the apiary's `pollination().orchard` bonus (up to +18 %, unchanged in bees.ts: no hives = the usual crop, wild bees) is scaled by each kind's dependence (cherry 0.95 ... plum 0.80, over the apple's 0.85), so hives pay most on cherries. **Fruit seasons:** ripening is a fixed time after bloom (cherry 62 days, plum 118, pear 128, apple 140) and is picked over 3 to 9 weeks: cherries in early summer, plums, pears, then apples into the fall (`calendarOfFruit`; the farm calendar has the bloom, the watering and each fruit's picking). Apples keep in the cellar bins (85 %), pears 60 %, plums 5 %, cherries none: they are eaten fresh and the kitchen jars the rest (the existing aggregate `fresh` + `shelveRoots` path; `produceParts.orchardKeep` is the keeping share, no longer a flat 0.6). **Yield:** pounds a tree at full bearing: apple 150, pear 140, cherry 60, plum 100, at the same 500 kcal a lb the orchard always used (so a mature plot is 675,000 kcal before frost, pests, pruning and bees; it was 900,000), times pruning (-20 % unpruned, as before), the year's pest (codling moth hits apple, pear and plum; fire blight apple and pear; not cherry), the care chore and the climate. **Water and the duty:** `orchardWaterL` adds 7 L a day per tree under 3 years, 4 per bearing tree (half in a wet region), June to mid-September, to the utilities day (the pond first, like the garden); the orchard chore on the Garden duty now builds backlog for the watering (more for young trees) as well as the winter pruning (judged 14 days before the median last frost instead of a fixed day 95) and the picking weeks. **Art:** each kind is a different tree (round apple, upright pear, tall open cherry, low spreading plum), with its own blossom (4 new kit materials: pink-white apple, white pear and plum, pinker-white cherry: 44 clusters a tree on the canopy) and fruit (cherry, plum added), shown by the world only in that kind's days (`fruitLook`: leaf before bloom, blossom browned by a killing frost, ripe fruit around the picking weeks); the plot is 6 meshes bigger (20-mesh budget, no new instancing). **Terraces and beds:** the orchard and the raised beds use the same placement and pad rules as every kit piece (`maxSlope` 0.35 / 0.45, graded pads with the retaining-wall cost, `sim/slope.ts` untouched): the shots put both on the hillside lot's terraces under the shelf. **Not done:** trees do not grow visibly (a young plot looks like a mature one), no biennial bearing, no rain-splitting of cherries, no netting against birds, no thinning, no separate pollinizer variety rule (every plot has all four kinds, so they always cross). |
| 2026-10-29 | Misty mountains: valley fog banks, low cloud and overcast light (inspiration north star, image 28/29; atmosphere batch A) | **Fog is a pure function of the game seed, the day, the hour and the place, and the number the mechanics use is the number the engine draws.** `sim/fog.ts` (pure, 19 tests) + `sim/fogFx.ts` (game adapters, 8 tests); nothing is saved (no state, no migration). **Day:** `fogDay(seed, doy, region)` rolls a valley-fog morning, an overcast day and drizzle by region and season in spells of 2-4 days (fall mornings in the Northwest and Appalachia: fog on 45-50 % of fall days; the Mountain West fogs in winter inversions only, and an inversion does not burn off; the desert hardly ever; a storm day is overcast and drizzling). **Hour:** `bankCurve`: the bank forms after midnight, is deepest 05-08, burns off from the bottom up by 9-16 h (later under cloud, never in an inversion) and re-forms from 18:30; the game's day turns at 06:00, so the small hours belong to the dawn that is coming. **Place:** `fogPlaceOf` reads the camp's height over the valley floor (the lot's low ground or the lowland and water round it) and under the ridges from the lot (`siteLot` stats + the same ground the world draws); a bank is a share (0.2-1.0, up to 1.5 in an inversion) of 110 m in mountain country (40 m of ground fog elsewhere), so a camp up the slope is above most banks ("fog down to 38 m below camp") and one in the hollow is in them ("camp in fog, 12 m under its top"): where you build now matters. **Sight:** Koschmieder visibility (dense 40 m, half density ~350 m, thin 1.6 km), per sensor: the eye 1.0, a visible-light camera 0.9, a night-vision tube 0.85, **thermal x8 + 200 m (mostly ignores it)**; `fogSightFactor` = sight / 400 m, clamped 0.1-1. It scales (a) what a camera, the floodlights and the NVG tube add at the gate (`gear.ts gearPosture`; thermal camera and handheld keep theirs; the alarm, trip wire, dogs and radios do not care), (b) the watch's own score, the towers' view and the posted guard (`encounters.ts postureOf`), (c) how far out the towers and the watch see a crew coming in a fight (`battle.ts detectAt`): dense fog roughly takes a third off the watch and leaves the crew that much more unseen time. Small, and thermal gear is the answer to it. **Solar:** `utilities.balanceDay` multiplies the sun by `solarFogK`: overcast keeps about a third (thin cloud more; floor 0.18), panels standing in the morning bank lose the hours to burn-off (panels above it only lose to cloud); the day's average falls a few percent in the Northwest, about 1 % in the Mountain West, nothing in the desert (one balance test now picks a clear day). **Words:** the daily log says what this morning's fog is and forecasts the next dawn (once a spell: `logGate`), the weather loop on the radio gives the same forecast after the collapse (own random stream: the rest of the day's news is unchanged), and the Terrain overlay / Sightlines panel shows one line: "Fog down to N m below camp, sight range N m (thermal N m)". **Look:** `engine/world/fog/heightFog.ts` patches three's fog chunks once: every standard material (and the regional terrain, far trees and farm props, which have their own fog code) is fogged by the HEIGHT of what it shows: a bank of density D under a top height Y (closed-form optical depth along the eye ray, so from above it is a white sea that follows the contours with the hilltops standing out, from inside a wall) and a low-cloud deck above a base height (ridges that rise into it dissolve), with two octaves of drifting noise; three shared uniforms, no geometry, no depth texture. The existing fog-sheet draw call rides the bank top for wisps. `ValleyFog` eases the sim's numbers (the bank rolls in rather than popping) and folds the sim's overcast / mist / drizzle into the weather layer (the spells' own rain, snow and storms stay cosmetic; fog, cloud and drizzle are the sim's); overcast also flattens contrast, quiets colour and cools the grade (`ColorGradingShader` uniforms only). | 2026-10-29 | Retaining walls and terraces (inspiration gap 1, coordinator batch 5: the north-star dry-stack walls and stairs) | **The wall is what the foundation's fieldstone already pays for, drawn along the pad, and it does real work.** `sim/retainPlan.ts` (pure, tested) plans the wall of any graded pad from its footprint and the natural ground: where the ground round the pad is 45 cm or more off the pad level the wall runs (a cut: ground above, the face looks into the pad; a fill: ground below, the face looks out), its height at every point, a flat 3.6 m apron on the walled sides (stops short of neighbours) so a stair has room, and up to two stairs (27 cm risers, 27 cm treads, 1.2 m wide, cheek stones) set through it where a flight fits clear of the building; a wall under 60 cm is stepped over, and a pad no stair or tapering end can reach leaves its walls open so no one is walled in. `game/terrain/RetainWalls.ts` draws it, one mesh per wall (vertex colours, no textures): courses of fieldstone in a running bond with a 1:7 batter, greener at the foot, a through-stone now and then, flat coping, weep-holes, a plain battered slab at distance (`low` 16 u, `medium` 28 u, `high` 40 u), rising with the foundation stage (0 before it, the stage's progress during it, whole after, a third of its height when slumped). On each fall the old wall posts are abandoned, three new wall posts stand on the inner ring, the defenders walk to them (a few seconds with no fire), and **the wounded are carried in to the sickbay** by the nearest defender on his feet (`carry` event; he is out of the line for 5 s; bleeding is held under pressure there; the wound is written 20 % lighter afterward). `PlannedBattle.rings` = rings, how many fell, wounded and dead per ring. **Sheltering by ring:** a raid that fell back and was then stopped takes what stood outside the ring that held: 35 % of the animals in barns, yards and coops out there and 12 % of the food in outer sheds and cellars (`exposure`), never the whole camp; a lot with no rings loses nothing extra but is breached at the first line. **Vetting:** with two or more manned rings, strangers on probation sleep in the outer ring and a thief steals only what stands there (at least a fifth of the camp's stores); the gate card says so. Measured, 60 seeds each (4 armed adults, crews of 6 and 10): breaches with 1 / 2 / 3 rings 1 / 0 / 0 and 5 / 1 / 1, against main's 1 / 1 / 1 and 5 / 2 / 0; defenders killed unchanged within noise. No save change. |

Their pace is the going: `lanePace` 1 on open ground, slower on a hard lane (1/hardness, less 0.1 for a cliff, 0.08 for a ford, floor 0.55), so a lot with hard ground gives its towers longer; no lane or no terrain = the old straight march. Tunnel-bridge lanes still gather at the door. `vehicleCan(grade, hardWinter)`: trucks stop at a pitch over 12 % (7 % once snow has closed the road). **Guard dogs:** a healthy dog on guard with training 25+ (two at most, `guardDogs`) is posted inside the compound at the inner gate; when a crew breaks in the dog goes for the first men through: each is held 2 + training/40 s and bitten (60 %, 12 + training/5 wound). Not yet done: the 3D wildlife and livestock still use their own movement (walkRoute is not wired into the wildlife renderer), and trucks in the 3D world do not read `vehicleCan` yet. No save change. |
| 2026-10-30 | Every defensive element is monotone (defense depth fix; the user's rule "a lot with strong defenses must make a raid harder"; coordinator task A) | **Found:** on an armed lot with no wall, three watchtowers were breached MORE (234 of 300 raids, against 218 with none) though fewer defenders died. The cause was the posting delay: when the alarm went the defenders *walked* to the tower or the sandbags (`dist / MOVE`), so the first volley, the one that makes a desperate crew break, came seconds late, fewer crews turned back before the line, and more raids ran on to the house. **Fix:** the watch is already up the tower and the sandbags were laid before the night (`assignPost`: 0.3 s to a tower or sandbag post, like an ambush's posts). Measured on 300 raids: 0 / 1 / 2 / 3 towers 218 / 159 / 141 / 139 breached; sandbags 239 -> 231. **Guard:** `sim/monotone.test.ts` fights 150 seeded raids on an armed lot with and without each element (one and three towers, a container wall, a stone wall with a tower, a guard dog, floodlights, sandbags, window bars, mines; three rings against one, and with sally ports), crews of 6 and 10, and fails if the breach rate with the element is higher than without it beyond two standard deviations. No save change. |

| 2026-10-30 | Roads polish (user's real-browser screenshots 30-32: loose cobble strip at the road's ends, faint grey strips in the grass near the compound; area B of the next round; save v71) | **What the strips were, and the rest of area B.** *Cobble strip:* the stone facing of a cut or fill bank (`roadWorks.bankStrip`) was drawn at the width the bank's tallest point asked for for the whole length of its span, so where the bank died away it lay flat on the grass as a loose strip and ended square at both ends. Now each row reaches only as far as the bank does (`bankExtent`: where the road's ground meets the natural ground again), the facing runs a few stations past the flagged span and thins to nothing, and a fill parapet is one unbroken course that is lower toward each end (it was a row of 3 m blocks with gaps). *Grey strips:* the drainage channel's gravel bed and lips (`perimeterGeometry` 'drain_channel') were boxes tilted by the ground at their two ends, so on lumpy ground they were half buried or floated over hollows; `beamFit` sinks each piece to the lowest ground under it and lifts it over a rise. *Road ends blend into the ground:* one soft mesh for the whole road (`edgeGeometries`): a gravel shoulder 0.2 unit (1.2 m) out each side that fades across (alpha 0 at the outer edge, 0.85 at the surface) and along the last 8 stations of each end that lies on open ground, and a dark ditch at the foot of every cut bank. *Stacked switchbacks:* hairpins were found by the heading change in a fixed 12-unit window, so a zigzag with a short leg between two turns cancelled in the window (no hairpin, or one); `sim/roadHairpins.ts` splits the road into runs that keep turning one way (a straight under 1.5 units bridges a run), takes the tightest stretch of each that turns 120 degrees in 12 units or less, and gives each switchback its own apex, landing, side and guardrail. *The entrance is the family's choice, suggest then confirm* (`sim/roadEntrance.ts`, `site.entrance`, the Land card's \"Where should the road come in?\"): the planner is run from five places along the east line plus the habitual one and each is read with the numbers the game already uses: the quote, days, length, steepest grade, hairpins, days a winter the road is shut with the truck as it is and with a plow blade (`closureFor`), the response multiplier (`responseOf`) and the raid choke (`chokesOf`, `startOf`: what a crew's trucks stop at and how many metres longer the watch sees them). Suggested = fewest shut days, then cheapest, then shortest; hovering a row draws its route in amber on the ground; \"Use this entrance\" confirms (`setEntrance`), until the drive is ordered, and not on an island lot (the causeway is the way in). *Listing = game:* a lot from the land market is flagged `site.listed` and its ground (county road, habitual entrance, woods, camp, road) is pinned to the lot's own seed (`sim/lotSeed.ts pinSeed`), so the road the listing describes is the road built; QA lots (`?lot=`, the showcase) and every older save keep the game's seed, so what they were tuned on does not move. *Snow plowing:* the Land card says it in numbers (`plowReport`: days a winter shut without / with the blade, the response multiplier in a closure 2.5 / 1.5), and with a blade in stock and snow down the pickup with the blade goes out the drive and back and leaves a snow berm on each edge (`game/fx/PlowRun.ts`); without the blade the drive is just snowed under. *Wildlife crossings* (`sim/roadWildlife.ts`): where the road crosses a draw it crosses over the animals' way: a pipe culvert is a passage for the small animals (relief 0.03), an arch culvert for deer (0.14), a bridge, ford or low-water slab for everything (0.2), a bore goes over the top; with all of them capped at 0.55 and 0.06 more for the warning signs, which stand either side of every crossing deer use (yellow diamond, a deer on it). A trip's chance of meeting a deer is 0.0006 a km x the region's hoofed herds against 8 a forty x (1 - relief), capped at 0.2, rolled in `roadTrouble` where the road's other trouble is rolled; a strike costs 0.06 of the vehicle's condition and a line; the Land card lists the crossings. *Razor wire on a wall with a patrol deck* (#116 gap): the deck no longer refuses it; the coils are drawn along the deck's outer rail (outside the guards' mesh) and the wall's own top is left bare; the sim effect is the wall's, as before. **Not built:** animals walking through the crossings in the 3D view, a plow driving in a closure's last day (the truck runs on any snow), choosing the entrance on the map by clicking the line. |

| 2026-10-30 | Micro-hydro and the wind turbine (area E, inspiration gap 1: infrastructure; stacked on the terrain analysis #101) | **Two more power sources whose output comes from the ground the lot really has; no save change** (the day's output is a pure function of seed, day, weather and ground; a broken one goes in `utilities.broken`, the pump's repair path; goods added since a save was made are filled at zero by `migrate`). `sim/renewables.ts` (pure, tested). **Hydro** (`micro_hydro`, 6 m turbine house on the creek bank, 2x2 cells): P = 9.81 x flow x net head x 0.62, capped at the 3 kW alternator and 30 L/s through the nozzles. Head = the creek's water-level fall between the intake weir and the house over the 100 m penstock the kit lays (shorter, so less head, when the lot's upstream edge comes first; net of 8 % friction); placement says no creek / not on the bank (1.2-14 u from the centerline) / under 2.5 m of head. Flow = 22 L/s x (rain/400 mm)^0.8, times the season (snowmelt peak around day 140 scaled by the region's snowfall, the PNW's wet winter, the August low), a 3-day jitter, a quarter of it in a drought, x6 in a flood; the water right lets you take 70 %. Freeze: intake ice from the mean overnight low (none above 22 F, solid at -12 F, `housing.meanLow`); a flood closes the intake gate (no output that day) and 12 % of the time takes the weir (`broken`: a hardware kit); leaves in October, silt in the runoff and ice load the new **`plant` chore** (intake screen, turbine and tower; 4 person-hours clears a full backlog; a buried screen costs up to 40 %), also on the Repairs duty. **Wind** (`wind_turbine`, 5 kW, 18 m guyed tilt-up tower): region mean at 10 m (plains 5.4 ... Appalachia 3.2 m/s) x the analysed ground (`terrain.wind`: hollow 0.6, ridge 1.4) x timber (-10 % per unit of cover) x hub shear (18 m, exponent 0.2) x season (+/-18 %, windier in winter and spring) x a seeded day factor (lognormal 0.35), then the power curve (cut-in 3, cubic to rated at 11, furled at 25) integrated over a Rayleigh spread (5.5 m/s hub wind = about 13 % capacity factor, 8 m/s = about 35 %). Storm gusts furl it; a derecho breaks it 10 % of the time, a windstorm 4 %, a tornado 40 %; ice storms and freezing fog ice the blades (a fifth of the output); about one year in six a bearing goes. **Scouts:** a running turbine adds up to 12 to `postureOf.visibility` (`turbineNoise`) and 9 to the opsec structure weight (the tower shows for kilometers); hydro adds 2. **Price table** (real 2025): turbine set $7,800 (Turgo runner, 3 kW alternator, controller), 50 m of 150 mm HDPE penstock $2,100, 5 kW turbine $14,500 (Bergey Excel class), 18 m tilt-up tower kit $5,200; hydro plant about $17k and 240 h (trench with the hired excavator, fused penstock, weir and screen box, set the turbine), wind about $25k and 120 h; county permits $1,800 (water right) and $900. **Looks:** block turbine house with a shed roof, the penstock laid along the ground with its trench spoil to a concrete weir and screen box on the creek (hidden when a flood has taken it), a lattice tower with guys and yellow guards, a three-blade rotor that spins with the day's output (still when furled, calm or broken). Balance: a 6 m head and 20 L/s makes about 0.5 kW (12 kWh a day, a 4.8 kW solar array's June); a 20 m fall hits the 3 kW cap. Perf: hydro adds about 9 and wind 8 merged meshes. |
| 2026-10-30 | Frost pockets and wind wired in (area E, stacked on micro-hydro and wind; the Terrain overlay already drew both) | **One module reads the lot's cold air and wind for everything that grows, makes power or burns wood; no save change.** `sim/microclimate.ts` (pure, tested; every number in `MC`). Cold air is the orchard's `plotSite` test (a pocket: low on near-level ground; a slope that drains it); wind is `renewables.windExposure` (the analysed wind field 0.6 hollow … 1.4 ridge, eased by timber); exposed is 1.15 or more (a crest about 3 m above the ground round it), sheltered 0.85 or less. **Garden:** each bed has its own season (`gardenSeasonK` in `needs.produceParts`, replacing the one shared ramp): a pocket starts 9 days later and ends 9 sooner, a draining slope starts 4 early, an exposed bed loses 5 %. **Field** (`crops.fieldFactor`): a pocket costs 8 % (frost in the grain-fill), an exposed field 4 %. **Orchard** (`orchard.fruitYear`): an exposed plot sets 8 % less fruit (bees stay in the hive above 5 m/s), on top of the pocket's blossom loss already there. A finished hedge, fence or wall within 2 units (12 m) halves every wind cost (`windbreakNear`). **Solar:** ground arrays run 0.7 % cooler-better per 0.1 of exposure above 1.0 (up to +3 %), but a windstorm, derecho, tornado or hurricane's remnants strips panels off an exposed one the day it hits (odds 50 % per unit of exposure above 1.0, doubled for a tornado; seeded by game, day and array; rooftop panels are the roof's business): the array goes in `utilities.broken` and makes nothing until a hardware kit and two solar panels (about $520) put them back (the pump's repair button). **Wood:** exposed homes burn up to 8 % more, homes in a hollow 4 % less (`heatWindK`, averaged over the homes, in `heating.demandNow`). **Siting:** the stake flash and the placement note say what the spot does for the kind (`siteNote`: garden, field, orchard, solar, turbine; polytunnels and the greenhouse join in the next PRs). Balance: an exposed knoll array loses a few percent of its panels a decade; a pocket garden loses about 10 % of its year; nothing is more than 10 %. |
| 2026-10-30 | Polytunnels, high tunnels and aquaponics (area E, stacked on the microclimate PR) | **Season extension on film, and a fish-and-greens bay, both on the existing greenhouse, pond, power and weather rules.** The tunnels join the greenhouse tiers (`sim/tunnels.ts`, pure, tested): polytunnel 6 x 12 m ($2.4k, 70 h: 21 days each end, peak +6 %, 250 kcal a day of cold-hardy winter greens) and high tunnel 9 x 30 m ($11k, 260 h: 35 days each end, peak +12 %, 520 kcal a day, unheated; extension-service figures). **Film:** 6 mil poly lasts 4 years (`Structure.filmAt`, optional, set by a re-cover job); after that 10 % a year less light to half, and a re-cover job (3 rolls, 30 h) is queued by `tunnelDay`; a new repair kind `film` joins the glass one (`improvements.crackFilm`; patch the poly 8 h or re-cover the hoops, `poly_film` $380 a roll). **Wind and snow:** a windstorm, derecho, tornado or hurricane's remnants tears the film with odds 0.3 x exposure squared (`renewables.windExposure`, halved by a windbreak, 1.6x for a derecho), a blizzard loads the hoops (high tunnels shed snow worst, cold regions most); hail and ice storms hole it like glass. **Water:** drip lines 120 / 420 L a day in the growing months, drawn from the pond first. **Pests:** whitefly, aphids, leaf miner or botrytis, a year-seeded outbreak of up to a fifth of the winter greens (damp regions more), halved when the greenhouse chore is tended (backlog under 40). **Aquaponics** (`sim/aquaponics.ts`, 6 x 12 m glazed bay, $13k, 230 h, `farm.aqua` made lazily like the pond's, no save bump): four 1,000 L tanks of tilapia (bag of 100 fingerlings $85) feeding 20 m2 of media beds. Fish grow 2.6 g a day at 26 C (none under 18 C, table size 450 g, 60 kg in the tanks, crowding slows them and invites disease), eat 2 % of their weight in the pond's `fish_feed` bags; whole fish are 0.45 kcal a gram, harvested 30 at a time from the Supplies tab; the plants (about 420 kcal a day of greens, 0.6 in the dark months, less under cracked glass) live on the fish waste, so unfed fish starve the beds. **Power:** 4 kWh a day for pumps plus up to 14 kWh of water heater against the cold (`aquaKwh`), first rung of the load list with the cellar and the well; **no power for a day in warm water kills a third of the fish (the air stops), and a few cold days kill them all** (a January blackout). Beds within 8 units of a running bay yield 3 % more (fish water). The pond chore (feeding, screens, a water test) is the care, and aquaponics adds to its load; the sump top-up (80 L a day) comes out of the stored water. Scouts: opsec weights 1 / 3 / 2; permits $300 for a high tunnel, $900 for the bay. Perf: the high tunnel is about 400 small pipes merged to 12 meshes; the bay about 10. |
