The other 35 Hardcore runs and all 36 Standard runs are unchanged, so it is a shifted outcome in a single seed rather than a balance change. |

| 2026-10-30 | Tunnel use: do people, dogs and vehicles really go through the rock tunnel (#124) and the tunnel-bridge (#107)? | **Yes: a corridor is a link in the route finding, with doors that open and shut.** `sim/corridors.ts` (pure, tested) names the finished, un-dropped, un-collapsed `vehicleWay` runs, their times (a loaded walker 1.35 m/s, a vehicle 7 m/s: the 236 m bore is 2.9 game minutes on foot, 34 s by vehicle), the doors (open by day, locked dusk to dawn and on the alarm; the family has keys, `KEY_S` 4 s a door; a crew is held `doorDelay`, the full lock at both doors) and the lost-way rules. `game/paths/linkRoute.ts` (pure, tested) labels the pieces of free ground (a flood fill of the world's `hard` test, cells 0.6 u, built once per layout and only where a corridor stands) and `routeLinked` sends anyone whose goal is on another piece through the links (up to three hops): ordinary route to the mouth, the deck (its height on every point), ordinary route from the far mouth; `deckWalk.ts` rides the figure on the deck under the hill and eases it back onto the ground. People (`FamilyRenderer`) and dogs (`DogRenderer`) take it, wait the keyholder's few seconds at a locked door and are never teleported across the rock (the 25-unit "far, just be there" shortcut only applies with no route); a goal that changes inside the bore is followed once they are out. Vehicles: the fleet drives out of and home into the compound through the bore (tilted with the deck, half the open-drive pace) and the delivery truck drives on past the end of the drive to a yard beyond the rock; both are key-holders, and `CorridorDoors` swings a bridge's leaves and lifts a bore's blast door for anyone with a key (and holds it shut while a crew works at it in the raid playback, forced when they are through). The sim reads it: the response factor adds the drive-through and, at night, the wait at the locked door (+2 for a fallen tunnel), a delivery is left at the portal when the way is choked, the weekend commute over a lost way (the bridge on the way in down, a tunnel-bridge dropped, a bore choked) leaves the cars outside, `Vehicle.stranded`, with the family walking in 0.2 day late and nothing leaving by car, until the way is open (`corridorDay` sends them home). **A crew with explosives that gets in by a tunnel may bring the portal down behind it** (`sapperBreach`: 10 % a charge, a rocket two, cap 55 %): the run stays standing but `collapsed`, nothing crosses on foot or wheels, and a contractor clears it (`clearCollapse`, $38 a m³ of a 640 m³ fall, 9 days; Defense tab button). A tunnel-bridge is still dropped by the family (`dropBridge`), never collapsed; the bore still cannot be dropped. Save 71 → 72 (`PerimeterRun.collapsed`, `Vehicle.stranded`, both optional). Not done here: the raid playback does not walk the crew through the bore (they gather at the outer door and are held per `doorDelay` as before), the planned road's own bores and fords are still driven at ground height, wildlife and livestock are unchanged (the doors and the hill keep them out). |
| 2026-10-30 | Micro-hydro and the wind turbine (area E, inspiration gap 1: infrastructure; stacked on the terrain analysis #101) | **Two more power sources whose output comes from the ground the lot really has; no save change** (the day's output is a pure function of seed, day, weather and ground; a broken one goes in `utilities.broken`, the pump's repair path; goods added since a save was made are filled at zero by `migrate`). `sim/renewables.ts` (pure, tested). **Hydro** (`micro_hydro`, 6 m turbine house on the creek bank, 2x2 cells): P = 9.81 x flow x net head x 0.62, capped at the 3 kW alternator and 30 L/s through the nozzles. Head = the creek's water-level fall between the intake weir and the house over the 100 m penstock the kit lays (shorter, so less head, when the lot's upstream edge comes first; net of 8 % friction); placement says no creek / not on the bank (1.2-14 u from the centerline) / under 2.5 m of head. Flow = 22 L/s x (rain/400 mm)^0.8, times the season (snowmelt peak around day 140 scaled by the region's snowfall, the PNW's wet winter, the August low), a 3-day jitter, a quarter of it in a drought, x6 in a flood; the water right lets you take 70 %. Freeze: intake ice from the mean overnight low (none above 22 F, solid at -12 F, `housing.meanLow`); a flood closes the intake gate (no output that day) and 12 % of the time takes the weir (`broken`: a hardware kit); leaves in October, silt in the runoff and ice load the new **`plant` chore** (intake screen, turbine and tower; 4 person-hours clears a full backlog; a buried screen costs up to 40 %), also on the Repairs duty. **Wind** (`wind_turbine`, 5 kW, 18 m guyed tilt-up tower): region mean at 10 m (plains 5.4 ... Appalachia 3.2 m/s) x the analysed ground (`terrain.wind`: hollow 0.6, ridge 1.4) x timber (-10 % per unit of cover) x hub shear (18 m, exponent 0.2) x season (+/-18 %, windier in winter and spring) x a seeded day factor (lognormal 0.35), then the power curve (cut-in 3, cubic to rated at 11, furled at 25) integrated over a Rayleigh spread (5.5 m/s hub wind = about 13 % capacity factor, 8 m/s = about 35 %). Storm gusts furl it; a derecho breaks it 10 % of the time, a windstorm 4 %, a tornado 40 %; ice storms and freezing fog ice the blades (a fifth of the output); about one year in six a bearing goes. **Scouts:** a running turbine adds up to 12 to `postureOf.visibility` (`turbineNoise`) and 9 to the opsec structure weight (the tower shows for kilometers); hydro adds 2. **Price table** (real 2025): turbine set $7,800 (Turgo runner, 3 kW alternator, controller), 50 m of 150 mm HDPE penstock $2,100, 5 kW turbine $14,500 (Bergey Excel class), 18 m tilt-up tower kit $5,200; hydro plant about $17k and 240 h (trench with the hired excavator, fused penstock, weir and screen box, set the turbine), wind about $25k and 120 h; county permits $1,800 (water right) and $900. **Looks:** block turbine house with a shed roof, the penstock laid along the ground with its trench spoil to a concrete weir and screen box on the creek (hidden when a flood has taken it), a lattice tower with guys and yellow guards, a three-blade rotor that spins with the day's output (still when furled, calm or broken). Balance: a 6 m head and 20 L/s makes about 0.5 kW (12 kWh a day, a 4.8 kW solar array's June); a 20 m fall hits the 3 kW cap. Perf: hydro adds about 9 and wind 8 merged meshes. |
| 2026-10-30 | Frost pockets and wind wired in (area E, stacked on micro-hydro and wind; the Terrain overlay already drew both) | **One module reads the lot's cold air and wind for everything that grows, makes power or burns wood; no save change.** `sim/microclimate.ts` (pure, tested; every number in `MC`). Cold air is the orchard's `plotSite` test (a pocket: low on near-level ground; a slope that drains it); wind is `renewables.windExposure` (the analysed wind field 0.6 hollow … 1.4 ridge, eased by timber); exposed is 1.15 or more (a crest about 3 m above the ground round it), sheltered 0.85 or less. **Garden:** each bed has its own season (`gardenSeasonK` in `needs.produceParts`, replacing the one shared ramp): a pocket starts 9 days later and ends 9 sooner, a draining slope starts 4 early, an exposed bed loses 5 %. **Field** (`crops.fieldFactor`): a pocket costs 8 % (frost in the grain-fill), an exposed field 4 %. **Orchard** (`orchard.fruitYear`): an exposed plot sets 8 % less fruit (bees stay in the hive above 5 m/s), on top of the pocket's blossom loss already there. A finished hedge, fence or wall within 2 units (12 m) halves every wind cost (`windbreakNear`). **Solar:** ground arrays run 0.7 % cooler-better per 0.1 of exposure above 1.0 (up to +3 %), but a windstorm, derecho, tornado or hurricane's remnants strips panels off an exposed one the day it hits (odds 50 % per unit of exposure above 1.0, doubled for a tornado; seeded by game, day and array; rooftop panels are the roof's business): the array goes in `utilities.broken` and makes nothing until a hardware kit and two solar panels (about $520) put them back (the pump's repair button). **Wood:** exposed homes burn up to 8 % more, homes in a hollow 4 % less (`heatWindK`, averaged over the homes, in `heating.demandNow`). **Siting:** the stake flash and the placement note say what the spot does for the kind (`siteNote`: garden, field, orchard, solar, turbine; polytunnels and the greenhouse join in the next PRs). Balance: an exposed knoll array loses a few percent of its panels a decade; a pocket garden loses about 10 % of its year; nothing is more than 10 %. |
| 2026-10-30 | Polytunnels, high tunnels and aquaponics (area E, stacked on the microclimate PR) | **Season extension on film, and a fish-and-greens bay, both on the existing greenhouse, pond, power and weather rules.** The tunnels join the greenhouse tiers (`sim/tunnels.ts`, pure, tested): polytunnel 6 x 12 m ($2.4k, 70 h: 21 days each end, peak +6 %, 250 kcal a day of cold-hardy winter greens) and high tunnel 9 x 30 m ($11k, 260 h: 35 days each end, peak +12 %, 520 kcal a day, unheated; extension-service figures). **Film:** 6 mil poly lasts 4 years (`Structure.filmAt`, optional, set by a re-cover job); after that 10 % a year less light to half, and a re-cover job (3 rolls, 30 h) is queued by `tunnelDay`; a new repair kind `film` joins the glass one (`improvements.crackFilm`; patch the poly 8 h or re-cover the hoops, `poly_film` $380 a roll). **Wind and snow:** a windstorm, derecho, tornado or hurricane's remnants tears the film with odds 0.3 x exposure squared (`renewables.windExposure`, halved by a windbreak, 1.6x for a derecho), a blizzard loads the hoops (high tunnels shed snow worst, cold regions most); hail and ice storms hole it like glass. **Water:** drip lines 120 / 420 L a day in the growing months, drawn from the pond first. **Pests:** whitefly, aphids, leaf miner or botrytis, a year-seeded outbreak of up to a fifth of the winter greens (damp regions more), halved when the greenhouse chore is tended (backlog under 40). **Aquaponics** (`sim/aquaponics.ts`, 6 x 12 m glazed bay, $13k, 230 h, `farm.aqua` made lazily like the pond's, no save bump): four 1,000 L tanks of tilapia (bag of 100 fingerlings $85) feeding 20 m2 of media beds. Fish grow 2.6 g a day at 26 C (none under 18 C, table size 450 g, 60 kg in the tanks, crowding slows them and invites disease), eat 2 % of their weight in the pond's `fish_feed` bags; whole fish are 0.45 kcal a gram, harvested 30 at a time from the Supplies tab; the plants (about 420 kcal a day of greens, 0.6 in the dark months, less under cracked glass) live on the fish waste, so unfed fish starve the beds. **Power:** 4 kWh a day for pumps plus up to 14 kWh of water heater against the cold (`aquaKwh`), first rung of the load list with the cellar and the well; **no power for a day in warm water kills a third of the fish (the air stops), and a few cold days kill them all** (a January blackout). Beds within 8 units of a running bay yield 3 % more (fish water). The pond chore (feeding, screens, a water test) is the care, and aquaponics adds to its load; the sump top-up (80 L a day) comes out of the stored water. Scouts: opsec weights 1 / 3 / 2; permits $300 for a high tunnel, $900 for the bay. Perf: the high tunnel is about 400 small pipes merged to 12 meshes; the bay about 10. |

A crew coming across it is held `CROSS_S` 14 s x level before the first are over and each raider at the water risks 15-45 hp a second at 5 % x level (cold shock, a fall); a filled moat floods a tunnel dug within 7 m like a ditch. It only ever adds time and hurt (never the line the defenders hold: a moat with no wall behind it is the line). **Covered bridge** (`covered_bridge`, flag `bridge`): a roofed timber Howe truss across a moat of yours or the creek (the planner says why not: 6-40 m, needs water under it; greyed out with no creek and no moat); a gated chokepoint, not a wall (blocks 0): with a bridge over the moat the crew walks to it along the moat (up to 30 s), files across (4 s) and is held at its plank gate like a tunnel door (`doorDelay`: 8 s locked at night or on the alarm, 2 s open by day), no wading. Weaker than steel (a gate hold, not a breach plan) and cheaper (timber, roofing, hardware; permit $600). The wet crossing costs a crew more than the bridge, so a moat with a bridge is slightly easier than a moat alone and still harder than none (`monotone.test.ts`: rain moat, pond-fed moat, moat with bridge, moat behind a stone wall and tower). No save change (new run kinds only). **Vehicle yard** (`vehicle_yard`, `sim/vehicleYard.ts`): a fenced gravel lot with the equipment yard's six bays, built outside the walls past the motor court; what stands in it is exposed whether or not the line holds: each vehicle is driven off (70 %) or burned out (30 %, condition 0) with the equipment-yard chance (looters 40 % / organized 60 %) x 0.6 fenced x 0.85 lit x 0.7 if a tower sees it x 0.5 if the raid was held; a crew of 5 or more sends up to a third of itself for the vehicles (0.12 per running vehicle), so the house is easier to hold at the price of the trucks. **Open:** the moat's numbers (14 s, 5 %, 15-45 hp) are first-pass; the user can retune in `WATER`. Fire on the timber bridge (a crew burning it) is not modeled. |

The game assigns by need (elders, little kids, then larger first): each household takes the first home with room for the WHOLE household (relatives and elders try separate dwellings first), else housing.ts spreads it bed by bed as before. | 2026-11-04 | The long game, part 2: orphans, yearly threats, off-grid milestone, upgrade lines (user approved the recommended defaults for the open long-game questions). Save v75. | **(1) Orphans** (`sim/orphans.ts`): when the last grown person is dead and only children remain, a neighbor household takes them in (the warmest, then nearest, that is still at home with room, not a recluse, disposition above -40), named in the chronicle. They leave the roster and become **wards** (`lineage.wards`): they age on their birthdays, pick up the host's skill now and then, still count as the line (the wipe test, the dynasty score's person-years), and the place is held as it stands (the sim runs on with nobody home, no report card is dealt). When the eldest turns 18 they are the heir: all the wards come home with them and the place is theirs. A host that dies out passes them to the next household; with none left they are lost. **A wipe only if no neighbor can**: with no household able to take them the children leave to find someone and the run ends. **(2) Yearly threats** (`sim/threats.ts`, `threatState.ts`): one roll a year (autumn) from a year after the hit, only while the world has not come back as far as it will (`stats.okSince < 0`, always so when it never comes back), one at a time. Odds at full strength: never back 70 %, partly 50 %, back but not yet there 30 %; **Cozy 0, Standard half strength, Hardcore full** (so 35 / 25 / 15 % a year on Standard). Kind is weighted toward what the world lacks (no doctors, empty shelves, no law). No new machinery: **plague** 60 days of fevers at home (`effects.infect`, 6 % a day x strength) and a worse year for the neighbors' sickness; **famine** 300 days of the growing season down by up to 35 % x strength (`effects.climate`); **hard winter** the existing freeze-and-thaw on a window of its own (`adaptive.winterDay`, strength scales the wood, fuel and spoilage); **warlord scare** 45 days of raid pressure up to +50 % x strength (`opsec.raidPressure`). One chronicle line when it starts and one when it ends. **(3) "First winter off-grid"** (25): only if the place started on the county grid (`lineage.startedOnGrid`) and the family pulled the meter (`lineage.gridLeft`, set by Leave, cleared by Rejoin), and a deep winter (the turn of the year) has gone by since. The grid failing in a disaster does not count. **(4) The chronicle logs upgrades and rebuilds** (a structure or wall upgraded to the next tier; a finished structure knocked back by a storm or fire and finished again, `Structure.lost`): one line each, at most 3 a year (`chronicleUpkeep`). Migration 74 -> 75 back-fills the fields; a place still on the grid counts as having started on it. **Balance:** the threats are the only change that touches survival, from year two after a hit on a world that has not recovered; sim-only bot playtest numbers in the PR. |
| 2026-11-04 | Raid depth: a tunnel brought down as a raid outcome, and per-ring gate vetting (NEXT-ROUND areas A / C stubs; save v78) | **(1) Collapse.** The sim already had `collapseCorridor`, `PerimeterRun.collapsed`, `Vehicle.stranded` and the contractor clearing; what was missing is wired now. A crew that gets in by a tunnel and carries explosives can bring the portal down (rock tunnel / slope tunnel: rubble, 640 m3, a contractor's $, ~9 days) **or drop a tunnel-bridge into the gorge** (a plan again until rebuilt; 0.6x as likely, a span is harder to charge than a portal). Vehicles out on a run are cut off outside (`strandAway`, drawn at the outer end, home again when the way opens), vehicles inside cannot leave, the bug-out route reads a fallen tunnel (no vehicle, a 15 % climb leg on foot), the chronicle gets a line, and the Defense tab says what holds it down. **Counters** (`collapseGuard`, all multipliers <= 1 so a counter never raises the chance): a blast door deep in a bore's portal x0.6; listening posts (powered ground sensors) up to x0.7 at full coverage of the wall; each manned tower / guard post within 14 tiles of a portal x0.85 (three count). **Balance flag:** a bore used to fall at 10 % a charge (cap 55 %); it is now 6 % (cap 33 %) because of the blast door. Monotone: `monotone.test.ts` compares a lot with a tunnel-bridge to the same lot plus each counter (and a collapsed portal) and checks the chance only falls as counters are added; per-ring vetting chosen but unused is an element too. Finding: a bare tunnel-bridge on the lot raised the crew-of-10 breach rate (0.85 to 0.91, it is an extra lane) before any counter, so it is compared against itself, not against no tunnel. **(2) Per-ring vetting.** With two or more manned rings the gate card (arrivals) has one line, *Vet at: nowhere / outer / inner / both*, with the detail in the tooltip. A look catches a fever (55 %, + 4 %/level of the best medic), a thief (35 %) or a scout (30 %, + 4 %/level of the best talker); the inner gate sees them after days under watch (x1.15) but a thief caught there has had the outer ring (25 % of a revealed thief's take, scaled by `vetting().reach`); a scout caught warns the place (`forewarned`). Both gates multiply out. Cost: 2 guard-hours a look and 4 points (max 12) off the watch's detection that day (`watchDetection`). A caught arrival is not admitted ('Turned back at the gate', counts as turned away, no humanity loss). Default is nowhere, so nothing changes until the player chooses; bots never choose. `gate.vet` / `gate.vetBusy`, save v78 with migration. |
| 2026-11-04 | GDS audit gaps (GDS front matter, §2.4, §5.3, §5.6, §6; docs/GDS_AUDIT.md) | **God Mode** = Free build plus checking topped up to $50M every step, run flagged: the report card grade is "S" with a "not graded" line (`sim/godMode.ts`; new `Mode` value `god`, no new state field, so no save bump). **Settlement stage** (homestead under 8 people, compound 8+, hamlet 15+, walled village 30+ with a closed ring, small town 80+ with 12 homes) and **play style** (Hermit, Clan, Ark, Haven from people taken in, turned away, strangers and exposure) are derived labels on the report card (`sim/settlement.ts`); thresholds are a provisional choice. **Comfort and beauty**: a "place worth living in" help in `sim/mind.ts` (garden / orchard / greenhouse up to 2.5, pond 1, kids with a home 1, cap 4 strain points; `sim/comfort.ts`). Playtest (36 two-year runs each): Standard prepper 24/33 alive both before and after, homestead 12/30 -> 13/32, idle 4/27 -> 6/29; Hardcore idle 2/35 -> 2/35, prepper 20/37 both, homestead 7/29 -> 9/32. **Fire overlay**: Terrain: Fire rings each building by its wildfire burn chance, the formula moved to `sim/fireRisk.ts` (effects unchanged). |

| 2026-11-04 | Starter compound (user playtest: "make the barrier to getting a full operational compound easier; still instruct/suggest") | `sim/starterCompound.ts` (pure, tested), `ui/hud/StarterCompound.tsx`, `game/StarterPreview.ts`. A **Lay out a starter compound** button on the Build tab's Get operational card (a one-line first-run hint when nothing is staked yet) proposes, for THIS lot, the smallest home that sleeps the family (cottage 3 / cabin 4 / log house 6), a well (drilled where water is deep), a privy kept 30 m from it and downhill, solar with a battery, a garden and a root cellar, and a woven-wire (else barbed, else split-rail) fence round the yard with a gate where the drive crosses it. It is a smart batch over the existing rules, nothing new: spots pass `siteProblem` and the graded-pad clash (so steep lots get pads and walls as usual), keep 4.5 m between footprints, and everything stands within 60 m of the house, inside the 70 m the utilities reach by themselves. Prices come from the graded blueprints net of the stockpile plus permit fees; the shelter, water, privy, power, food, fence order is funded until the cash less a 25 % living reserve (min $15k) runs out, and the rest is listed as "later", never blocking. Shown as ghosts with one line (pieces, cost, hours, permits); Place all, One by one, or Cancel; each row has an x to leave a piece out. Staked pieces are ordinary structures (move, cancel, upgrade as any), logs trigger the usual tree marking, and "Order what the plans need" continues the flow; Next steps carry on from state. Deterministic by seed. No save change. |
| 2026-11-04 | Flow as extra labor hours (GDS §3 flow; user chose option a) | `sim/flow.ts` (pure, tested, derived from finished structure positions, **no save change**): each chore duty's person-hours are divided by 1 + overhead, where overhead is 0 within 25 m of the nearest home and +1 % per 15 m beyond, capped at 6 %. Legs: water = home to the nearest well / cistern / tower (creek hauling keeps its own rate); wood = home to the wood oven / sugar shack; kitchen = mean of the root cellar and the garden / greenhouse; garden = nearest garden; animals = nearest coop / barn. A duty whose target is not built has none. Applied once in `daily.addChoreWork`. UI: one `Note` in the Utilities water section, only when a duty is at 1 % or more ('Long carries: +3% hours on water, ...'). No overlay. Playtest numbers in the PR. |
| 2026-11-04 | Terrain and infrastructure pass (user: a dark road ran down into a red-brown gorge with no bridge: "shouldn't that be a bridge?") | **Finding:** the lot's roads and structures were already sane (the planned drive has culverts, fords, bridges and bores; placement rejects water, cross-slope and steep ground; lakes count as water), but the **county road** was a ribbon draped on `groundAt`, so wherever a creek, a ravine or a gorge crossed it the road dived into the cut and climbed the far wall, through the water. The red-brown band is the gorge's own steep wall (the terrain shader's rock colour on slope), not a road feature or clay. **Fix (no save change; derived from the ground):** `sim/countyCrossings.ts` (pure, tested) finds the gaps from the ground under the road (grey closing over ±84 m; a dry cut of 6 m or more, or any water, is a gap; the deck rests on the rims; dry valleys wider than 72 m are graded as before, water is always bridged) and `sim/roadState.countyGapsFor` reads the creek and lake; `HomesteadWorld` draws the county ribbon on the deck height over each gap and `roadWorks.buildCountyBridges` adds a two-lane steel-girder deck (deeper girders on long spans), stone abutments, rails and concrete piers every ~48 m down to the floor (merged geometry, one extra mesh per material); the delivery truck follows the deck; the neighbors' line fences stop at the span. Audit test over 11 regions x 8 lot kinds x 2 seeds keeps the gap list sane. **Open (options in the PR):** the county road over a lake (island and bluff lots) is a long trestle rather than a reroute; dry washes and small gullies still dip. QA harness `scripts/terrainQa.mjs`. |

| 2026-11-05 | Refugee-loop investigation + spend guard counts hired crews (strategy-sweep QA; no save change, no balance change) | **(1) The reported loop is not a sim loop.** Reproduced the family scenario through the real HUD: every card (kin, shelter, arrival, refugees, looters, gang) cleared on its first stance; nothing queued or overwrote. What the harness notes meant: `people 26-32` is `game.people.length` (everyone who ever lived here, guests included), `left 22` is neighbor guests who went home or out of the door (`needs.left`; they are also `died != null`, so not in `alive`), `hunger 700k+` is the largest *cumulative* shortfall of an idle family that never stocks food (`needs.hunger` is uncapped, drains 600/day when fed; left alone), the 14-day beat is the harness's own `continueStep`, and `kind=null` is not a gate: nothing is pending, a result card (`gate.card`, e.g. "Yesenia is gone") waits for Carry on and the loop clears it. Refugee columns are 6-15 heads (x the difficulty raid factor) and have no Take in stance, so no group can grow. Engine tests (`sim/gateLoop.test.ts`) step rich idle families 5 years through pandemic / drought / grid-down and pin: one card at a time (never swapped), every card has a kind and stances, every stance of every card kind clears the gate, sizes <= 30. One real hole closed: `gateDay` now also waits while a raid is `incoming` (scheduled for its hour) so a second card cannot be rolled over it. Found on the way: a person with two infections made two identical `medReport.needs` lines (React duplicate-key console errors, the same line twice on the next-step card); now one line, plus the list keyed by index. Harness: the NULL-KIND note says what it is. **(2) Spend guard counts committed crew wages.** `spendGuard.committedCrewWages` (hours left on each hired crew's building x `CREW_RATE`) and `spendCheckCommitted(money, cost, committed)`; `useSpendGuard` uses it, so successive crew hires (and any purchase) are judged against cash minus wages already claimed, with the reserve still 25 % of cash (floor $15k). Guidance only: same "Do it anyway / Keep a reserve" buttons, the line adds "once the $X of crew wages still to pay is set aside". Open: idle-family hunger is uncapped by design (recovery 600/day); a cap would change balance, left as is. |

| 2026-11-05 | Life on the county road (user: "occasional cars and trucks and motorcycles... life as normal, then when SHTF less so, but roving raiders and other wandering parties") | **Visual only, no save change, no sim change.** `sim/roadTraffic.ts` (pure, tested): every party is a function of (seed, slot, context), nothing stored. **Peace:** cars, pickups, SUVs, vans, semis, flatbeds, motorcycles (warm months), RVs, cyclists, tractors in farm regions (plains, upper midwest, ozarks, rust belt, hill country), a school bus on weekday mornings / afternoons outside June-August; density by hour (commute peaks, quiet nights), day of week, region (rust belt / New England about 1.5x, desert about 0.5x). Headlights after dark. **Collapse curve** from the real clock (`disaster.phase`, `tension`, days since the hit, the fuel / deliveries / law tracks): warning = busier (fleeing, loaded cars, convoys for nuclear / invasion / civil war / zombies, hazmat for pandemic / bioweapon); hit = sharp drop to each disaster's floor; aftermath follows the tracks. Solar flare strands up to 6 cars at once and kills headlights; grid-down strands 3 (an ice storm fewer); stalled cars are seeded and only ever added, towed when grid and fuel are both back. Nuclear / asteroid / ash winter leave almost nothing; financial / cyber / drought thin the cars and add feet and bicycles. **Aftermath life:** wanderers on foot (lone scavengers, pairs with packs, families with a handcart, columns) and cosmetic roving crews running dark; they never feed the encounter generator. **Real encounters on the road:** a scheduled raid (`gate.incoming`) is the same crew (`partyForEncounter`: same heads, vehicles by kind: looters one pickup, gang two or three trucks of about four, militia / patrol / requisition olive trucks, strangers on foot or in the pickup / rig they bring) driving the last stretch of road as its hour comes (`approachDistance`, from the day change it was rolled to `at`, never faster than a car); any other encounter pending at the gate stands on the road outside it with the real headcount, and drives or walks on when it is dealt with unless the family took them in. No new odds: raid strength, timing and defense scaling are untouched. **Render:** `game/RoadTraffic.ts`: one `BatchedMesh` (all kinds, per-instance paint, no shadows) + one additive `InstancedMesh` for head- and taillights = 2 draw calls, hidden when empty; caps Low / Medium / High 3 / 7 / 12 parties, 14 / 30 / 52 drawn, 70 / 100 / 140 draw range; riding the county deck over bridged gaps via `countyY`. Geometry `game/props/trafficGeometry.ts` (procedural, SCALE v2). QA `scripts/roadLifeShots.mjs`. **Not built (options in the PR):** passers-by noticing your lights and coming back, trading with passers-by, road blocks, radio rumors of road activity. |
| 2026-11-05 | Regional visual fixes from the start-matrix sweep (visual only, no save change, no new draw calls) | **(1) Woodpile** (`props/yardGeometry.ts`): the camp pile was 63 three-sided prisms thrown at random angles (floating wedge shards); now 7 hex-packed rows of 7-sided logs lying across the pile, bottom row on two skids, under the tin sheet (about 1.2 m high, 3 m long). **(2) Eye camera inside props**: `openSpotNear` (the QA eye spot) ignored the yard, camp, buildings and the stockpile road; it now rejects spots within reach of any of them, and the in-game camera (`crownAt`, which `DioramaCamera.solidAt` and Walk use) and `walkBlocked` now treat the yard's picnic table, woodpile, grill, tub, block and stools as solid (`YARD_BOXES`, tested). **(3) Desert**: ground palette tan / sand (`regionalLandscape` desert look: grass `0xa8956a`, dry grass `0xc2a678`, scrub dots olive-gray instead of forest green), `ShrubField` gets a `desert_scrub` form (`buildDesertScrub`: creosote lobes + a yucca rosette + prickly-pear pads in the one merged geometry, so the same single sage draw call), desert scrub density 0.55 to 1, wash / creek-line trees are junipers (scale 0.34-0.58) not cottonwoods, juniper palette sun-bleached. **(4) New England**: the listing-timber to region-woods handover (`ParcelTerrain.natural`, `wt`) was the parcel's axis-aligned ruler edge; its distance is now warped by two octaves of noise and lot cover is dithered, so the stand edge is ragged with tongues, clearings and the understory saplings. **(5) Great Plains** leaf-out day 112 to 103 (mid-April; a game that opens mid-April no longer starts on bare cottonwood trunks). The real gaps were things with no way to spend: no medical bay above ground (only the bunker's `bunker_medical`), nothing past a ham rig on the radio, nothing at the drive, and land that a neutral neighbor "won't sell" at any price. **Built (`sim/richSinks.ts`, goods in the stockpile like the house hardening chain, no new geometry, no new draw calls):** (1) **Clinic suite** $42,000 (Medical shelf): counts as the medical bay (`medicalBay(s)` replaces the four bunker-only checks in `medical.ts`, `needs.ts`, `effects.ts`: faster healing, fewer infections, the sick kept apart); (2) **Radio mast & repeater** $11,500 (Kit shelf): the set hears 15 points more often and truer (`reception`), nothing without a radio; (3) **Hardened gate house** $68,000 (Kit & security): +10 wall strength and +6 warning in `gearPosture` (clamped like the rest, so it can only raise the gate odds); (4) **Cash offer for land** (`land.ts`): an at-home neighbor who is neutral (disposition -20 to 29) and has not listed the parcel will sell at 1.6x asking; a feud (< -20) still refuses, a friend still sells at the going rate; the Land panel marks such parcels "cash offer, +60%". | 2026-11-05 | Cozy: warn, then kill slowly (user decision; save v79) | Cozy used to hold health at 5 forever, so a family that never ate stayed alive for years (16 of 36 playtest runs >30 hungry days, 726 hungry days with 4/4 alive) and `needs.hunger` grew to 700k+. **Rule** (`sim/starvation.ts`, pure, tested): a person's `Needs.starved` counts consecutive days at hunger >15,000 while eating under half their need; eating at least half resets it. At 30 days a Family-panel line and a log entry say "X has been starving for N days; they will begin to die if this goes on" (again at 110). The Cozy health floor (5, in `needs.ts` and `body.ts`) is replaced by `healthFloor`: 5 until the person's own threshold (120 days for kids, 65+ and the sick; healthy adults 150, plus 0-40 days by id), then sinking 0.1 a day, so health drains over about 50 days before death: weakest first, staggered. Standard/Hardcore: floor was already 0, no change. `hunger` clamped at 400,000 (all difficulties; far past the 15k where health falls). **Save v79**: `Needs.starved` (old saves start at 0, hunger clamped in the migration). **Playtest (YEARS=6, 36 runs each, before/after):** Standard and Hardcore reports identical row for row (58/124 alive, 8 wipes; 36/132 alive, 18 wipes). Cozy 148/151 alive, 0 wipes before; after 103/148 alive, 8 wipes, all in runs that were already starving 600+ days or idle bots that never stocked food (first deaths ~day 210+ of the run, none before 120 starving days); runs with a few weeks of hunger are unaffected. | Warn first, kill slowly, Cozy stays gentle for anyone who eats even badly. Tune `STARVE_*` in `sim/starvation.ts`. |
| 2026-11-05 | Encounter lead time on the road (user: non-raid encounters should be seen walking or driving up the county road before the card, like scheduled raids) | **Only WHEN the card appears moves; same encounter, headcount, stances, results.** `sim/roadLead.ts` (pure): refugees, trader, arrival, kin, shelter and neighbor_help, whether rolled by `gateDay` or by kin / newcomers / neighbor life at the day change, are held in the existing `gate.incoming` (`{ e, at, from }`) for an approach time: 30-90 game min driving, 60-180 on foot (quarter-hour steps; foot or vehicle read off `partyForEncounter`), deterministic in (seed, encounter id, day) from its own generator so no sim roll shifts. The card appears at `at` through `arrivalCheck`; the road draws the real party approaching from `from` (`RoadTraffic` passes `inc.from` to `approachDistance`), then the existing tableau and leave logic. Raids, break-ins, tunnelers, move-in, stranded, trial end and ransom are untouched, so defenses and raid odds are unchanged. One at a time: `gateDay` already rolls nothing while `incoming` is set, and a held card never starts if one is incoming. Nobody home: no approach starts, and one under way is dropped at its hour with no card; also dropped if the family has gone to town (`emptyNow`). A non-raid approach is not an alarm (`emergencyOf` ignores it), and no new detection mechanic was added (the family does not know they are coming). The producer's own "at the gate" log line is removed so the title reads once, at arrival. At 1x a game hour is about 2.5 s, so a 90 min approach shows for about 4 s and walkers cover roughly 60 units (the 8 u/s MAX_RUSH cap); faster speeds show less. **Save: no bump.** `gate.incoming` only gains an optional `from`; old saves (no incoming, or a raid incoming without `from`) load and play as before (tested). Playtest (36 runs per difficulty, same seeds): Standard 5 wipes, 60/122 alive, grades A4 B9 C1 D3 F19; Hardcore 15 wipes, 39/133 alive, A5 B1 C3 D2 F25; reports byte-identical before and after (the agent answers cards once a day, so a 1-3 h delay inside the day changes nothing it sees; the lead path was confirmed active in those runs). |

| 2026-11-05 | Wall tops and weak spots (user: study South African home security and apply it; PR A of 3; docs/sa_home_security.md; no save-version bump, see below) | **Audit first:** electric_steel (a whole wall tier needing power) and per-run razor wire already existed; the electric fence on outriggers is the add-on for every other wall, and razor gains the missing pieces. **(1) Electric fence** (`sim/wallTops.ts`, pure, tested; every number in `WALL_TOP`, all tunable): `PerimeterRun.electric` on a finished chain-link / iron / bar / container / stone / concrete / palisade run (wire + cable + hardware per 6 m, 2.2 h, $650 energizer, +$250 certificate while the county works; optional sealed battery $400). **Never on a run with razor, in either order** (razor cannot be electrified). Live on the grid or lit on-site power; with the battery it rides out `BACKUP_DAYS` = 2 of mains outage (`darkSince`, set by the daily tick) then goes dark; dark it adds +0.02 of stop instead of +0.10 and shocks nobody. Live it adds +0.10 to what the run stops (`peopleBlock`), slows a climber (x0.55 breach chance), shocks at the line (p 0.14, 20-60 hp, in `battle.ts` as a second hazard beside the wall's own), and 30 m or more live is an alarm (+8 detection through `gearPosture`; a cut or climb trips it). Yellow signs come with it: `signs` deter opportunists only (the warning stance ignores them for gang / militia / patrol / requisition / tunnelers; break-in chance x0.85). A home install with no county to certify is `noCert`; while the county works and permits are on, the inspector may fine it ($600 + the certificate). **(2) Razor** keeps its price and +0.06; now also slows a climber (x0.8) and snags (p 0.05) in the battle, and in a strict county (`strict` >= 1) in peacetime the inspector orders it down once ($400 fine, coils removed, cannot be hung again while the law works). **(3) Weak spots:** a house or outbuilding whose edge is within 0.3 unit (about 2 m) of a climbable wall (not towers, the gate garage, vehicle yard, bunkers, fields), a tree within reach (read from the world, overlay and felling only), brush past 0.5. The sim cost is `weakPenalty` = 0.04 each, up to 0.2 off what that run stops in `postureOf` (the compound ring; the property line is untouched) and x(1 + 2 x penalty) on the climbers' breach chance; trees are not in the sim (the world's trees are renderer-side) so brush stands in for them. **(4) Brush** grows on every climbable wall in the growing season (day 90-290, sine; 0.6 %/day at the height, about half a year to full), is cut back by hand (`trimRun`, 1.2 h per 6 m, logged like the razor hours, no cost), shades the cameras (up to -40 % of their reach), cuts a live fence's stop and shock by up to 60 %. **Monotone:** every top (razor, live, backup, dark, brush-choked) only helps a raid (10 new cases in `monotone.test.ts` against the bare stone wall: breach rate and defender deaths); the weak spots are a maintenance penalty on existing structures, not a defensive element (towers are excluded so building one never weakens a wall). **UI:** Defense > Wall tops and weak spots (fit, battery, trim, mark trees, Show overlay). **Visual:** outriggers at 35 degrees with three copper wires, white insulators and a yellow sign every fourth post; brush clumps on both faces; a one-draw-call overlay (red ring: building, orange: tree, green: brush). **Save:** only optional fields on a run (`electric`, `backup`, `darkSince`, `noCert`, `brush`, `razorOrdered`) whose absence means exactly the old behavior, like `razor` before; no version bump (main is at 81). **Balance flags:** brush and weak spots are new pressure on every existing walled lot (capped at -0.2 of a wall, trimming is free in cash); the electric numbers are guesses, not measured. |

Individual options: stove *smokeless* (chimney x0.3, +15 % wood), *dusk only* (chimney x0.2, -20 % wood, but a cold house on a cold night: morale up to -6 and a frostbite exposure of 0.3 even with wood in hand), *quiet hours* (21:00-05:00: night work at half rate, stock and party noise x0.6/x0.3), *dogs trained quiet* ($150 per dog once, the dog still warns the family, so no defense is lost), and a new household good *Wall insulation* ($320 a house: sound from inside x0.5, stove demand -10 %). **New give-aways** (after the hit, after dark): chimney plume (up to 4 points, scaled by the night's cold and the burn mode), stock (0.1 a head, cap 2) and dogs barking (0.6 a dog, cap 1.8), both added to the exposure sum and to `postureOf.visibility`; **heat** (stove, generator, glasshouse) is shown as a number but adds no exposure because nothing that raids sees heat yet. **Readout:** the Defense tab Light discipline panel now has a Stealth block listing light, smoke, noise and heat as numbers (`giveAwayReadout`), the options, and what each costs. **Lit response (confirmed and changed):** a sensor light stays dark until a PERSON is near (animals never light an AI-module light). Before, any lights out (manual, dusk) forced every floodlight off, even for a raid; now an AI-module sensor light stays armed under lights out and lights for a person, and full stealth makes every light that is not off act as a sensor light (an `on` light goes dark until a person comes). Two ways, both hidden: a *plant* (5 % of applicants, fixed by the seed and the guard id) or *bought since*: loyalty (0-100, starts 55) drifts toward a target set by the wage tier (below rate -25, agency rate 0, above rate +18; pay x0.8 / x1 / x1.25), tenure (up to +10) and family nearby (+8); an honest unvetted guard is bought at 0.014 %/day at loyalty 50 (less when loyal, more when not) plus up to 0.17 %/day as loyalty falls from 50 to 0 (softened 2026-11-05 on user request, was 63 % too high: measured over 300 seeds with plants excluded, a below-rate wage turns 28 % within a year and 42 % within two, was 63 % and 80 %; the agency rate 5 % and 7 %, was 8 % and 12 %; a high wage 3 % and 4 %, was 6 % and 10 %; vetted and taken-in loners stay at 0). **What a compromised guard does** (1 %/day): leaks the layout (40 %, the existing scout crew: `scoutCrew`), opens the gate for a crew (30 %, `ComeBack.insider` then `Encounter.insiderHelp`: the next raid is fought with `insideBreach`, i.e. no wall, moat or ring to cross; he drives off), or steals half the cash and half the rounds and drives off (30 %). Wall strength does not stop any of it. **Vetting:** a paid background check, $450 and 5 days per guard (a *Vet* button per guard, or *Vet new hires* at hiring), $300 and 4 days per joiner (button on the Newcomers panel). It finds a compromised man 9 times in 10 (flagged: the dismissal card); the tenth passes and, by the user's rule, a cleared man is loyal for good (the flag is cleared, no roll of any kind touches him again). Joiners: the existing hidden secrets (thief / scout) are the joiner insider; a cleared joiner's secret is forced to solid, and an unvetted joiner under loyalty 20 can be bought (0.5 %/day, then the scout path). **Loners taken in** (`guard_stay` -> `take_in`) are marked cleared at once: no betrayal roll, ever. **Discovery:** a compromised guard is noticed 2 %/day (+3 % with powered cameras or the intercom camera) and raises a dismissal card (*Let him go* / *Keep him*; kept: the card does not return for 30 days, the risk stays). Wage tier and *Vet new hires* live in the Defense tab Security staff section, with loyalty words and the vet state per guard. **Monotone:** nothing here is a defense and nothing touches a vetted guard, a taken-in loner or a lot with no guards; a lot with vetted (or honest unvetted) guards, and a lot where a plant was vetted out, is tested in `insider.test.ts` against the never-hired lot with crews of 6 and 10 (150 seeded raids each, 2-sigma rule like `monotone.test.ts`). The threat is deliberately the price of skipping the check. **Guesses:** all rates and costs are tunable in the one `INSIDER` table (no real data); a missed plant is cleared and stays loyal (the rule beats realism); the three-guard detail shares one wage tier; the pay cut applies at once. **Save:** every new field is optional and read with a default equal to the old behaviour (`loyalty ?? 55`, `wage ?? fair`, no `vet` = unvetted), so no version bump and no migration. |

It ends for good when banks and law have both been under 25 % for 7 days in a row, or lapses after two unpaid months; after the collapse nobody comes. **(2) Road watch** (`sim/watch.ts`): found it with two or more neighbors at home who rate the family at 10 or more ($150, then $20 a month while the banks work; no dues once they are shut, it keeps going: that is the point). It adds +3 warning always and +10 more when the next visitor is within 2 days (the chat lights up once), members within 3 km with disposition over 0 come when there is shooting (`pact.defenders`), members gain +1 disposition a month (cap 70), a household that dies or leaves drops out, under two it dissolves, leaving costs the leader 5 goodwill. **Not modeled, flagged:** the exclusion critique (a fortress look or private patrol cooling neighbor goodwill) because no existing mechanic ties the fortress look to neighbor disposition; a person-shift (the single `locals.duty` slot belongs to the militia and the pact work day), so the shift cost is the dues; alarm signs are one gate sign, not per-wall. **Monotone:** two new cases (a subscription after the collapse, and a watch) must not raise breach rate or defender deaths. **UI:** Defense > Armed response and the road watch. **Visual:** a sign plate on the gate post while the firm answers. **Save:** `GameState.security` is optional (absent = neither), old saves load unchanged; no bump, flagged for the coordinator to bump with a no-op migration if wanted. |
| 2026-11-05 | QA matrix 4 (broad QA pass on main; no save change) | Ran the sim playtest across all 16 disasters x 3 difficulties, a 40-year long game, browser kinds / save-load / sweep scenarios, the new shop / starvation / road-lead shot scripts. **Fixed:** (1) refeeding: hunger debt only drained 600 kcal/day when fully fed, so a family fed again after ~20 starving days still lost 1.5 health/day for months and died with a full pantry (Standard), or sat at health 5 for 100+ days (Cozy); a fed day now clears max(600, 8% of the debt) (`needs.ts`, test in `starvation.test.ts`); (2) (wipe over gate card: fixed on main by #208); (3) `spread()` let a flu catch someone whose flu had already turned to pneumonia, stacking four pneumonias on one person (`medical.ts`); (4) People panel said "Everyone is holding up" with the whole household dead (`PeoplePanel.tsx`); (5) peeling paint (`weathering.ts`) read as digital camouflage: flakes now lie along the boards and top out near a fifth of the wall. **Harness:** `playQa.mjs` disaster picker (the first `<select>` is now an Occupation menu), `QA_UNLOCK=1` for the pulpy disasters, `lotPick: 'farmhouse'`; new `playQa.kinds.mjs` and `playQa.wall.mjs`. **Left (see PR body):** Cozy chronic under-feeding never kills (hunger pins at the 400k clamp, health at 5); fostered-ward runs show no card for up to 17 years. |

| 2026-11-05 | Fortress reputation on the county road (user request, theme C; `sim/reputation.ts`, `sim/encounters.ts` `whoComes`, `sim/breakins.ts`, `sim/engine.ts`, `ui/hud/DefenseTab.tsx`; no save change) | **Derived, not stored:** a *hardness* score 0-1 from the look of the walls (`Posture.fortress`, weight 50 %), the word that this place shoots back (`gate.respect`, 25 %), guards at the gate (15 %, two guards = full) and warning signs (a live electric fence, 10 %). **Opportunists only:** looters come up to 35 % less often and peacetime burglaries up to 30 % less (`opportunistK`, `burglarK`, both never above 1); organized crews (gang, militia, requisition, patrol) are not touched by the name, so they plan around the wall (their own weight already rises with the fortress look). **Neighbors:** a fortress look (above 50 % of a fortress) cools neighbors who do not know you yet (disposition between -30 and 25, 0.04 a day at the full look, never below -30), but NEVER an ally (at 25 and above), so a wall can never cost a raid its help; a good-neighbor record (humanity above its starting 70, and people taken in against turned away) warms every home neighbor up to disposition 70 at up to 0.05 a day, 60 % slower behind a full fortress look, and a record of 0.3 or more outweighs the cooling. A default family (humanity 70, nobody taken in or turned away) has a record of 0, so nothing moves until the player's choices make a record. **Readout:** Defense tab, *Reputation on the county road*: the label (An open place / Closed up / Hard-looking / A fortress), the score, each driver as points, what the neighbors say, average goodwill, and the effects in words. **Monotone:** only removes opportunist visits and moves goodwill in small capped steps; changes nothing inside a fight; every fortress-looking element only raises hardness (`reputation.test.ts`, with the walls + word + year-of-drift lot fought against the same lot with crews of 6 and 10 and the ally count compared). **Guesses:** all numbers are tunable in the `REP` table; the ally line for cooling is 25 (below the lowest pact line, 30); hardness is not shown to the player as a promise (the road is a rumor), only as a readout. **Cost:** the posture pass is not on the hot path: burglary reputation is applied only on the rare day the break-in dice already say yes (same draw, so the dice stream is unchanged; `breakInRateNamed` is the UI/test rate), and neighbor drift runs monthly in 30-day steps, and only when a neighbor could move and a wall is laid. `breakins.test.ts` went 39.7 s to about the main time again. **Test change:** `roadLead.test.ts` "totals of each kind" now uses seeds [3, 5, 7] instead of [3, 5] (the bound `a > 20` is unchanged; two seeds sat at exactly 20 once the good-neighbor record shifted the neighbor dice). |

| 2026-11-05 | Light household bonds (user request, lean; `sim/bonds.ts`, hooks in `sim/mind.ts`, `sim/daily.ts`, `sim/people.ts`, `ui/PeoplePanel.tsx`, `ui/Hud.tsx` Years; **no save version bump**: optional `GameState.bonds`, made lazily, an old save has none and plays exactly as before) | **Model:** per pair `warm`, `strife`, `mentor` hours, a tag (friend / rival / mentor; couple is read from the family tree, not stored). **Inputs, all existing sim data:** hours two people spent on the same duty in the daily roster (grouped by hour and duty, groups capped at 12, one pass a day at midnight from `peopleDay`), shared danger from a fought battle (`peopleAfterFight`: held the line together, a `carry` or `aid` event, a defender hit while a child waited below), and the quarrels `mind.ts` already stages (`Rift`). **Chemistry is a hash of seed and the two ids** (-1..1): suiting pairs warm up, clashing pairs build strife (full rate only if one is strained, else x0.3). Blood kin and couples never become friends or rivals (the tree already says what they are); a parent or elder can still mentor. **Numbers in one table `BONDS`:** friend at warm 45 (kept to 25), rival at strife 40 (kept to 15), mentor at 10 shared mentoring hours, +3/day cap, decay 0.12 warm and 0.4 strife a day apart, 80 pairs kept, 6 chronicle lines a year. **Effects, small and one-way:** a friend or mentor in the house lifts strain 0.5 point each (cap 1.5) through `reading()`; the roster scores a candidate +5 % per friend or mentor already on that chore (-5 % per rival, capped at +-10 %) in the main assignment loop only, never for watch; a rival costs nothing. **Monotone for defense:** nothing in battle, posture, the gate or opsec reads a bond; the only path in is lower strain, so `fightNerves` can only get steadier (property test over seeds). **Surfaces:** People panel "Close to each other" (read-only, six rows), chronicle kind `bond` (✧) in Years, plus the log. **Guesses to tune:** family-only households mostly get mentors and couples (friends and rivals need joiners, guards or neighbors who moved in); a mentor bond from a parent teaching a child writes a chronicle line once; moments are plain templates (no per-person flavor). **Timings:** `bondsDay` 0.2 ms a day with 44 people (0.02 ms for a family); breakins / roadLead / gateLoop sim tests within run-to-run noise of main. **Deferred backlog (not built, per user):** escalating siege-wave clock, named antagonists, wants / aspirations, visible zones, damage scars, legacy carry-over, economy-of-scarcity pass, edge-of-world quests. |
| 2026-11-05 | Compound board (user request, lean; `sim/compoundBoard.ts`, `ui/CompoundBoard.tsx`, `scripts/boardShots.mjs`; no save change) | **One read-only page, a HUD "Board" button (bottom right) opens it** (Esc or Close puts it down; a jump closes it so the tab is in view). **Pure selector, no new sim:** rows Power / Water / Food days / Defense / Morale, then Threats, Waiting on you, Defense gaps (folded). Reuses `operational()` (now takes an optional shared `DefenseReport`, and exports `OP_COVERED`), `defenseReport`, `powerSummary`, `moodSummary`, `suggestions`, `needsHookup`, `holdReason`, the yearly threat, the disaster warning and the storm. A row opens its tab (`hudFocus`); the unmet checklist item's one fix sits on its row (Dig a well, Stake solar...); jumps are `open / select / stake / shop` acts only. **Cost:** worked out only while open, memoized by `boardCache` (day, structure count and finished count, people, a card at the gate, wall runs, disaster phase); about 5 ms a call uncached on a 25-building place (loaded machine), roughly once a day cached. **Defaults I chose:** lists capped at 3 gaps, 4 threats, 4 decisions (`BOARD`); food red under 7 days (amber under 30 unless something is growing); morale red under 40 or anyone breaking, amber under 55 or anyone struggling; water counted at 10 L per person a day; the shelter checklist item lives under Waiting on you (it has no row). No 3D clipboard prop in the safe room (the HUD button only). The selector reads only: it uses `s.weather?.active`, not the lazy-creating `weatherOf`; the first call may create the empty lazy defaults the sim makes anyway (`medical`, `courses`, `learning`). **Deferred backlog (not built, per user):** escalating siege-wave clock, named antagonists, wants / aspirations, visible zones, damage scars, legacy carry-over, economy-of-scarcity pass, edge-of-world quests. |
| 2026-11-05 | Gate kit follows the drive (user bug: the double gate / sally port from #209 stood crooked beside the curving road; visual only, no save change, no sim change; `game/perimeter/gateKitLayout.ts` pure + `gateKitLayout.test.ts`, `scripts/gateAlignShots.mjs`) | **Root cause:** the kit was laid out in the WALL's frame (side walls along the wall normal at the gate piers, inner gate parallel to the wall, pole / cage / sign offsets along wall direction), on every vehicle gate of every run. The drive crosses a wall at any angle and bends, often right at the property line, so the port stood skewed or sideways and the road ran through its walls. **Fix:** the kit is derived from the drive's own center line (heading = chord of 0.5 either side of the crossing, the same frame the road ribbon uses): the two side walls are the center line offset +-(0.3 + half a wall) for 1.15 of drive, so they bend with the road, start flush on the real wall (a wing closes any gap to the pier), the inner frame is square to the road and centered, open inner leaves lie against the walls; shortened (min 1.0) where the road hugs the wall, none under a 30 degree slant (`roadGate` cuts gates only down to sin 0.5); walls sunk into rolling ground. Intercom pole (driver's side, camera and plate face the approach), response sign (square to the approach), motor cage are placed from the real wall along the road, on the right side of it, off the road. **Kit only on the first way in** (the first finished gate the drive meets; a gatehouse garage is that gate and takes none), not on every gate. **Audit, fixed:** outer gate leaves of any drive gate swing open along the drive on the road side (were 75 deg off the wall: across the road on slanted crossings); the ranch gate on the property line is square to the drive and hidden inside a wall's own gate; the guard house frame sits inside the entry wall (clear of a slanted wall and of the sally port) on the drive; guard cars no longer overlap each other (0.5 apart, 0.8 long) and stand on the ground under them; the guard house footing is sunk (floated on slopes) and its two bollards stand outside the lane (they left 0.2 for a 0.34 pickup); camera lens added facing the road. **Left:** a road within 30 degrees of a ring facet gets no kit (and in about 1 lot in 100 a ring's own gate beside such a crossing leaves it ungated: `cutRoadGates` will not stack two gates); covered bridge and vehicle yard are drawn from their own run end points and meet the drive through the generic gate rule, unchanged. Test: regions x lot kinds x seeds x 3 wall layouts, heading within 3 deg of the road, side walls a constant offset from it, flush, centered, clear width for the motorhome plus margin, nothing on the road, pole / sign outside and cage inside the wall. |

| 2026-11-05 | Gate follow-ups: every road crossing gets a gate, guard cars park on level ground, covered bridge leaves open, fly / walk cameras stop at walls (visual / geometry; no save version bump: no saved field changes) | **Gates (`sim/roadGate`).** (1) A very slanted crossing (sin < 0.5) that fell beside a gate the wall already had was skipped because two gates cannot stack; the new gate now takes over the old gate's span (union, up to 2 units wide; a wider union replaces the old gate), so the road is always gated. (2) A closed ring cannot carry a gate across its seam (first = last point), so a road that crosses within half a gate of the seam was left ungated; `cutRoadGates` first turns the ring's start to the vertex farthest from the crossing that is clear of every gate (points rotated, gate `at` re-measured; same wall, same length). `gateKitLayout.test.ts` now demands zero ungated crossings over 330 generated lots (it allowed 3%) and the sally port is built down to sin 0.2 (12 degrees; `KIT.minSin`): the later side wall starts further along the road, so the port is stretched (`KIT.minLeg` 1.0 after the later wall's start) and the guard house frame moves in to clear it. **Guard cars (`HomesteadWorld._guardSlots`).** The cars were rotated with their length across the lane (a vehicle's front is +z), overlapping the lane and the barrier's catch post; they now stand along the drive, nose out. Each car takes a slot on ground that is level under it (5 cm), clear of walls, retaining walls, trees, buildings, water, the lane and the house, searching up to 19 units in; with no level place the lowest slope under 0.3 is used and the car is tilted to it. In a road cut between steep banks the cars therefore park further in, not on the bank. **Covered bridge.** The gate leaves were drawn as flat boards across the portal (the bridge read as shut); they now lie open along the inside of each truss. **Cameras.** The walker could walk through a retaining wall (`walkBlocked` ignored them) and the drone through any wall: `walkBlocked` now includes `walls.blocks`, and `TourController` takes an optional `solidAt` (`HomesteadWorld.tourSolid`: standing stone / container walls below their top, retaining walls) and slides along it. **Not done, and why.** Gate leaves stay static: the leaves are part of each run's merged mesh (one draw call per material); animating them means a separate mesh and material per gate (+2 draw calls at the entry) or a vertex shader on the merged mesh, and the perf budget (383/387/388) may not rise. **QA.** `scripts/gateAlignShots.mjs` gains guard-car, guard-house close-up, moat + covered-bridge and vehicle-yard scenarios; every eye camera is tested against the scene (rays with back faces counted, a grid of rays through the screen, retaining walls and rising banks) and moved until it is clear, and a shot with no clear camera is named `-INSIDE`. |
| 2026-11-05 | Gate realism 3 (review of #222: yard, bridge on a slope, moat water, road-cut facing, guard cars) | **Yard.** `buildkit/yardSite.ts` (pure, tested): a vehicle yard within `snapR` of the drive snaps beside it, turned to the drive's heading (a fractional quarter turn in `Structure.rot`; `rotQ` and `placementProblem` handle it, no save change: rot was already a number), gate toward the road, set back from its edge (nearest setback whose ground takes it, sliding along the drive, R flips side); a gravel spur is laid draped on the ground from the gate to the road's edge (`BuildOpts.spur/spurH`). The pad is the box round the turned yard with a 0.2 skirt so grading never reaches the road bed. A site above a road cut (grade, retaining wall, bank) is refused. **Bridge.** The deck pitches between the ground at its two ends (grade <= 12 %, `perimeter/bridgeDeck.ts`); abutments stand to the highest ground across their width (no pit, no gap). **Moat.** Water is level: `perimeter/moatLevel.ts` splits the run into flat pools (depth >= 25 % of the lift everywhere) with a short water step between them; edges stop where the bank does. **Road-cut facing.** `bankStrip` smooths the crest line along the road and lies on the highest ground within a terrain cell. **Guard cars** follow the drive's own offset line (`perimeter/guardCarSlots.ts`). **Still wrong:** moat on a steep hillside reads as a ragged thin ribbon (no berm on the downhill side); the road-cut facing folds on the inside of a tight bend; a yard on the mesa road-cut lot is placed beside the cut with the spur meeting the bank; gate leaves still static (budget). |
| 2026-11-06 | Gate realism 4 (review of #223: moat channel on a hillside, road-cut facing on a bend, yard on a road-cut lot, shot helpers; no save version bump: no saved field changes) | **Moat.** A ditch cut a fixed depth under the ground it crosses has no bank on the downhill side of a hillside, so the water ribbon was clipped to a thin ragged strip. `perimeter/moatChannel.ts` (pure, tested) now grades a channel the way a crew would dig it: the bed of each pool is flat and level across (a pool spans at most 0.16 u of natural fall, so the high end is cut deeper), the uphill side is cut back (1 : 2.4 wet batter, steeper face above the crest), the downhill side is built up with the spoil to a level crest 0.1 u over the full water, and the crest falls away to the hill; pools drop with a rounded step. The moat is about 13 m across at the water and 3 m deep (the old one was about 5 m: narrower than 2 cells of the terrain mesh, which is why it aliased). `ParcelTerrain.sample` applies it (`Earthwork.channel`, `moatHeight` from the ground the road bed and pads leave), `HomesteadWorld` plans one channel per moat run from the natural ground, and the water builder takes its levels from the same pools (a short steep ramp of the surface at a drop, not a vertical sheet; edges are drawn a hair wide and the bank hides the excess, so the visible waterline is the surface meeting the bank, not per-station clipping). **Road-cut facing.** `roadWorks.insideRoom` (pure, tested) limits how far the stone facing reaches on the inside of a bend (offset line radius R - offset must stay positive; turn rate over several windows so a one-station kink pinches too) so the facing follows the bend as one band instead of folding over itself. **Yard.** `yardSite.spurProfileOk` (pure, tested): the ground from the gate to the road's edge must lie within 0.1 u (plus 5 % of the drop) of a straight slope on three lines across the spur, else the site is refused (a road cut's bank or a bench edge beside or across the way); the placement tool tries the next setback or slide, and refuses cleanly with the usual "cannot build here" when none fits. **QA.** `scripts/gateAlignShots.mjs` yard views take the road's real distance from the placed yard (`b.road`) instead of assuming 1.58 u, the moat bank-side shot stands on the bank instead of in the water. **Still wrong:** see the PR (waterline is the terrain mesh's contour, so on a steep cross-slope at Medium quality it still shows a ~0.3 u sawtooth; gate leaves still static (budget)). |
| 2026-11-06 | Gate realism 5 (mesa yard spur ledge, bend facing sliver; no save version bump: no saved field changes) | **Yard.** `buildkit/yardSite.spurReachesRoad` (pure, tested incl. a mesa-style road end in a shallow cut) checks the spur the way the builder lays it (highest ground within a terrain cell round each point): the whole far end of the spur lies on the roadway (not beyond the end of the drive), is level with the road within 0.1 u, and every row is within a tolerance of the straight line from the pad's level to the road's; `PlacementTool._spurOk` calls it. A cursor within `snapR` of the drive that finds no good site is now refused (`_yardRefused`, ghost red) with `NO_LEVEL_SITE` ("No level site beside the drive here...") instead of falling back to a free placement whose spur stops at a ledge. The mesa yard was beside the last stretch of the drive, where the road ends: the old checks sampled plain ground and passed. **Facing.** `roadWorks.facingReach` (pure, tested): a bank facing narrower than 45 % of the bank's full reach (never under 0.12 u) is not drawn; where a bend pinches the bank the room limit left long runs a few tenths wide, which drew as thin diagonal strips up the slope. It only ever lowers a reach, so the fold cannot return. Not done: the moat waterline sawtooth (optional; no safe change without more geometry). No perf budget change (383/387/388). |
| 2026-11-06 | Escalating siege-wave clock (user-approved from the Deferred backlog: "make the call" on what fits the spirit; `sim/waves.ts`, `sim/waveStatus.ts`, `ui/WaveClock.tsx`, hooks in `encounters.gateDay`, `DIFFICULTY.wave / lull`, `scripts/waveShots.mjs`; **save v82**: optional `GateState.waves`, a run from before has none and plays as before, the v81 step deletes it from very old migrated saves) | **How it plays:** once the collapse hits, hostile crews (looters, gang, militia, patrol, requisition, horde, whichever the disaster sends) come in numbered WAVES on a schedule from the seed, not at random. The first is about 2 weeks after the hit, then a lull of about 7 weeks that shortens by up to 30% as waves climb; wave 10 is the peak and later ones repeat it. **Warning signs** the 3 days before (smoke on the horizon; radio chatter, or restless dogs and an unlit vehicle without a radio; an empty county road) and travelers (arrivals, refugees, traders) stay away in those days. **A wave is an ordinary raid:** same arrival hour, Watch cutscene, siege scenes, strain, battle and aftermath. The clock only decides WHEN and HOW MUCH: crew heads x(1 + 0.7 x wave weight x climb) x(1 + 0.15 x defensive strength), and the kit (`extraKit` via `adapt`) rises with the climb. **Random hostile visits are held off while the clock runs** (waves replace them; a crew coming back to a place it stripped still can, arrivals and traders unchanged outside the 3 warning days); the Hardcore adaptation/probe of the same crew is skipped for a wave (the wave's own growth stands in). Waves stop when the law track is back at 0.75. **Defense:** `defensiveStrength` (shared with adaptation) gives at most +15% heads and half the kit, small beside what a wall, towers and rifles take off the odds; the property test (`waves.test.ts`) fights the real wave crew (crew growth included) against lots with 1 / 3 towers and a stone wall and tower, waves 2, 6, 10, crews of 6 and 10: breach rate and defenders killed are never worse beyond noise (in the runs: breach 72% to 22%, 87% to 62%). **HUD:** a chip in the top bar ("Wave 3 in 12 days" + a ten-step ladder, colored by nearness, click opens the Board), and a Raid waves card at the top of the Compound board with the one line "Raiders now come in waves. Each wave is bigger and better armed than the last. Between waves it is quiet: repair, rotate guards, stock up." plus what to expect ("a sizable crew, larger than the last") from today's defense. The date is exact, the crew is a word. **Difficulty (DIFFICULTY table):** `wave` weight 0.35 / 1 / 1.4 and `lull` x1.5 / 1 / x0.75 (Cozy: fewer, smaller, weeks apart; Hardcore: faster climb, short quiet; summary lines on the setup screen). **Constants in one table `WAVE`** (all guesses to tune): FIRST 14 days (+-35%), LULL 52 days (+-25%, min 18), LULL_SHRINK 0.3, PEAK 10, WARN 3, HEADS_ESC 0.7, HEADS_DEF 0.15, KIT 0.7, LAW_END 0.75, HEADS_CAP 2.1. **Measured (bot, `scripts/playtest.test.ts`, 3 kinds x homestead / prepper / fortress, 3 seed offsets = 27 runs a cell, `YEARS=6` so the runs play 3 years past the hit; main vs this branch):** people lost overall Cozy 20% vs 20%, Standard 67% vs 67%, Hardcore 78% vs 80% (the bots die mostly of hunger and the disaster); fights fought 92 / 62 / 54 on main vs 54 / 29 / 18 here (waves are fewer than random hostile visits); defenders killed or taken in fights 6 / 25 / 26 on main vs 4 / 34 / 24 here, so Standard fight deaths rose about a third on a small sample (noise is about +-8), Cozy and Hardcore did not. No absurd deaths; it is a harder, more legible Standard, not a different game. **Guesses to tune:** the 7-week lull and peak at wave 10 (a three-year run sees about 15 waves, so most of it is at peak); whether Standard should climb slower (WEIGHT 0.8); the HEADS_DEF share. **Not done:** the chip does not show the kind of crew, wave tallies are not in the report card, no campaign-long escalation beyond the peak (waves repeat it). No perf budget change (383/387/388). |
| 2026-11-06 | Gate polish 6 (mesa yard spur apron, black road-cut facing, bend facing band; no save version bump: no saved field changes) | **Facing was black.** `roadWorks.facingWindingUp` (pure, tested): a bank strip's winding faced up only on the left of the road (the strip's handedness flips with the side), so right-hand facings were lit from below and read as black stone (the black cut beside the mesa yard spur, and the right bank of the bend at the gate). The winding now follows the side. **Bend band.** The thin diagonal band at the bend was a facing sheet floating over the bank: it was laid on the highest ground 0.18 u across the bank, which on a steep cut is up to a metre above the terrain mesh; the reach is now 0.09 u (the band is a hairline). `roadWorks.facingReaches` (pure, tested) also drops runs of facing shorter than 4 stations or never reaching 60 % of the bank's full reach, and tapers each kept run over 3 stations at each end, so a band never starts square; it only lowers a reach. A few short facings that were drawn before are now plain bank. **Yard spur.** `vehicleYardGen.spurGeometry` (exported, tested): the apron's two outer columns tuck 2 cm into the ground at the edge (was 4 cm proud with a visible gravel skirt), a shoulder column eases the edge down, the crown is draped 2 cm over the highest ground a terrain cell round it, and the far end lies 6 mm over the road's own level; it reads as a graded driveway into the cut, not a slab on top of it. Same mesh and draw calls as before (8 x 12 grid instead of 6 x 12; still one merged piece). **Not done.** Moat waterline sawtooth at Medium: the waterline is the terrain mesh's own contour on a steep cross-slope; a fix needs a finer terrain mesh along the moat or a second water mesh (more triangles / draw calls), so left. Gate leaf animation: the leaves are part of the merged static kit; a moving leaf needs its own mesh per gate (a draw call over the 383/387/388 budget), so skipped. No perf budget change. |

Invariants: no wait over 21 days for materials when cash covers them and an order is possible; a buyable wait past 4 days has a stuck card; nobody still outdoors at day 200+ while the crew and the order are affordable for two weeks and no crew is on the job. 321 starts: **18 failed before** (15 waits, 3 shelter), **0 failed after** (and 0 of 300 more fresh seeds). Root cause: on a lot with no drive the truck left a whole order as one lump at the road (the family carries it, 180+ hours for a first order, a weekender 2 days in 7), the lump counted as "on order" so no order or stuck card was ever offered, concrete / a drilling rig waited behind two hundred hours of lumber, and a hired crew would not carry. **Fix:** each good is its own load, goods that need no carrying are delivered to the stock at once, the load a waiting project needs is carried first, hired crews carry the pile on their hours (billed), and a Next-steps card "Put in a gravel drive" (new `hookup` card action) appears when 24+ hours wait at the road. Never above 90%, never below 8%. A failure is a refusal (the normal fight follows) or a trap (the crew is already inside the wall when the fight starts, the same `insideBreach` a forced hatch uses); a toll or a meal is still lost in a trap. A speaker never repeats their last line; no line is used twice in a cutscene until its pool is spent. `voiceCutscene` runs once over all scenes in order (so scene 4 knows what scene 1 said): a line the sim wrote stays if it is new and fits the moment and the crew; otherwise it is replaced, and quiet stretches (a crew creeping up, the first shots, a man down, the break) get a line. "Retreat" starts when 40% of the crew has run, not when one man runs dry. Scouts do not shout an assault, families do not "hit the house", and no retreat line can land before the first shot or the break (the test scans whole simulated cutscenes: no back-to-back repeats, under 3% repeats overall, no retreat in an approach). Parleys and break-ins keep their written lines. **2 The death.** The victim is the first named defender the sim really killed (the `death` event AND the person is dead in the staged game: a Cozy "badly wounded" or someone taken is not shown). The camera finds the shooter, the playback slows to 30% for about 1.7 s of fight time, the round arrives, the figure staggers back a step, leans and goes down; the nearest defender runs to them and kneels, the next drops to a knee. **Blood (graphic, the default):** red mist at the chest, droplets on the ground behind (and a spatter on the wall if one is behind), a dark wound patch on the figure where it was hit, a shirt (and trousers, for a leg) stained by how bad the wound is, a pool that spreads for a few seconds and stops, a small pool under a wounded raider, and a pale bandage with blood coming through on the injured once they are tended. No dismemberment; the camera cuts away after a few seconds and never circles it; the alarm light goes out when the shooting stops. **The wake** plays in the tail of that scene (the tail is longer in playback; the game clock and the siege's length are untouched): the family comes out of the house (children included, if any are alive) and gathers round on one knee; the closest of kin (partner, then parent or child, then sibling) speaks first, by relation (a partner, a parent of a child, a child, a guard, an elder); caption and lines use the name and no pronouns; the injured are bandaged where they sit and the worse hurt is helped inside; two carry the fallen to the house. **The raider left behind:** only if the sim left one alive (`leftBehindRaiders`, the report's wounded count): he is laid on the ground, two armed defenders walk up, one speaks, he answers by temper (desperate begs, hard dares them, savage spits), and the caption says what happens to him is the family's call; the card that follows is the existing aftermath choice. No such raider, no such scene. **3 Tracers.** The renderer drew every tracer chest to chest through walls. `lineOfFire` (pure, tested) checks the segment against the solid walls (log, container, berm, steel, concrete, stone) and the buildings: a round crosses only if the line clears the top (a tower, a wall walk, a roof) or the shooter or target stands against it (a loophole, a window). Otherwise the streak stops at the face with dust and a spark. Chain-link, wire, pickets and a thorn hedge do not stop a round. In `planBattle`: a blocked shot is not fired (no ammo spent); defenders with no line to anyone in reach move to a post that has one (`seekLine`: tower, deck, upper window, a wall post when the crew is on the wall), raiders sidestep along the wall or close in (`crewSeek`); posts are ranked by whether they can see where the crew comes from (a post with no view drops 3 points); wall posts are snapped to just inside the real wall; raiders reaching the line climb onto it (y = wall height, a `move` event with `y`); farmhouse-type homes get two upper-floor windows (y 0.85); every rifle that bears on the climbers slows them (was: only wall and tower posts). The surrendered are held, not left hurt: `leftBehind.surrendered`, `Battle.surrendered {raiders, defenders}`, `Aftermath.surrendered`; the GateCard says "N of them gave up" and the four choices read for men on their knees (release / hold / send off with nothing / finish, which costs 6 humanity a head instead of 4); the report shows "surrendered" and "Held at gunpoint"; the cutscene's ending draws exactly the sim's hands-up men and lays the rest down (no cosmetic re-roll). **Measured** (240 games, harness before/after, same seeds): wipe Standard 45% -> 46% (careful 19 -> 25, walled 31 -> 31, average 31 -> 31, within 1-2 games of 16), Hardcore 69% -> 66% (walled 81 -> 69), Cozy 15% -> 15%; Standard wave-1 win 87% -> 75% (n 15), waves 2-3 85/75 -> 93/83; people lost per Standard wave-1 fight 0.07 -> 0.13. Probe (60 fights each, 6 raiders): defender hits through a wall at ground level 119 of 119 -> 2 of 80, raider hits 55 of 55 -> 5 of 66 (the rest is replay timing). A farmhouse behind a container wall with no tower is worse than before (held 85 -> 70%, lost 0.43 -> 0.62 a fight): its defenders must stand in upper windows, exposed at long range. With a tower 80 -> 85%. **No number changed.** **Questions:** (a) a wall alone is no longer a kill zone (see the firing step below). (b) disarmed defenders keep their guns after being held (no loss mechanic). (c) the renderer has hands-up but no dropped-weapon prop. **Firing step (follow-up, coordinator's call):** `FIRING_STEP` (`sim/lineOfFire.ts`) is a per-wall-type property: log palisade, stone and container walls (brick does not exist as a wall type here; steel and concrete are plain faces) come with a free firing step. Defenders at a wall post stand on it (`stepHeight`: the muzzle clears the top by 0.05), sandbags are laid on it, a ground raider outside can hit them only over the top (partial cover). The Loopholes / firing ports hardening item is NOT built: `houseHardening.ts` only has doors / gates / windows / safe layers, and a priced item needs a design pass (follow-up). Measured (150 fights, 6 raiders, farmhouse behind a container wall): held 85% before the whole change, 72% with the step (open ground 69%), lost per fight 0.39 to 0.50; with a tower 81% to 73%. So the wall is roughly even with open ground, not back to the old 85%, because the old number came from shooting through the wall both ways; hits through a wall are 8 of 279 defender hits and 1 of 133 raider hits. Harness unchanged (same 240-game table as above). |
