Property test: every counter only ever lowers the odds. **2 The break point is the drawn wall.** `wallTarget` finds the real run segment (or gate) the approach crosses; `lineR` is now that distance, not the farthest corner capped at 30; the `breach` event carries `method` and `where` (run id, kind, gate, distance along, width, heading, outward vector), the sim emits the `ram`, `charge`, `explosion` and `gap` events itself (a failed ram says why and where it stopped), and `decorateBreach` only adds voices and camera meta on top. Hiding families are found or not by the seconds the crew had at the door against `doorSeconds` (6 plus 1.1 per barrier point, plus 28 barricaded, plus 90 safe room, 600 hardened; a merciful crew does not stay to break a door). Each defender answers a surrender for himself (`snapOdds`: grief, strain, trauma, a kinsman fallen beside him, minus calm, service and the negotiator); a man who shoots a surrendered raider takes a mind shock and the family loses 4 humanity. **5 Outcomes.** Hostage events on a captive (rescue.ts keeps the record and the ways back), arson (`raidFire.ts`: a state on the place, grows 0.17-0.3 an hour, jumps to a wooden neighbor, put out by able hands and water, an all-hands order from Next steps), itemized loot (`planLoot`: what the crew wants, reaches, carries), a mid-fight parley (tribute 15 percent of food and fuel, or passage) offered by a hurt but unbroken disciplined crew and accepted by how the family is doing, raiders who carry their hurt out, a lieutenant who may rally the crew when the leader falls, greed (men stop to loot the yard). **6 Orders** (`raidOrders.ts`): hold (the old fight, byte for byte), fall back (wall, yard cover, house: 3 s pause, better cover, what stands beyond the line is exposed), counterattack (shoots 30 percent better, is hit 12 percent more, the crew breaks sooner). Balance (40 raids per cell, farmhouse holdouts, before to after): held 33 to 31 percent Standard, 49 to 50 Cozy, 23 to 18 Hardcore; deaths per raid 1.14 to 1.38 Standard (hostile crews now kill what they find). |

## Environment visuals quick wins (cliff, storm aerial, overlays, night)

- **Cliff / rock face**: the regional terrain shader's rock branch gets near-camera strata (warped bands that pinch out, warm and cool tints), water streaks, lichen on ledges and a fine strata / grain normal. Same material, same draw calls; the shader cache key is bumped (`regional_terrain_v6_`).
- **Storm aerial**: `fog/aerialFog.ts` `weatherFogAerialScale(h)`: the weather terms of the distance fog, the fog-bank sheets and (in rain or snow) the valley bank and low cloud thin from full at eye level (below 4 units) to 18% past about 26 units. A fog-only day is left as it was.
- **Overlays**: the Sun overlay is one slate-to-amber ramp at low opacity; the Water overlay is a layered blue family with a pale lilac frost wash (no white stripes); soft modes are box-smoothed (`smoothColors`). The View-from sight overlay shows a "Click a point" hint (`sightPicked` in the world store, not saved) until a spot is picked.
- **Night**: ambient sky 0.30 to 0.40, fill 0.08 to 0.12, moon 0.62 to 0.78, ground bounce and environment floor lifted. Still clearly night; lit windows and lamps keep their contrast.

| 2026-11-12 | Wall quality and hide vs defend (owner: "wall quality must matter a lot more in raids"; "defending viable, hiding no longer strictly dominant"; playtest evidence in the PR; **no save change**, sim numbers only) | **Walls.** `sim/perimeter.ts` `WALL_HP` (a split-rail fence 10, log palisade 70, wrought iron 90, container wall 140, stone 200, steel 210, poured concrete 250). Read in four places: the posture's wall strength is the people-block times `holdScale` (0.3 to 1), so a ring of palisade reads about 30 % and concrete 97 % (it was 85 vs 97, saturated); the battle's climb rate is times `climbRateK` and the first climber needs `climbHoldS` more seconds; stone, steel and concrete (`needsMeans`, 190 hp up) are almost never climbed, so a crew needs a truck or a charge (`breachMeans.WALL_RESIST` lowered for them) or it gives up at the wall; a defender's wall post has better cover behind a heavier wall (`wallPostCover`, 0.25 down to 0.16). A crew that gives up at an unbroken wall now ends the raid **repelled** (it was always "fled"), and a repelled raid earns extra standing in the county (`REPELLED_RESPECT`). Log palisade and thorn hedge stop people at 0.6 (was 0.85 and 0.9; a hedge also grows over 4 years), and sharpened logs let some rifle rounds through (`LEAKY_WALLS`, a partial hit). **Towers.** Only the best two tower cabs fight at full strength (`TOWER_POSTS_CAP`); mounted guns and turrets always count; the detection bonus was already capped. **Militia** has its own battle parameters (`MILITIA`: more rifles, soft armor, +1 skill, steadier). **Hiding** costs more on Cozy and Standard: +2 points of find chance per wave that has come (to wave 10), +10 per time a crew already found the place hiding in the last 120 days (to 3, stacking with the +20 for a crew that came back; read from the gate history), and a found hide strips 15 % + 2 % a man (to 45 %) of the stores, not 8 % + 1.5 % (to 30 %). **Hardcore keeps the old hiding costs** (`HIDE_COST_K`, `STRIP_HARDCORE`): its best hiding bots survive about a third of runs and must stay there. Crews also grow faster against a fortified place (`WAVE.HEADS_DEF` 0.15 to 0.3). Tests: `sim/wallBalance.test.ts` pins the tier order (barrier, battle outcomes), repelled, towers, militia and the hide numbers; `barrier`, `enclosure`, `propertyWall`, `perimeter` and `adaptive` tests moved to the new scale (a palisade is no longer the "strong wall" in their fixtures). |

| 2026-11-12 | Cutscene router: no unit left without a route (**no save change**, sim/cutscene only) | A 108-scene sweep (3 walls x night gang / day raid / invasion x seeds 1-12) left a raider unrouted in 5 scenes. Two root causes in `sim/cutNav.ts`: (1) the failure memory keyed on the set of open doors, which is identical before a door first shuts and after it reopens, so a leg that failed because the door would be shut on arrival was refused again when retried for the reopening; the key now includes the next door/breach change; (2) the router retires a search box that rarely succeeds in a scene, but on a short leg inside the house only the finest box can resolve a 0.07 wide interior doorway; the smallest box is kept for legs under 4 units. Routes of legs that already worked are unchanged; `boxWorld` (tests/tools) got a bucket index (about 25 percent faster scene prep, no route change). Seed 3 restored in `deadSilent` and `peopleVoices` tests. Sweep: 5 of 108 failing before, 0 after. Not done: a door-aware hierarchical router; slow scenes are still 4 to 6 s (about 6M fine-grid cell expansions). |
| 2026-11-12 | Road-pile stall, permit-wait advice, goal card queue (early playtest; **no save change**: one optional field `stalls[].seen`) | **Cause of the 30-day "Waiting for 9 fieldstone" with $695k:** with no drive the truck leaves each good at the road as one pile, and a pile reached the yard only when the whole of it was carried (37 fieldstone = 74 person-hours; the family has about 4 to 6 h a day), while the stuck card stayed silent because the pile counted as "on order". Fixes: (1) `economy.releaseCarried`: a single-good pile is released a whole unit at a time as it is carried; (2) the family and a crew carry first only while a project waits on something at the road (or nothing else can be built), so spare lumber no longer holds up the outhouse; (3) `blockedReason` says "(at the road: the family is carrying it in by hand)", the Projects note no longer says "order first" for it, and the `road_pile` card appears as soon as a project waits on road goods (`stalls.roadWait`) with the drive contractor one tap away; (4) stall marks survive a one-day gap (`seen`), because a unit arriving and being used by a neighbor is the same wait. Regression: `roadPile.test.ts` plus the existing fuzz seeds. **Permit waits:** rules, fees and review days untouched; a "permit_wait" Next-steps card (`sim/permitWait.ts`) names the wait and says what to do with the days: work on staked permit-free pieces or stake a privy and garden, order the plans' materials, buy tents for anyone with no bed. **Goal cards:** the watcher itself fires the day a goal is done (tested with a 10-day step); the lateness came from the card queue (12 s per card at fast speeds, behind other overlays), so waiting goal cards merge into one, wait 4.5 s each, and a card shown 2 or more days late says "done N days ago". |

Real anatomy in metres (Holstein about 1.4 m at the withers, ewe 0.78, hog 0.8, hen 0.34, rooster 0.5), scaled by 1/6 at the instance (SCALE v2: a cow is 0.84 of a person's height at the withers, tested). 2 to 4 breeds per model (`animalMaterial.ts` `Breed`): coat patches, face, socks, belts, bars and spots are painted in the fragment shader from 3-D noise in the rest pose (no textures, no HDRIs), plus a fur / wool / feather bump that fades out with the pixel footprint. **Animation costs no CPU geometry work**: the vertex shader poses a 26-bone skeleton (shoulder / hip, elbow / stifle, knee / hock, fetlock) from four per-instance numbers: lateral-sequence walk with knee and hock flex, stride tied to distance travelled so feet do not slide, hop for the rabbit, head bob in the walk, grazing (neck and head down, muzzle on the grass, tested with `animalPose.ts`, the shader's maths in TS), pecking (body tips about the feet), tail swish, ear twitch. **Cost:** one `InstancedMesh` per (model, LOD) that is in use, hidden (not counted by the census) when empty; LOD by camera distance and tier (High 4 400 / 2 500 / 840 tris for a cow near / mid / far, Medium starts at the mid level, Low at the mid / far levels; triangle ceilings per level are tested); one transparent instanced contact shadow for all animals; still no shadow-map casters and no SSAO. The first hen of a coop is drawn as its rooster. `spreadGoals` / separation spacing follows the real body length (cow 0.4); animals queueing at a door no longer shove each other (a raid test caught it). QA: `scripts/animalSheet.mjs` + `animalviewer.{html,js}` (turntable contact sheet per breed, four angles, walk phases, graze, LOD) and `scripts/livestockShots.mjs` (person beside a cow, herd, pig and sheep at eye level, pasture from above, yard at dawn / noon / dusk / night, coop run). |

| 2026-11-13 | Roof before and after a blizzard (strategy playtest seed 5; **no save change**, optional fields `Structure.roofLostAt` and `Structure.kept` only) | **Evidence:** north woods, full-time family in a listing farmhouse with an unrepaired roof. A blizzard on day 238 dropped the roof (`weatherThreats.ts`), nobody built it back (the bots never act on cards), the family sat in tents and the stove-less shop, and all 12 bots were dead by day 286 holding $140k or more. **Two causes found in the sim:** (1) a knocked-back farmhouse had to redo its roof stage (400 person-hours) and then its whole finish stage (1,600 hours): about 2,000 hours, a crew job of two months, while everyone was hypothermic and so "unwell" and off the work roster; (2) nothing asked for the roof before the snow. **Fixes (`sim/roofSafety.ts`):** (a) a `roof_winter` Next-steps card ("this roof will not take a snowload; fix it before winter") shows from 120 days before the blizzard season opens, rises to urgency 90 inside 60 days and 96 in season, and its first button is order the roofing and hire a crew in one tap (the existing `crew` act with `lines`). (b) A snow-load collapse takes the roof, not the rooms (`Structure.kept`): putting a roof back finishes the home again, so the job is the 400-hour roof stage only (the finish stage's refunded materials are taken back out of the yard). (c) The `reroof` card's first button is now order + hire in one tap. (d) A day after a home loses its roof, if no one is under a finished roof and no crew is on it, the household orders what is missing and hires the crew itself (`roofEmergencyDay`, log line says so); cash, delivery and `crewProblem` rules are unchanged, and with no money or no crews nothing happens. **Result, seed 5, the 13 strategy bots, run to 400 days after the hit:** before, every family dead by day 286 (cold); after, all 13 live through the first winter (the baby reaches chill 71 and recovers) and 11 are alive at the end; the other two (`sprawl`, `economy`) die after the hit, with the farmhouse burned by the wildfire, a different problem. Tests: `roofSafety.test.ts` includes real-sim seed-5 runs (a bot that presses the cards, a bot that takes the top card the day the roof falls, a bot that presses nothing). `passiveBot` now sends a crew card's `lines` as the HUD does. |

| 2026-11-13 | Crews build walls; money buys speed; wall-first card; crews after the hit; independent start cash restored (strategy playtest; owner decisions A, B, C and the wealth principle; **no save change**: no new state fields, `Crew.target` may now hold a run id) | **Owner principle (recorded):** money solves everything: things can be built quickly with a lot of money and manpower, an advantage for wealthy families, while a poor family still takes much longer by hand. Wages and materials stay realistic, so it is expensive, not free. **Evidence:** `pickWork` puts buildings ahead of perimeter runs, so walls/fortress bots left a 100-unit stone ring (about 3,800 h) at 0 for 800 days, and crews could only target structures, so cash could not buy it; crews also quit the moment any of banks/law < 50% or fuel < 30%. **A) Crews take runs.** `hireCrew(id)` accepts a run id (wall, fence, hedge; not bores or spans). Same rules as buildings: four people, $60 an hour a head, weekdays 8 to 5, billed only for the hours worked (`addRunWork(.., crew=true)` returns the hours used; a crew brings its own tools, so no family tool wear), materials taken from the yard when work starts, a crew waits unbilled for stone or a county permit and carries a road pile in. **No cap but the physical one:** several crews work one run side by side, up to one per 80 ft of wall and none with under a week of work (`maxRunCrews`), and any number of runs and buildings in parallel; a family with cash is limited by the wage bill only. The Idle-cash card no longer stops at 2 or 3 crews (two plus one per $150k of spare cash). Materials come from the same one-tap order (`structureNeed.runNeedLines`); delivery stays as modeled (the drive contractor card; a hired crew carries a road pile in). **B) Cards.** `sim/wallCrew.ts`: a free "Work on the wall first" card (`setFocus` to the biggest unfinished wall run, once a home stands) and an "Order and hire N crews to put up the wall in about 4 weeks" card (urgency 92; shows when the cash covers the stone plus half the wages plus $6,000 and the family would need over 2 weeks). The Projects list shows family and crew ETA for each run and a "hire N crews" link; the pace box already shows the crews' ETA (now also for runs). **C) After the hit.** `crews.crewMarket(s)`: crews keep working while banks, law and fuel hold; wages are x(1 + 1.5 x (1 - min(banks, law))) (up to x2.2); at most 8 x min(banks, law, fuel) crews can be had at once (the newest hires go home first as it falls); under banks 20%, law 20% or fuel 15% none can be hired and the ones at work go home (was: any of 50/50/30). Peace and a healthy world: unlimited. Raid/wall hit points, hide costs and bunker logic untouched. **Start cash:** the independent range is back to $1.0M to $2.0M (`FUNDS_RANGE`, GDS §10.9 restored; #294 had trimmed it to $0.7M to $1.4M on the theory that cash strands; with money buying speed that trim ran against the principle). `idleCash.test.ts` assertion updated for the range (reason: the range is the thing being restored). **Harness (strategy playtest, bots `walls` and `fortress`, 16 seeds, random disasters, 400 days after the hit):** survival main to PR: independent walls Standard 50 to 56, Hardcore 31 to 50; full-time walls Standard 31 to 50, Hardcore 19 to 25; fortress unchanged within noise (the woven-wire ring was already built); the ring first finishes about sim day 90 to 250 instead of 650 to never, and wealthy (independent) families have it finished by about sim day 90 to 150 in 16 of 16 runs while full-time families (about $200k to spend) finish it by about day 120 to 250 in 10 to 15 of 16 runs, so wealth is visible. Cash at the hit falls by $50k to $100k for full-time families (the wages). Tables in the PR. Tests: `crewsRuns.test.ts`. |
| 2026-11-13 | People look and move like people (owner: "people look and move like real people"; **no save change**, drawing only) | **Appearance is derived, not saved:** `engine/world/people/roleWear.ts` (pure, tested) makes skin (10 tones), hair color and style (buzz, bun, ponytail, long, curly, bald), beard (none / stubble / full), and build (child with a big head, elder with a stoop, broad, slender) from a hash of the person id; the outfit comes from role (adult, teen, child, elder, farmer, builder, cook, medic, guard, neighbor, trader, scavenger, militia, tactical), season and climate (`seasonState.params.cold`): coats, knit hats, scarves and gloves in the cold, shorts and sun hats in the heat, boots, and for guards and raiders a vest or plate carrier, radio, helmet or cap, belt and a rifle slung on the back. `dressForTask` adds an apron at the stove, gloves and a belt at the saw, a sun hat in the garden, a vest and radio on watch. **Motion:** new poses DIG, SLEEP, AIM (rifle shouldered, hands on it), COVER, FALL, CARRYP (carrying a person), new tools SHOVEL and LOGS; runners lean forward, elders stoop; the walk cycle now advances with the ground covered (`stridePhase`), the pelvis drops as the legs spread so both feet stay on the ground, the swinging foot lifts, legs follow the slope under the feet, the head turns toward the nearest person (or glances while idle), idle people fold their arms, scratch their heads or put hands behind them, and breathe. The idle and work clock `uTime` was never advanced (chopping, hoeing and stirring stood frozen); each layer now has its own clock (real time, or `setTime` from the cutscene playhead). The battle scene arms every defender and every raider who has fired, aims on a shot, ducks under fire (COVER), flails as it falls and bends the carrier under the load. **Cost:** draw calls +0 (same two instanced layers: family and battle); triangles per figure 438 -> 546 (+108: 8 look boxes, most collapsed in the vertex shader when unused or farther than 9 / 16 / 28 units at Low / Medium / High; family layer 16 figures = +1,728 tris, battle layer 64 = +6,912 tris at most); one more instance attribute (`aGear`, 16 of 16 vertex slots used); no new textures or materials. |
| 2026-11-13 | Scene audit: individually sound structures that combine badly (owner: "make sure all buildings and structures look sound, and no incorrect procedural generations"; whole-scene level only, the per-kind generators are the structure audits' job; **no save change, no balance change**: placement, grading and drawing only) | **A reusable audit.** `sim/sceneAudit.ts` (pure, Node, no rendering) builds a real compound on the game's own ground (the parcel, road plan and bed, camp, what came with the land, the starter planner, and a hamlet's worth of hand placements through the same rules the placement tool uses) and reports what does not fit: structures on water, off the lot, on the camp, the drive, the county road or the road's cut and fill; buildings on each other; a building buried in or hovering over its own pad (the 45 cm step the pad rule calls a wall); doors onto another building, a retaining wall, water or a cliff; a building standing in a neighbour's retaining wall; a pad graded across the drive or the road; fences through the creek with no crossing or on a cliff; trees through a house (`planParcelTrees`); livestock turned out into a building, the water or the road. `sim/sceneAudit.test.ts` runs it in CI on all 11 regions' six listings (66 scenes), 14 steep / creek / gorge / mesa / bluff / island lots with trees, and three hamlets. `scripts/sceneShots.mjs` lays the very same scene in the browser (aerial, mid, eye level with a person; `--hamlet`, `--state stages|damage|snow|night|rain`); `?listing=region.index&seed=n` starts on a real listing. To make the audit grade the scene the way the world does, the pad rule (`buildkit/sitePad.ts`) and the placement of what came with the land (`buildkit/improvementSpots.ts`) were pulled out of `HomesteadWorld` unchanged. **Defects found and fixed (all in placement / grading, none in a structure's own generator):** (1) the septic placed beside a farmhouse cut its pad 3 m into the farmhouse corner (what came with the land ignored the pad rule: it now asks `padClash`); (2) `placementProblem` tested a footprint on 5 x 5 points, 1 to 1.5 u apart on a big one, so a 0.55 u drive, the county road or a 1.2 u creek slipped between them (a heated greenhouse stood on the county road, a workshop in the creek): thin things are now tested on a 0.3 u grid, out to the 0.06 u margin the graded pad takes; (3) the first fence round the starter yard stood in the creek or lake or on a cliff (`fenceLineProblems` pulls an edge in, or the fence is left for the player), and a property line that grazed the channel without crossing it got no creek bars (now barred too); (4) pads were graded over the drive and the county road: the road's bench and banks now go on after the pads (`ParcelTerrain.sample`), and a piece whose flat ground (apron and wall included) would reach the drive, the camp or the road's cut and fill is refused (`pad_over_road`, `bedMoves`, also for the planned road before the drive is built); (5) a pad could be graded over the end of a neighbour's retaining wall at the same level, leaving a wall stub in the new floor (`padClash` now refuses it, with its own sentence); (6) livestock turned out into the paddock were sent into the next building, the greenhouse, the creek or the road (`animalGoal` takes the world's `free` test and tries eight spots, then stands at the door). **Handed to the structure audits** (not fixed here): see the PR body. |
